| EC3 | the same, scale and blend | `Scale:` and `Blend:` write one stack value: the percent, then the blend index over it; the entry is translucent (flag 0x8, art id bit 8) when that value is not 0 — a blend other than none, or a scale without a blend (no installed line has one). The scale index is the percent's place in {50, 62, 75, 87, 100, 125, 150, 200} (4 without `Scale:`); a percent not in it is logged and takes index 0. | Original (latent quirk, no installed line) |
| EC4 | the same, sound | An entry without `Sound:` after `Art:` takes its group's first entry's sound plus {0, 4, 5, 6, 3, 7} by its place (0x5B0DC8), unless that first sound is −1; a sound whose file does not exist is then dropped (0x41A940, not modelled). | Original |
| EC5 | parts check (0x4CE1A0) | Each part the flags name must load: the foreground (variant 0), the background (1), the underlay (2); one that does not leaves the entry without art and flags (logged). | Original |
| EC6 | eye-candy art id (0x504AE0, 0x504B90, 0x504BC0, 0x502B50, 0x41DF84) | Class 14; bits 1..3 scale, 4..5 palette, 6..7 variant, 8 translucency, 9..11 rotation, 12..18 frame, 19..27 the number of `art\eye_candy\eye_candy.mes`; a part out of range is error 12 (logged; the entry keeps no art and no flags, 0x4CE028). The variant setter logs and takes 0 for 3 or more. The file is `art\eye_candy\<name>_<F|B|U>.art`. | Original |
| EC7 | casting goal 27 (0x5A8BD0; 0x429F00, 0x4315B0, 0x431340, 0x42CAA0, 0x429C40, 0x429C80, 0x431A40) | Node rows are a function, three argument slots, the false result's step and delay, the true result's (0x4237CF). Without a missile: the first node sends a goal whose eye candy has not begun to 0x429F00, which places the cast entry (6·spell) on the caster directly (EC9) — none, or a failed target re-check, releases at once and ends. The begin (0x4315B0) sets the pace and restarts at once. Each step then moves the animated parts one frame (0x431340): with an underlay only the underlay, else the foreground and then the background; a part already at its last frame makes the step false, and the goal ends one pace later through the removal (0x431A40). The timing part's new frame equal to its ART action frame sets goal flag 0x4 (only a foreground or underlay); the gate (0x42CAA0) then sets 0x8 and passes once; no missile (0x429C40 false) runs the release (0x429C80): passed, the goal restarts at once (so the next frame follows without a pace), failed, it ends through the removal. Every other step waits one pace. Frame k shows at (k − 1)·pace before the release and (k − 2)·pace after it; an action frame of 0 or past the last frame never releases (the end frees the run, CG7). | Original |
| EC8 | the same, pace (0x431725) | The pace is 1000 / the frame rate (unsigned division; a rate of 0 divides by zero) of the art the play built last (goal +0x48): the underlay, else the background, else the foreground (two installed eye candies have parts of different rates). With the run's flag 0x800 the begin starts at a random frame (not set by the cast; not modelled). | Original |
| EC9 | play (0x4CCF70, 0x4CCD20) | The descriptor's +0x1C chooses: 0 (the cast overlay, 0x456D20) places every flagged part in a free slot of the object's foreground (field 6, 7 slots), background (7, 7 slots) or underlay (8, 4 slots) list and returns the slots for goal 27; 1 (the release's 0x456E00, the EyeCandy component) places nothing itself and starts an eye-candy goal only for an animating entry (0x20) with a foreground or underlay slot (0x4CD49D): 45 (`ag_eye_candy`), 46 reverse, 47/48 callback, 58/59 end callback, 75/76 with fire damage, by the play flags. An entry that does not animate is then not shown. With `can_autoscale` (0x80) a critter's body size sets the scale (0x503EA0; no installed entry is drawn on a critter with it in the cast path; not modelled). A multi-rotation eye candy takes its object's art rotation (0x502930; not modelled). | Original |
| EC10 | eye-candy goal 45 (0x5AAFD0) | The begin (0x4315B0) places the parts at frame 0 and waits one pace; each step moves the animated parts one frame and waits one pace; the step that finds one at its last frame removes them (0x431A40) and the goal ends: frame k at k·pace, the end at frames·pace. An `anim_forever` entry (goal data bit 0x80 from flag 0x40) is not removed: 0x431A40 fails, the begin places the parts at frame 0 again and the loop repeats every frames·pace (its sound loops too) until a Remove ends the goal (0x4243E0). | Original |
| EC11 | EyeCandy component (0x451FE0, 0x4CD940) | On the component's target object only (none: nothing). Add plays entry 6·spell + its number with the run's id and play flags (EC9), skipped for a run forced to end (run flag 0x40); played with a callback flag (0x8 or 0x10) the run waits for the animation (0x5E75CC); not played, the callback runs now (0x5E75D4, the END callback for 0x10). Remove takes the entry's parts off the object, only this run's unless `no_id` (0x100). | Original (callbacks since ARC-DEV-352) |
| EC12 | object draw (0x43B390) | The translucency bit draws a part additive (blit 0x10). Foreground and background parts go to two lists drawn around the body; their scale is the object's scale, times {50, 63, 75, 87, 100, 130, 160, 200} % of the art id's index when that is not 4 (0x5A548C, another table than the parse's); underlays (field 8) are drawn at 100 %. | Original |
| EC13 | run start (0x456CD0) | With a parent, the six entries of the spell are preloaded (0x4CCD80); nothing is shown. | Original |
| EC14 | S9b read (ARC-DEV-143; goal-table.md GT83–GT93) | Corrects EC9 and EC10 where they were loose. The goal an EyeCandy component's play flags choose: no callback flag → 45 (75 with `fire_dmg`), the reverse flag the twin; else `callback` (0x8) → 47/48 and beats `end_callback` and `fire_dmg`; else `end_callback` → 58/59. `ice_dmg` (0x240) is `fire_dmg` with normal damage. The callback goals call back once (47/48 at the timing part's action frame, 58/59 at the end; 58/59 never loop); a looping 45's second begin keeps its overlay places and, with `random_start`, starts at a random frame (the first begin never does). The reverse step sets the action flag one frame late (an action frame of 0 or 1 never fires; unclassified). The hover (AGoal 44) raises the object 15 px and bobs it 2 px a step (ten steps, 100 ms apart) in the object's own offset; an end leaves it where it is. | Original (reverse flag slip: unclassified) |

**F0 facts raised:** the eye-candy rows of [FR-MAGIC-001](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-001) and
[FR-MAGIC-012](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-012) (the list load, the parse, the art id, goal 27's timing, goal 45,
the EyeCandy component and the overlay draw: F2). Left at F0: the missile (goal 28, 0x458B70), the callback goals 47/48/58/59,
the light (field 17) and sound parts of a play, tinting (0x43EF10).


## Queued cast (ARC-MAGIC-019)

Source: the [spell cast host's viewer](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#the-viewer-arc-magic-015) (item 7), the third magic
leftover after MT20a-2. Read on 2026-10-28 by this task in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`llvm-objdump`, data words of the goal
table by section offsets): the spell run start's goal branch (0x455C30 from 0x456069), the goal finder (0x44E830), the goal
depth getter (0x4373A0), the queueing wrapper (0x44DBE0) and the insertion (0x44D9B0), the goal stepper's step words and end step
(0x423560), the run kill (0x44E2C0), the casting goals 26 (0x5A89D0) and 27 (0x5A8BD0) with their end nodes (0x42B090,
0x429CD0) and goal 27's overlay nodes (0x429F00, 0x431A40); a second reader that saw only the questions (method step 6) read
the same functions and agrees on every row, adding two facts (QC1: goals 82 and 83 are not found; QC2: the insertion's own limit
only logs in single player). It read 0x4B6D70 as a global of unknown meaning; it is the turn-based test the stepper's end step
and the attack and invocation code use (TB15, BR6, IV1). Every direct branch stays inside `.text`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| QC1 | goal finder (0x44E830), insertion (0x44D9B0 through 0x44DBE0) | With a parent and no casting animation, the start looks for a running casting goal: the caster's animation run whose bottom goal (stack slot 0) is 26 or a goal of 26's list (27). Goal 82 or 83 (a spell with a casting animation) is not found, so a cast during one starts a new run (no college spell has a casting animation). None: a new run with goal 26 starts (0x44D520). Found: the new goal 26 is inserted at the bottom of that run's stack — every goal moves up one slot, the current index grows by one, so the playing goal stays current — and the queued goals play in the order they were cast, each when the goals above it have popped. | Original (refined by [AC5](npc-ai.md#npc-ai-queued-cast-arc-world-104): the finder runs whatever the record's casting animation, and a queued goal is 82 for a record that has one) |
| QC2 | the same, limits (0x4373A0, 0x4B6D70) | The queue is refused when the found run's current index is 4 or more (the goals beneath the playing one plus the playing goal's own subgoals, such as goal 26's get-up, unconceal or turn) or while turn-based combat is on (any round); the insertion's own limit (index 7, 8 goals) refuses only in multiplayer and only logs in single player, never reached from here. A refusal frees the run (0x4558D0), silently. | Original |
| QC3 | goal 27 (0x5A8BD0), overlay removal (0x431A40) | Goal 27 places the cast eye candy (0x429F00, EC7); once it has played, its removal node clears the goal's overlay flag and answers true when the goal holds overlay slots, which pops the goal (step 0x30000000): the goal beneath, a queued goal 26, restarts at its first node in the same step (its start: action points, the pre-animation check, the target, CG2–CG4). It answers false without slots or for goal flag 0x80, which takes the end step. | Original |
| QC4 | the same, no overlay (0x429F00) | 0x429F00 checks the target again (0x456A90) before it places the overlay; a failure, or no cast eye candy to place (EC7), leads to the release node (0x429C80) and then the end step. A queued goal 26 refused at its start ends through its end-goal node (0x429CD0) and the end step too. | Original |
| QC5 | end step (0x423560), run kill (0x44E2C0) | The end step (0x90000000) marks the run done, charges a pending action-point cost in a round, calls the end node (descriptor +0x1E0) of every goal on the stack — slots 1 to the current one, then slot 0 — with the current goal's data, and the run is killed. The kill (0x44E2C0: a lost caster, an interrupt) calls every goal's end node from the current one down the same way, unless its priority test refuses the kill. A queued goal 26's end node is the animation reset (0x42B090), which frees no spell run (goal 27's is 0x429CD0, CG7). | Original; MG43 |
| QC6 | run start (0x455C30), cancels (0x4534E0) | The queued cast's run is created and active from the click; its BEGIN waits for its own goal's release. A run of the same source starting before it cancels it when their `Cancels_SF:` overlap (RN, 0x4534E0), like any active run; the queued goal then starts without a run and ends (QC4). | Original |
| QC7 | queued casts on a target that dies (0x45DA20, 0x459740, 0x4571E0, 0x456FA0, 0x4507D0), ARC-MAGIC-1399 (#1399), read 2026-11-12 in the text export | A queued cast's run is active from the click with a BEGIN never processed (QC6). The kill (0x45DA20, UE2) calls 0x459740, whose third loop (every active run but the guard, target +0xA0 the dead object) ends each by event (0x4571E0). 0x4571E0 looks the run up (0x4557C0); a run with the action BEGIN (+0x8 = 0) and the processed bit clear (+0x13C bit 0x4) goes to the free 0x456FA0 with the charge flag set, which pays the BEGIN cost (0x4507D0: the source's fatigue through 0x450420; the college half, the 2x of a critter with the 0x1B state) before freeing the slot. The lookup (0x4557C0, through 0x455820 and 0x444020) first requires the run's source (+0x10), parent (+0x58), target (+0xA0) and every object of the lists at +0x130 and +0x134 to still exist; if one is gone the original queues the delayed interrupt (code 5, 1 ms) and charges nothing (`EndByEvent` does not model this check; it matters only for a run that lists a destroyed object, not for a target that is dead but still there). Otherwise nothing tests the target's life or a queue: **each queued run on the dead target pays its BEGIN cost once and is freed**, so one Harm kill with three casts queued costs the caster 5 fatigue for each of the three (plus the killing cast's own, paid at its BEGIN; that run is the guard and is skipped). The runs of MG43 (queued, but whose goals never play because the stack ended) are another case: no run ending reaches them. | Original; the port (SpellRunSystem.EndByEvent / ObjectGone) matches for a normal paid cast such as Harm, in both modes (the Steamwoven fix of MG40 only skips the charge for a free run, a technology record or `ChargeBeginCost: 0`; MG39 is the goal-side twin); pinned by a test; no change. |
| MG43 | dropped queue (QC5) | Queued casts whose goals never play (the stack ended through the end step, or the run was killed) keep their runs in their slots — unbegun, unpaid, in no goal — until the game ends. | Original bug, technical: the leaked runs hold slots of the 512-slot run table for the rest of the game (fix in both modes without a switch, owner decision 2026-10-28: the runs are freed unpaid) |

**F0 facts raised:** the queued cast of [FR-MAGIC-012](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-012) (0x455C30's goal
branch, 0x44E830, 0x4373A0, 0x44D9B0, the stepper's pop and end step, 0x44E2C0's end nodes: F2). Left at F0: goal 26's turn
(0x42C850) and its subgoals 22, 54, 63, and goal 82's casting animation.

## Item spell triggers (ARC-MAGIC-020)

Source: the [item spell triggers](../../world/item-spell-triggers.md) contract, the shared item trigger slice of
[FR-MAGIC-011](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-011) ("Outside this plan" of the [magic implementation
plan](../magic-implementation-plan.md)). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task and, independently, by a second reader that saw only the
questions (method step 6): the executors (0x4CB830 with its wrapper 0x4CB800, 0x4CBFF0), their helpers (0x4CB760, 0x4CB790, 0x4CB7A0,
0x455A20, 0x450A50), the run stop (0x4573D0), every event wrapper (0x4CB7D0..0x4CC130), the wield, unwield, insert and remove callers
and the activation wrappers (0x57C040, 0x57C080). Both agree on every row. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. It confirms DV3 and
TP4 of the [technology ledger](technology.md#tech-devices-arc-technology-008).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IT1 | executor (0x4CB830, through 0x4CB800) | Arguments: the item, its parent, the event's other object, a tile and the event mask. Nothing on a network client (0x62AC6C bit 0 without bit 1). The five slots (fields 0x64..0x68) in order; a slot holding 10000 ends the walk. The record's trigger mask (+0x38 of a 0x158-byte record) is read for the slot's whole value (0x4CB760), the run's spell is its low byte (0x4CB790, LM7); a mask sharing no bit with the event mask is skipped. | Original |
| IT2 | the same, chance | Only when the record's mask holds a chance bit (0xE10): an else-if chain Rare 0x200 → 10, Uncommon 0x400 → 20, Common 0x800 → 40, Frequent 0x10 → 66, each a d100 (1..100) that fires at or below the number. | Original |
| IT3 | the same, aim | Read from the record's mask. With an aim bit (0xE100): `Target_Attacker` 0x2000 (so `Item_Used`) the other object; else `_Weapon` 0x4000 the other's item in slot 1004; else `_Armor` 0x8000 its slot 1006 (a null other gives no target); `_Weapon_Melee` 0x100 alone sets no target. Without one: `Parent_Atks_Location` 0x800000 the tile, else the parent. The run starts (0x455AC0) only with a target object or a tile. | Original (the melee bit alone never fires; the only installed record with it, 87, also holds 0x2000) |
| IT4 | the same, invocation | 0x455A20: source the item, its location field, parent 0x450A50 (an item in an inventory: its field 0x58; a critter itself), no target, no other; then the event mask at +0xD8 (the run's triggers, which gate the components) and flag 0x1 at +0xDC (the cast without the casting goal: goal 73). The global attacker (0x5FF618), set only by the attack wrappers 0x4CBD40 and 0x4CBDB0, becomes the other object (+0xA0) and is cleared at the end of every call, so in a worn-items loop only the first item's run gets it. | Original |
| IT5 | withdrawal (0x4CBFF0; wrappers unwear 0x4CBFC0 0x40000000, drop 0x4CC130 0x1000000) | The same network gate, also nothing for a null item or parent; the same walk and mask test, no chance roll. Per match: an invocation of the slot's low byte aimed at the parent is handed to the stop (0x4573D0), then a second one, aimed at the parent with the event mask at +0xD8 and no flag 0x1 (the casting goal), starts. | Original |
| IT6 | run stop (0x4573D0) | Nothing without a source in the invocation. The run slots 0..511 in order; the first run whose parent, target object and spell equal the invocation's is interrupted (0x457110). The source is not compared, so another item's or the holder's own run of the same spell at itself can be the one stopped. | Original (latent: only when one holder wears two items carrying the same wear record; whether the installed armour 8168..8170 can be worn together is not checked) |
| IT7 | event wrappers | Wear 0x4CB7D0 and pickup 0x4CBAA0: the item, parent given, no other. Parent hit 0x4CBAD0 (0x2000100 for a melee hit: weapon range ≤ 1, 0x465E30), stunned 0x4CBB80, going unconscious 0x4CBBF0, dying 0x4CBC60, hit by an attack spell 0x4CBCD0, attacks an opponent 0x4CBD40, going-unconscious target 0x4CBE00: each item a PC or NPC wears (slots 1000..1008) with the other party. Attacks a location 0x4CBDB0: the one item with the tile. Damages an opponent 0x4CBE70: each worn item 0x10000 then 0x40000, and 0x20000 for the weapon used; a thrown item (types 5..14) gets 0x40000 with the victim as parent and itself as other. Item used 0x4CBF70: 0x1000 (one bit of `Item_Used`'s two) for items 5..14, parent field 0x58. 0x4CBCD0 has no caller: `Parent_Hit_By_Atk_Spell` never fires (no installed record uses it). | Original |
| IT8 | callers | Wield 0x4677B0: the wear executor, then the light recalculation (0x467E80), then script point 24. Unwield 0x467CB0: the unwear withdrawal, then the light, then point 25. The inventory insert (its call at 0x466907): pickup after the item is listed and its location and parent set, before a wield into slots 1000..1008. The inventory remove (its call at 0x467C01): runs sourced from the item ended (0x457450), the item unlisted, an unwield from a wear slot, then drop, before point 28. | Original |
| IT9 | activation wrappers (0x57C040, 0x57C080) | 0x57C040 (an object, a slot index) reads the slot through 0x4CB7A0 (−1 for a null object or 10000); 0x57C080 (an object, a spell) takes it as given. Both call the activation (0x57BC70) only for a value that is not −1 and whose record has no trigger mask (0x45A030); the value is never masked. 0x57C040's callers are two copies of a window handler whose five buttons map to the item's slots (0x54BC68, 0x54CF78); 0x57C080's is the hotkey entry of an object and a spell (0x54FF85, 0x54FFCC, wielding the object into slot 1004 first when it is not the weapon). | Original (activation and hotkeys: the item spell UI follow-up) |
| IT10 | goal 73 (CG1) for an executor's run | The executor's run has the parent and flag 0x1, so it gets goal 73: the casting goal's start without its end node. A start refused (the action points in a turn-based round, the pre-animation check, a target gone) ends the goal with nothing freed, so the run keeps its slot and never begins, as a run that fails its release re-check does (MG41). | Original bug (a slot that is never freed; fixed in both modes without a switch, owner decision 2026-10-28: the run is freed unpaid) |

Installed data (census, `ItemSpellCensusRealDataTests`): 158 slots on 155 item prototypes name 146 records, none out of range. By the
records' masks: none 94 (the 83 scrolls among them: the use and activation path), `Item_Used|Target_Attacker` 42,
`Parent_Atks_Location` 14, `Parent_Dmgs_Opponent_W_Item|Target_Attacker` 2, `Target_Hit|Target_Attacker` 1,
`Parent_Hit|Target_Attacker|Target_Attacker_Weapon_Melee` 1, `Wear|Unwear` 4. No record fires on pickup or drop. The four wear items
are armour 8168, 8169, 8170 (record 168: a maintained technology heal with cells, its MAINTAIN components gated by `Wear`) and 8207
(record 182: object flags set on the wear, cleared on the unwear), all with unlimited charges and no move scripts.

**F0 facts raised:** the executors of [FR-MAGIC-011](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-011) (0x4CB830, 0x4CBFF0, the
wrappers 0x4CB7D0..0x4CC130 and the stop 0x4573D0: F2); the activation wrappers of
[FR-MAGIC-012](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-012) (0x57C040, 0x57C080: F2). Left at F0: the attack record's flag
0x40000 read by 0x4CBAD0, the windows that own 0x54BC68 and 0x54CF78, and whether the remove clears the item's inventory flag before
the drop (which decides whether the drop's stop compares the holder or the item as parent; settled by IU9: the holder is passed).

## Item use and moves (ARC-MAGIC-022)

Source: the [item spell triggers](../../world/item-spell-triggers.md) contract (its item use and moves sections), the second part of
the shared item trigger slice of [FR-MAGIC-011](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-011). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task
and, independently, by a second reader that saw only the questions (method step 6): the item use (0x462CC0) from its use script on,
0x4605E0 and the callback table it reads (0x45FD40, the builder 0x57C6E0), the callback 0x57CE30, the activation's returns (0x57BC70),
the cast at a target (0x57C110) and the struct 0x4F2810 builds, 0x45A030 for an empty slot, the item used wrapper (0x4CBF70), the
inventory removal (0x467860) and insertion (0x466640) around their executor calls, the source functions 0x457450 and 0x4574D0, and
0x4B1750. Both agree on every row; the second reader added that the callback skips the cancel when the record has item triggers (IU3).
Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. It agrees with IT7, IT8, SU1–SU8, AG15 and TP1.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IU1 | item use (0x462CC0), single player | After a use script (0x441980) that lets it go on: a charge count (field 0x69) that is not 0, negative included, or the magical item flag (0x57 & 0x4) takes the spell branch: the use's target into an object struct (0x4F2810, even a null one), slot 0 (0x4CB7A0, −1 for none), 0x4605E0(item, struct, slot-0 value), then 0x4CBF70(item, target). No action points (0x4B7CD0 is called only when the script stops the use and on the other branch) and no charge write. Then food (9) and scrolls (10) only: 0x4574D0(item) and the destruction 0x43CCA0. | Original |
| IU2 | 0x4605E0, callback table (0x45FD40, 0x57C6E0) | Calls the pointer at 0x5E86D0, when set, with its arguments unchanged. 0x45FD40 copies it from offset 0x78 of the interface callback table; the builder 0x57C6E0 stores 0x57CE30 there (the only occurrence of that value in the file). | Original |
| IU3 | callback 0x57CE30 | A record with item triggers (0x45A030: record +0x38 not 0) returns at once (no cancel). Otherwise the activation 0x57BC70(item, spell); only when it answers 0 the cast at the use's target (0x57C110 with the struct); then the cancel 0x57BFF0. No test for −1 (the wrappers 0x57C040 and 0x57C080 have one). | Original |
| IU4 | activation (0x57BC70) answers | 1 (the use's target is not read): every early refusal of SU1, a failed interface mode change, the casts at once (an area 0, exactly 0x10, or self without the tile bit: at the caster's location, 0x4F27F0) and the Shift cast at the player (SU3). 0: the cast's action-point check refused (0x4B7AA0) or the targeting cursor opened (the inventory bit first opens the source's inventory, 0x572240). So the use's target is cast at only for a spell that needs a picked target; the cancel then closes the cursor. The caster is the item's holder (0x4612A0). | Original |
| IU5 | 0x4F2810, cast at a target (0x57C110) | The struct holds a handle and a word at +8: 0 (0x4F2810) an object, read through 0x4440E0 into the invocation's target object; not 0 (0x4F27F0) a location, copied to its target tile. The cost check (0x4508A0) runs only for a PC or NPC source, so an item source skips it; the invocation (0x455A20) has the item as source and its holder as parent. | Original |
| IU6 | 0x45A030 for −1 | An empty slot 0 on the spell branch reaches 0x45A030(−1), which reads the dword 0x120 bytes before the record table (and 0x459F60(−1) would read 0x124 before it): outside the table, a value not determined. No installed item on the spell branch has an empty slot 0 (census). | Original bug (technical: a read outside the table; fixed in both modes without a switch: nothing is cast, owner rule 2026-10-28) |
| IU7 | item used (0x4CBF70) | Nothing for a null item or a type outside 5..14; parent field 0x58; 0x4CB800(item, parent, the other object, mask 0x1000), which calls 0x4CB830 with no tile. Callers: the use (0x462F83) and its tile variant (0x4630DD). | Original |
| IU8 | 0x457450, 0x4574D0, 0x4B1750 | 0x457450(object): every run whose source (+0x10) is the object, unless its record is technological (+0x34 bit 0x4) or has item triggers (+0x38) without a maintenance period (+0x18 ≤ 0), is ended (0x4571E0). 0x4574D0(object): every run whose source or parent (+0x58) is the object has its source cleared (0x457530; +0x48 = −1) and goes on; neither touches the target. 0x4B1750 returns the constant 5 (0x4502E0), the scroll test's minimum intelligence. | Original (agrees with RP13 and SpellRunSystem) |
| IU9 | inventory removal (0x467860) | The holder is the explicit argument (the item's parent is only compared, 0x4612A0, and a mismatch prints a debug line and goes on). In order: 0x457450(item); the item unlisted (field 0x5E −1); the unwield (0x467CB0, which runs the unwear 0x4CBFC0) for a worn location 1000..1008; 0x4CCC00(holder); the drop 0x4CC130(item, holder), which passes the holder unchanged to the withdrawal 0x4CBFF0 with mask 0x1000000; then the remove-item script. Field 0x58 is not written. | Original (closes IT8's F0 question: the drop compares the holder) |
| IU10 | inventory insertion (0x466640) | The pickup 0x4CBAA0(item, holder) (its only caller, at 0x466907) runs after the item is listed, its location written and field 0x58 set to the holder, before a wield into 1000..1008; it calls 0x4CB800 with no other object and mask 0x80000000. A merged ammunition stack (the inserted item deleted, 0x462930) returns before it. | Original |

**F0 facts raised:** the item use's spell branch of [FR-MAGIC-011](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-011) (0x462CC0's
spell branch, 0x4605E0, 0x57CE30, 0x4CBF70: F2) and the removal's and insertion's executor calls (0x467860, 0x466640: F2). Left at
F0: what 0x4CCC00 does and the AI's use path through the activation (an NPC holder is refused by 0x40DA20, SU1).

## Combat and life item triggers (ARC-MAGIC-023)

Source: the [item spell triggers](../../world/item-spell-triggers.md) contract (its combat events section), the third part of the
shared item trigger slice of [FR-MAGIC-011](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-011). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task
and, independently, by a second reader that saw only the questions (method step 6): the wrappers 0x4CBAD0..0x4CBE70 and their every
direct caller (found in the listing and by a byte scan for calls and absolute references): the damage step (0x4B4390), the fatigue
setter (0x45D5A0), the kill (0x45DA20), the dying animation's end (0x42FF40), the attack animation's start (0x432700) and the
projectile's tile crossing (0x4B2870). Both agree on every row; the second reader added the kill's script gate (IC5) and that the
fatigue write is skipped for a fatigue-immune critter. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`, and none of these functions has an
indirect jump or call. It agrees with IT7, CW8, CR12, PF8–PF10 and C27.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IC1 | wrappers (0x4CBAD0..0x4CBE70) | The parent must be a PC or NPC (type 15 or 16, field 0x14F); each walks its worn slots 1000..1008 in order (0x4649C0, an empty slot skipped) and calls the executor (0x4CB800) with the item, the parent, the other object and the mask. Parent hit 0x4CBAD0 (other, context, parent): 0x2000000, or 0x2000100 when the context's flags hold 0x40000 (the attack entries: the ordinary attack, the throw, the projectile's strike; never a spell's damage) and its weapon (+0x10) is null or has a range (0x465E30) of at most 1. Stunned 0x4CBB80, going unconscious 0x4CBBF0, dying 0x4CBC60 (other, parent): 0x4000000, 0x8000000, 0x100000. Attacks an opponent 0x4CBD40 (parent, other): 0x400000. Target going unconscious 0x4CBE00 (parent, other): 0x80000. Damages an opponent 0x4CBE70 (parent, weapon, other): each worn item 0x10000 then 0x40000, and the item equal to the weapon also 0x20000; a parent of an item type (5..14) instead makes one call with itself as item and other and the third argument as parent, 0x40000; any other type nothing. Attacks a location 0x4CBDB0 (parent, item, tile): no type test and no walk; for a non-null item the executor 0x4CB830 with no other object, the tile and 0x800000. 0x4CBCD0 (0x200000) has no caller and no reference. | Original |
| IC2 | damage step (0x4B4390), its calls | Only on the PC/NPC branch, after the early exits (no target, the taking-damage script, a destroyed or invulnerable object, a wall, a critter dead at entry). Stun (damage flag 0x40, critter flag 0x20 clear, no 0x180): stunned (attacker +0x08, target) before the stun animation's start (0x4357B0), so also when that start fails and the flag is cleared again. Knockdown or knockout (0x180): going unconscious (attacker, target), then target going unconscious (parent the attacker, other the target), then the knockdown animation; a knockout of a conscious critter then sets fatigue through 0x45D5A0. Much later the fatigue write (0x45D5A0, skipped for a fatigue-immune critter), the kill (0x45DA20, IC5) when the critter is now dead, then, unless the attacker (+0x08) is null, parent hit (attacker, context, target) and damages an opponent (attacker, the context's weapon when its flags hold 0x40000 else none, target), then the weapon wear (0x4B5580). The hooks also run when the target died in the step. A non-critter target makes no wrapper call. | Original (latent: a knockdown fires going unconscious too, and a knockout of a conscious critter fires it twice, with and without the attacker; no installed record uses `Parent_Going_Unconscious`, `Parent_Stunned` or `Target_Going_Unconscious`) |
| IC3 | the same, a critical's items | The crippling drops, the armour wear and the helmet drop come between the knockdown and the hooks, and the weapon wear after them, so the parent hit and the dying walk the target's items without what this step dropped or broke, the stun and knockdown walk them before, and the damages opponent walks the attacker's items before its weapon wear. | Original |
| IC4 | fatigue setter (0x45D5A0) | PC and NPC only. Reads whether the critter is unconscious (0x45D800: fatigue at most 0, never for a critter with flag 0x4), clamps the value at 0 and writes it (field 0xE2); for a value that is not 0 and a critter that is no prototype (0x405BC0) it schedules the fatigue recovery, then, when the critter was conscious and is now unconscious: the runs it sources end (0x457450), going unconscious with no other object (other 0, parent the critter), the knockdown (0x435A90) and the action points end (0x4B7C90). The check reads the fatigue only, so a hit that kills and empties the fatigue fires it too. Its other callers (spell payment 0x450420, the step move 0x430FC0, the recovery timers, healing, scripts) fire it the same way. | Original |
| IC5 | kill (0x45DA20), dying animation end (0x42FF40) | The damage step passes the victim, the second attacker copy (+0x30: the attacker, or a spell run's other object, DH1) and the animation. The kill's last call is dying (other the killer, parent the victim) when the killer is not null, the victim's dying script (0x441980, point 12) let it go on and the victim's object flag 0x2 is clear. 0x42FF40 (from the goal table at 0x5A94F0) is the dying animation's end: unless the goal's flag 0x200, for a dead critter it sets object flags 0x404, for the local PC starts the end of the game (0x460520, C27), then fires dying with no other object. So a kill with a killer fires dying twice: with the killer at once, without one when the fall ends. | Original (latent: no installed record uses `Parent_Dying`) |
| IC6 | attack start (0x432700), projectile (0x4B2870) | 0x432700 (from the goal table at 0x5A7E90), the attack animation's begin: after its checks (attacker and target set, attacker field 0x14 without 0x10000, a critter not flagged 0x60 in field 0xDA) and the action point charge (0x4B7CD0 of 0x4B7C30, a refusal ends the goal), the animation's speed and the sound, attacks an opponent (parent the attacker, other the target); each swing fires it. 0x4CBD40 and 0x4CBDB0 also store their parent in the global 0x5FF618, which the executor copies into the invocation's other object (+0xA0) after its aim and clears at its one exit, so only the first worn item's run gets it. 0x4B2870 calls 0x4CBDB0 with its attacker, the projectile's item (field 0x50, the context's weapon) and the struck object's tile (the strike), or the destination (a shot that reached it without a target) or the projectile's tile (the range end). | Original |

Installed data (census, `ItemSpellCensusRealDataTests`): no record has a chance bit or uses stunned, going unconscious, dying, attacks an
opponent or target going unconscious. Weapon 6130 (record 147, `Target_Hit|Target_Attacker`: a damage with cells), weapons 6175 and
6179 (records 203 and 204, `Parent_Dmgs_Opponent_W_Item|Target_Attacker`: a cast and a damage) and armour 8215 (record 87,
`Parent_Hit|Target_Attacker|Target_Attacker_Weapon_Melee`: a damage) fire on the damage step, all with unlimited charges. The 14
`Parent_Atks_Location` records are thrown items' (food 10105 with one charge, 13 generic items): they summon, damage or set flags at
the landing tile and destroy themselves.

**F0 facts raised:** the wrappers and their callers above (0x4CBAD0..0x4CBE70, the damage step's calls, 0x45D5A0's going unconscious,
0x45DA20's and 0x42FF40's dying, 0x432700's attacks an opponent, 0x4B2870's attacks a location: F2). Left at F0: 0x465E30 (a weapon's
range, read by the parent hit; RH1 reads it the same way), 0x405BC0 (a handle test that reads as "is a prototype") and 0x441980's
point 12 (the dying script).

## Life item triggers outside the attack (ARC-MAGIC-024)

Source: the [item spell triggers](../../world/item-spell-triggers.md) contract (its life events section), the fourth part of the
shared item trigger slice of [FR-MAGIC-011](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-011). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task
and, independently, by a second reader that saw only the questions (method step 6): the fatigue setter (0x45D5A0), the dying
animation's end (0x42FF40), the spell's damage component (0x4518D0) with the context builder (0x4B2210), the damage step's hooks
(0x4B4390 at 0x4B5379..0x4B53C0), the damages opponent wrapper (0x4CBE70) and the executor (0x4CB800, 0x4CB830). Both agree on every
row. It agrees with IC1–IC5, IT1, IT4 and DH1.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IC7 | fatigue setter (0x45D5A0), dying animation end (0x42FF40) | The setter calls going unconscious (0x4CBBF0, mask 0x8000000 per worn item) with no other object and the critter as parent only for a PC or NPC, a value written that is not 0, a critter that is no prototype (0x405BC0), conscious before (0x45D800) and unconscious after; IC4's callers reach it the same way. 0x42FF40 reads the goal's object (+0xD00; none asserts and exits) and calls dying (0x4CBC60, mask 0x100000) with no other object when the goal's flags (+0x0C) lack 0x200 and the critter is dead (0x45D8D0). | Original |
| IC8 | spell damage step (0x4518D0 → 0x4B4390) | 0x4B2210 writes the run's parent as the attacker (+0x08) and clears the flags; the run's other object, when set, becomes the damage source (+0x30); the flags gain 0x280 and never 0x40000. In the step only a null attacker skips the parent hit (0x4CBAD0, with the context) and the damages opponent (0x4CBE70), whose weapon is the context's only with flag 0x40000, so none for a spell: no melee bit and no damages opponent with the item. The flags word is tested nowhere on that path; the step's earlier exits (no target, the network gate, the taking-damage script, object flags 0x400001, a dead or non-critter target) skip both calls. | Original |
| IC9 | the same, an item run's damage | An executor's run has the item's holder as its parent (IT4), so its damage step's attacker is the holder; 0x4CBE70 gives every worn item 0x10000 then 0x40000 (`Target_Hit`), and the executor has no recursion or re-entrancy guard and does not test whether the item sources a running run (its exits: a network client, slot 10000, a mask that shares no bit, a failed chance roll, no target). So a `Target_Hit` item whose spell damages the opponent fires again on its own damage, and two wearers of `Parent_Hit` armour aimed at the attacker answer each other, each round a new goal 73 run, until the target dies or the item's charge runs out. | Original bug (MG46) |
| MG46 | IC9 | An on-hit item spell repeats itself on its own damage: installed weapon 6130 (record 147, `Target_Hit\|Target_Attacker`, damage with cells) spends its cells or kills on one hit, armour 8215 (record 87, `Parent_Hit\|Target_Attacker`) worn on both sides ping-pongs. | Original bug (gameplay, the owner's bug rule of 2026-10-28: the original behind the Original switch, `MagicAnomalyProfile.ItemRunDamageFiresHooks`. Owner decision 2026-10-28, ARC-MAGIC-025: the narrow fix, [decisions](../../architecture/decisions.md): Steamwoven lets each item fire at most once in a chain of item spells, skipping in a walk the items whose runs led to the damage step, so every other item's first answer stays; it replaced ARC-MAGIC-024's fix, which fired no parent hit and no damages opponent for such a step at all) |

**F0 facts raised:** 0x45D5A0's going unconscious and 0x42FF40's dying (F2, registered as partial methods); the spell context's attacker
and damage source and the hooks' gate (F2). Left at F0: 0x405BC0 (still read as "is a prototype") and what the host does with the
goal flag 0x200.

## Attack item triggers (ARC-MAGIC-025)

Source: the [item spell triggers](../../world/item-spell-triggers.md) contract (its attack section), the fifth part of the shared
item trigger slice of [FR-MAGIC-011](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-011). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task and,
independently, by a second reader that saw only the questions (method step 6): attacks a location (0x4CBDB0), the executor
(0x4CB830) with the invocation init (0x455A20) and the parent getter (0x450A50), the projectile's tile crossing (0x4B2870), the item
branch of damages an opponent (0x4CBE70), the attack animation's begin (0x432700) and a scan of every absolute reference and direct
call of 0x432700, 0x4CBD40 and 0x4CBDB0, with the throw (0x4B3C00) and its dispatcher path. Both agree on every row. It agrees with
IC1, IC6, IT4, RN2, TW1–TW6, PF8–PF10 and CT2.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IC10 | attacks a location (0x4CBDB0), executor (0x4CB830) | 0x4CBDB0 takes the parent, the item and a tile; it tests only that the item is not null (no type test, no walk), puts the parent in the global 0x5FF618 and calls the executor with the item, the parent, no other object, the tile and 0x800000. The executor reads the global for every slot that passes (after the aim) into the invocation's other object (+0xA0) and clears it at every exit, so with 0x4CBD40, which sets it once before its walk, only the first worn item's calls carry it. A record with 0x800000 and no aim bit aims at the tile; a tile of 0 (and no target object) starts no run. The executor's parent argument is only the parent aim's target: the invocation's parent comes from the item (0x455A20 → 0x450A50). | Original |
| IC11 | parent getter (0x450A50), invocation init (0x455A20) | 0x450A50 returns a critter itself, an item (types 5..14) with item flag 0x1000 (in an inventory) its holder (field 0x58), any other item or object itself, and none for none. 0x455A20 fills the value, the source, the source's location and the parent (0x450A50 of the source) and clears the target, the tile, the other object and the trigger fields. So an item on the map (a thrown or fired projectile's item after its end) runs its spells with itself as the parent, and its damage step takes the item as the attacker. | Original |
| IC12 | projectile tile crossing (0x4B2870) | Two calls of 0x4CBDB0, reached from three places, each with the shooter as parent and the projectile's item (field 0x50) as the item: (1) the destination end (the tile left is the destination and the shot has no target, or flies back, flag 0x2000): the projectile's end 0x4B2690, script point 7 (and 0x22 for a weapon with flag 4), then 0x4CBDB0 at the destination; (2) a strike (the target on its tile, a stray critter 1 in 3, or an obstruction): the context (0x4B2210), the projectile's end 0x4B2690, the hit 0x4B2F60, then 0x4CBDB0 at the struck object's location read after the hit, then the context's end (0x4B6930); (3) the range end (the distance beyond 20, the thrown range or the weapon's): 0x4B2690, script point 8 (and 0x23), then 0x4CBDB0 at the projectile's own tile. A projectile flying back (flag 0x2000) leaves at once anywhere but its destination. | Original |
| IC13 | damages an opponent (0x4CBE70), item parent | For a parent of an item type (5..14) one executor call with the parent as the item and the other object and the third argument (the victim) as the executor's parent, mask 0x40000 (`Target_Hit`), tile 0, no global; the weapon argument is not read. A critter parent walks its items (IC1); any other parent fires nothing. | Original |
| IC14 | references of 0x432700, 0x4CBD40, 0x4CBDB0; throw (0x4B3C00) | 0x432700 has no direct caller and one absolute reference, a node of an animation goal's table (0x5A7E90); 0x4CBD40 is called only from 0x432700, 0x4CBDB0 only from 0x4B2870 (called only from the flight node 0x42F6A0). The throw goal (0x42AB90, table 0x5AA0B0) runs 0x4B3C00, whose calls never reach 0x432700 or 0x4CBD40: a throw fires no attacks an opponent. Its dispatcher's throw branch (0x4B3170 → 0x4B39B0 → 0x4B24F0) creates the projectile with the thrown item in field 0x50, so the thrown item's flight ends fire attacks a location with it. | Original |
| IC15 | attack animation begin (0x432700) | In order: the attacker and target (asserted, none returns), a stoned attacker (field 0x14 bit 0x10000) or a stunned or paralysed critter (0xDA bits 0x60) fails, the action point charge (0x4B7C30, 0x4B7CD0; a refusal ends the goal with 6), the animation, its speed and sound, then 0x4CBD40 with the attacker as parent and the target as the other object. | Original (as IC6 and CT2) |

Installed data: as IC1's census; the 14 `Parent_Atks_Location` records are thrown items' (food 10105, 13 items of type 14). The
session throws only a wielded "default throws" weapon (TW7: the throwing dagger 6072, no item spells), so they fire only once a
host throws such an item (not ready).

**F0 facts raised:** 0x4CBDB0, 0x450A50, 0x455A20's parent and the executor's other object (F2); 0x432700's order (F2, as IC6).
Left at F0: the script points 7, 8, 0x22 and 0x23 of the projectile's ends (no script runs here).

## Spell missile and caster facing (ARC-MAGIC-018)

Source: [the spell cast host's missile](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#the-missile-arc-magic-018) and its casting goal's facing turn,
the second magic leftover after MT20a-2 (owner 2026-10-28). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task in the 1.0.7.4 executable
(`llvm-objdump`): the node tables of goals 26 [ag_throw_spell], 27 [ag_attempt_spell], 28 [ag_shoot_spell], 78
[ag_attempt_move_straight_spell] and 36 [ag_projectile] (goal-type table 0x5B03D0, names 0x5A164C) and the stepper's actions
(0x423560, the pop 0x423D10, the parameter loader 0x44DD80: slots 0..4 objects, 5 and 6 locations, 7..19 values; slot 31 the first
object's location, 34 slot 5 or else the target object's location written into it); the launch node 0x429C40 with 0x424820,
0x4248A0, 0x4246E0, 0x458B70, 0x458C00; goal 28's nodes 0x424D90, 0x425740, 0x426840, 0x42A180, 0x429C80, 0x424D00, 0x429ED0
(0x458CA0), 0x42BEC0 and its end 0x429E70; goal 78's 0x42CA90, 0x42F2D0, 0x42F390; the line 0x4201C0 with its sampler 0x420330;
the facing turn 0x42C850 with 0x4B8D50, 0x502930, 0x502220 and the cast start 0x455C30's goal slots; the art setters 0x4B6B10,
0x502710, 0x502780, 0x502930 for class 14. Object field, spell flag and critter flag names are the executable's own tables. A
second reader that saw only the questions (method step 6) read the same functions and agrees on every row; it added the missile's
sound (SM2) and found that nothing on goal 28's creation path sets the path flag (SM9, corrected here). Every direct branch of the
functions read stays inside `.text`. Installed counts are from the reader on the owner's install (opt-in `SpellEyeCandyRealDataTests`).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SM1 | casting goal 27, launch (0x429C40, then 0x424820, 0x4246E0) | After the release gate (0x42CAA0, frame flag 0x8 set) the node asks the run's missile art (0x458B70: the projectile entry 6·spell + 1, −1 without art or without a run parent, turned to the parent's art rotation). Without one the release (0x429C80) runs. With one it stores the art in the goal's slot 17, plays the secondary cast eye candy (6·spell + 4, 0x456E00) on the caster and creates the missile (0x4248A0, its handle into slot 3), then starts goal 28 on the missile (0x4246E0: only in single player or as a network host; goal 28's slots are goal 27's with the caster in slot 4 and the art in slot 8) and waits one pace. No target re-check and no BEGIN at the launch. The casting goal then plays its eye candy to the end; its end node frees nothing (CG7, flag 0x8). | Original |
| SM2 | missile object (0x4248A0, 0x458C00) | Prototype 5028 (the combat projectile's) at the caster's location; object flag OF_DONTLIGHT (0x100000). Its art is the missile art faced (0x4B6B10) in the screen direction (0x4B6A00) from the caster's location to the target object's location, or the target tile without one, set as its base and current art (fields 0x18 and 1). For an eye-candy art (class 14) the facing takes rotation direction mod 8 in bits 9..11 (0x502930) and the number (bits 19..27) raised by direction / 8 when it stays below 512 (0x502710, 0x502780). The entry's light (+0xC, colour +0x10) is attached when its art loads, and the entry's sound (+0x8, unless −1) is played on the missile (0x458C00). | Original |
| SM3 | goal 28 (0x5A8DD0) | The first node (0x424D90) asks whether the missile stands on slot 34 (the target tile, or the target object's location). If not: with the run's path flag (+0xC00 bit 0x1) set the line is built (0x426840), a refused line ends the run; then the speed is read (0x429ED0: the entry's `Proj Speed:`, 0x458CA0, into slot 18) and goal 78 is pushed. If so: the target test (0x42A180) passes without a target object or with the target object on the missile's tile, and the release runs (0x429C80: the re-check 0x456A90; no secondary cast eye candy, since the missile art exists; flags 0xC; the BEGIN) and the missile is destroyed (0x424D00), the goal popping (the run done). A target that moved: its location goes into slot 5 and the path flag is set (0x42BEC0), and the goal starts again: a new leg from the missile's tile. | Original |
| SM4 | goal 78 (0x42CA90, 0x42F2D0, 0x42F390) | The setup (run flag 0x10 clear) sets the run's pace to 1000 / the missile art's frame rate (100 ms when the art does not read; a rate of 0 divides by zero), the step index to 0, clears the path flag and sets flag 0x10, and restarts. The tick runs every 35 ms (delay code −3): slot 18's steps, or 4 when it is 0 (a negative speed takes none). Each step advances the art frame (0x502BC0), adds the line's next step to the pixel offsets, finds the tile under the point (0x4B8730; a point off the map stops the game with an error) and moves the missile (0x43E770). When the index reaches the line's end the tick clears flag 0x10 and pops with a pace's delay (−2). Unlike the combat projectile's tick (0x42F6A0, goal 36), nothing is called for the tiles crossed: no strike, no obstruction, no range. A leg that ends off its destination flies the same line again (goal 28 finds the path flag clear and pushes goal 78 again). | Original |
| SM5 | line (0x4201C0, 0x420330) | The combat projectile's line (PF3): the Bresenham line between the two tiles' centres, a step every 10 pixels as byte deltas; the 101st step fails the line (0 steps), so a leg longer than 100 steps is refused. | Original |
| SM6 | goal 28's release refused (0x429C80 false) | The run ends (action 0x90000000); the end node (0x429E70) destroys the missile unless already destroyed and frees nothing: as MG41, the run keeps its slot, never begun (freed unpaid in both settings since ARC-WORLD-108). | Original bug (MG41; freed unpaid in both settings since ARC-WORLD-108, no switch) |
| SM7 | facing turn (goal 26 node 8, 0x42C850) | After the start (0x429B50), 0x429BB0, the target node (0x429BC0), the range node (0x42BEA0, range 8) and the overlay reset (0x431A40). Slot 1 is the target object (0x455C30 gives goal 26 the run's target object +0xA0 and target tile +0xE0; a tile cast has none). No target object, or the caster as its own target: true, nothing turns. A caster with spell flag OSF_STONED (0x10000), or a PC or NPC caster with OCF_STUNNED or OCF_PARALYZED (0x60): false, and the goal goes to its end node (0x429CD0, MG39's path). Otherwise the caster's current art takes the rotation from its location toward the target's (0x4B8D50, 0x502930), set when that art exists (0x502220, 0x43EB30): an instant turn without animation. True: goal 26 is replaced by goal 27. | Original |
| SM8 | cast start (0x455C30, 0x455FC2–0x456052) | Before goal 26 is started, a rotation from the run's location toward the target is computed and applied to the parent's art id, and the result is dropped (nothing writes it): no effect. | Original (dead computation, no effect) |
| SM9 | path flag (run +0xC00 bit 0x1: 0x423A94, 0x423D93, 0x426840, 0x42F2D0, 0x42BEC0; run creation 0x44D520, 0x44D730, 0x44CDA0) | Set when a run ends through the end step (0x423A94), by a pop with action bit 0x2000000 (0x423D93; no action of goals 26, 27, 28 or 78 has it) and by the retarget; cleared by the line builder and goal 78's setup. Nothing on goal 28's creation path writes it, so its first leg builds a line only when the reused run slot's last run ended through the end step; otherwise goal 78 flies the slot's stale line, and a never-used slot (zeroed at start) has a line of no step, so the missile never advances. Whether a missile's slot can lack the flag in play was not checked. | Original (latent; Steamwoven always builds the first leg's line, no switch) |
| MG44 | goal 28 ended before the release (0x426840 false, the run stopped) | A refused leg (or any end of the missile's run before its release) reaches the same end node (0x429E70): the missile is destroyed, nothing is freed, and the casting goal's gate has already handled its frame, so the run keeps its slot, never begun, until the game ends. | Original bug (fix in both modes, owner decision 2026-10-28: fixed in both rule modes, no switch: the run is freed unpaid) |

**Installed missiles** (counts only): 12 spells have a projectile entry with art, 6 of them college spells (0, 2, 16, 22, 33,
34); one has `Proj Speed: 4`, the others none (the default 4); none is negative; all are eye-candy arts whose four faced numbers
fit. The cast smoke's missile cast learns the first rank-1 critter spell that has one (it does no damage).

**F0 facts raised:** the casting goal's facing turn and the missile of [FR-MAGIC-003](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-003)
and [FR-MAGIC-012](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-012) (goal 28 and 78, the launch, the facing:
F2). Left at F0: the missile's light and sound in the viewer, NPC casters' missiles in the viewer.

## Target picker (ARC-MAGIC-021)

Source: the [spell cast host's viewer](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#the-viewer-arc-magic-015) (its "Not ready" list: the
picker 0x4F28A0's own object/tile order), a magic leftover of MT20a-2; SU9 left the pick's order unread. Read on 2026-10-28 by
this task in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`llvm-objdump`): the window wrapper (0x4F2830), the pick (0x4F28A0), the stand-in
(0x4F2C60), the picker's wrapper (0x4F2680) with the result setters (0x4F27F0, 0x4F2810), and a scan of the whole `.text` for
the flag calls (0x43D0E0, 0x43D280) with an immediate 0x800; a second reader that saw only the questions (method step 6) read
the same functions and agrees on every row, adding the window wrapper's flag (SU16), the second wrapper 0x4F2CB0, the repeat of a
refused screen-picked object in the tile list (SU16) and the cleanup's own dead test (SU19). Every direct branch stays inside `.text`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SU15 | the pick's prologue (0x4F28A0; 0x441260, 0x441310, 0x440FC0, 0x43D0E0) | For the pick's duration object flag 0x800 (`OF_CLICK_THROUGH`) is set on: the local PC (0x40DA50) when the cursor's mask has `Tgt_Obj_No_Self`'s bit (low 0x10000000); every object of the PC's follower list (0x441260) when it has `Tgt_Non_Party_Critters`'s bit (high 0x10000000), and with the network active (0x62AC6C bit 0x1) every object of the PC's party list (0x441310) too; every dead (0x45D8D0) PC or NPC (type mask 0x18000) in the game-window-sized rectangle around the PC (0x440FC0) when it has the not-dead bit of `Tgt_Obj_No_ST_Critter_Dead` (low 0x80000000). The screen pick skips click-through objects (ARC-UI-005), so it sees through them, and the wrapper refuses them (SU17). | Original (the party list: single player only, not modelled) |
| SU16 | the pick's order (0x4F28A0, from 0x4F2830 and 0x4F2CB0) | 0x4F2830 answers nothing for a point outside the game window (0x603BB0..0x603BBC) and passes x relative to it, and y too unless its flag argument is set; 0x4F2CB0 does the same around a pick with a mask of its own arguments (saved and restored, 0x4F25D0, 0x4F25B0). The pick tries, and stops at the first candidate the wrapper (SU17) passes: (1) the object of the screen pick at the point (0x43D9F0); (2) when there is none or it fails, the tile under the point (0x4B8730; none: nothing is found) and the objects that tile lists (0x4407C0 with the mask 0x3FFEF, every type but projectiles; destroyed and switched-off objects are never listed, FC2), in the list's order, so a refused screen-picked object on that tile is tested again; (3) the tile itself. Each object stands for its occupant first (SU18). | Original (the order of the objects within one tile's list, the sector module's, is not read: F0 at 0x4407C0) |
| SU17 | the picker's wrapper (0x4F2680; 0x4F27F0, 0x4F2810) | The result is a handle or a location with a kind word (0 object, 1 tile). The wrapper fills the area test's context with the PC as source and self and the candidate as candidate and target: an object with no tiles, a tile with no objects. An object with flag 0x800 is refused untested. A passing area test (0x4F2D20) passes only when the cursor's mask is not 0 (no cursor opens with a mask of 0: SU3). An object that fails is tested once more with its own location (0x406DA0 field 2) as candidate and target tile, the object kept; when that passes, the result becomes that tile, not the object (so a tile spell clicked on a body casts at the body's tile, and a radius test measured from the caster passes again at distance 0 from the object's own tile). | Original |
| SU18 | the stand-in (0x4F2C60 in 0x4F28A0) | Before the wrapper, a scenery object (type 3) with an occupant (field 0x46) is replaced by the occupant: the cast's target is the sleeper, not the bed. | Original |
| SU19 | the pick's end (0x4F28A0, 0x43D280, 0x4410E0) | The same objects lose flag 0x800 again (0x43D280) whatever they carried before the pick — of the dead-critter list those 0x45D8D0 still finds dead — and their lists are freed. A pick that found nothing clears the result (object, handle 0) and answers false. The flag calls are given an immediate 0x800 nowhere else in `.text`. | Original; MG45 |
| MG45 | the pick's end (SU19) | An object of those lists that carried `OF_CLICK_THROUGH` before the pick (the local PC, a follower, a dead critter near the PC) loses it for good after a pick with that mask bit. No code sets the flag through the flag calls besides the pick, so only an object whose data carries it can lose it. | Original bug (technical, latent: fixed in both modes without a switch by the owner rule of 2026-10-28 — no flag is written, so such an object keeps it; whether the installed data has one is not checked) |

**F0 facts raised:** the spell UI's pick of [FR-MAGIC-012](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-012) and the target
choice of [FR-MAGIC-003](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-003) (0x4F2830, 0x4F28A0, 0x4F2680, 0x4F2C60 in the pick:
F2). Left at F0: the order within one tile's object list (0x4407C0 over the sector's lists) and the screen pick's own fallbacks
(0x43D9F0, ARC-UI-005's "not modelled").

## Item spell pane (ARC-UI-016)

Source: the [spell cast host](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#the-weapons-spell-pane-arc-ui-016), the item spell UI follow-up of
ARC-MAGIC-020 (IT9 left the windows that own 0x54BC68 and 0x54CF78 at F0). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task in the 1.0.7.4
executable (`llvm-objdump`): the bar's weapon button (0x556EA0, 0x556EF0, 0x556F80), the bar mode switch (0x5506C0), the mode-8
build (0x551660), the gauge at the end of 0x54B3C0, the two copies of the bar's click handler (0x54BC34, 0x54CF44) and its hover
(0x54C204), the inventory callback 0x5570D0 (installed by 0x57C6E0, called through 0x4602A0 from the insertion and the removal),
0x556E60, the hotkey activation (0x54FCF0 and its jump table), the hotkey keys (0x54DE50, 0x57F1D0), the hotkey bar's click handler
(0x57DC60), its drag start and drop (0x57E6C0..0x57E8A0, 0x57E9EC..0x57ED40) and its save and load (0x57DB40, 0x57DBA0).
A second reader that saw only the questions (method step 6) read the same functions and agrees on every row; it added the button's
record and visibility bit (IP1), the handlers' owners (IP2), the order of 0x57C040's tests (IP5), the quick-slot clear on a removal
(IP6) and the saved fields per entry type (IP9), and could not tell whether the key event's byte +0xC is the press (IP8, F1).
Every direct branch stays inside `.text`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IP1 | weapon button (0x556EF0, 0x556F80) | The bar's weapon button (0x5C6F74) is shown by 0x556EF0 only while hidden and only when the PC's item in slot 1004 (0x4649C0) has spell charges (field 0x69) not 0, negative included, or the magical flag (field 0x57 bit 0x4), and a magick/tech complexity (field 0x60) above 0. 0x556F80 hides it when shown. 0x556EF0 runs on every insertion into the PC's inventory (through 0x5570D0) and after the interface's load (0x54A2EC, right after the hotkeys' load); 0x556F80 on a removal from slot 1004 (0x5570D0) and once the button is created (0x54A680). Visibility is bit 0x8 of the window table (0x5388B0 asks, 0x5387E0 shows, 0x538840 hides); the spell fields are not looked at. A weapon whose charges run out keeps its button until the next inventory move. | Original bug (technical: a stale button; fixed in both modes without a switch by the owner rule 2026-10-28: the viewer re-reads the condition each frame) |
| IP2 | weapon button press (0x556EA0 from 0x54BA6E and 0x54CD7E; 0x5506C0) | The two copies of the bar's click handler (0x54C8E0, the bar window's handler installed at 0x54A3E7, and its twin 0x54B5D0) call 0x556EA0 with no object for the weapon button: the item global (0x64C688) becomes the PC's item in slot 1004, then the bar mode 8 is requested (0x5506C0). 0x556EA0 with an object stores that object instead (no caller passes one). 0x5506C0 requesting the current mode returns the bar to mode 0, so a second press closes the pane. | Original |
| IP3 | mode-8 build (0x551660) | Nothing without a local PC or an item in the global. Only an identified item (field 0x57 bit 0x1) with a complexity above 0 gets buttons; then, for the five slots in order, a slot shows its button when its value (0x4CB7A0, −1 for 10000) is not −1, its record is not technological (record flag 0x4, 0x459FC0) and the button exists; the button's icon is the spell's (0x4B1570). A record with item triggers shows its button. The pane is the item's own: other items are never shown. | Original |
| IP4 | the gauge (end of 0x54B3C0) | In bar mode 8 with an item, the gauge shows the item's charges for an identified item (a negative count as −1, unlimited) and −2 (nothing known) for an unidentified one. | Original |
| IP5 | press and hover (0x54BC34 and 0x54CF44 → 0x57C040; 0x54C204 → 0x5507E0) | A press of a mode-8 button calls 0x57C040 with the item global and the button's index (IT9: the activation only for a value that is not −1 and a record without item triggers; the trigger read 0x45A030 comes before the −1 test, so an empty slot reads before the table, as IU6, and the −1 test then discards it), so a shown button whose record has item triggers does nothing. Hovering one shows the description of the slot's spell (0x5507E0 with the slot value). | Original (the explicit trigger test: by design, not a dead button) |
| IP6 | inventory callback (0x5570D0, from 0x4669D1 and 0x467BE7; 0x556E60 from 0x57CAD1) | Arguments: the item, a removed flag and the item's inventory location. On a removal (flag 1): the hotkey entries whose object is the item are cleared (0x57EF90); when the item is the mode-8 item and the bar is in mode 8, the bar returns to mode 0 and the global is cleared; the two quick slots naming it are cleared too (0x57F2C0); a removal from slot 1004 hides the weapon button (0x556F80). On an insertion (flag 0) only the item entries' counts are refreshed (0x462B40) and 0x556EF0 runs. 0x556E60 clears the global when the item's parent (field 0x58) is no longer a player's PC. | Original |
| IP7 | hotkey activation (0x54FCF0, jump table 0x54FFDC) | Nothing for an entry flagged 0x1 (being dragged), without a local PC, for a dead or unconscious PC, or one whose critter flags (field 0xDA) hold 0x20 or 0x40. By the entry's type (+0x8): 1 an item (field +0x10, used or wielded by its type), 2 a skill (0x579FA0 with the PC), 3 a spell (0x57BC70 with the PC), 4 an object and a spell (+0x10, +0xC): nothing unless the object's parent is the PC; the wielded weapon goes straight to 0x57C080; another item first needs the wielded weapon (if any) to come off (0x464C80) and itself to be wielded into slot 1004 (0x464AE0), else nothing, then 0x57C080. | Original (the hotkeys: a later slice) |
| IP8 | hotkey keys and clicks (0x54DE50 → 0x57F1D0; 0x57DC60) | A key event of scan codes 2..11 (the keys 1..0) whose byte +0xC is 0 (read as the press, F1) activates the entry 0..9, unless another critter acts in a turn-based round or the condition 0x566EF0 holds; an entry being dragged or empty is skipped. A left click on a hotkey button activates its entry; the two quick slots at 0x683518 (the last spell and skill used, written by the spell and skill bars, 0x57EFA0) are activated by their buttons and the first one by the key A. | Original (a later slice) (corrected by HK2, HK8) |
| IP9 | hotkey drag, drop and save (0x57E6C0..0x57E8A0, 0x57E9EC..0x57ED40, 0x57DB40, 0x57DBA0) | Dragging from the spell pane makes a type-3 entry (the spell), from the skill bar type 2, from the mode-8 pane type 4 (the item global and its slot's spell; a slot whose record has item triggers cannot be dragged: 0x557C00 answers 5); an item dragged on a slot is type 1 and moves into inventory location 2000 + slot. A drop overwrites the slot (a drag from another hotkey slot swaps or clears it). The ten entries and the two quick slots are written to and read from the save (0x57DB40, 0x57DBA0): each entry's type and index, then for type 1 the item's id and count, for types 2 and 3 the skill or spell, for type 4 the item's id and the spell; the load sets each entry through 0x57EFA0. | Original (a later slice) (corrected by HK2, HK5, HK7) |
| IP10 | mode-8 release, hover, icon, payment and toggles (0x54B5D0 release branch, 0x5507E0 → 0x4B1600, 0x4B1570, 0x57BC70, 0x450420, 0x4605D0 → 0x54B3C0, 0x5506C0; ARC-UI-2304) | The weapon button (record 0x5C6F68, made by 0x54AAE0 with the push sounds 3000 and 3001, no hover message) and the mode-8 slot buttons act on the release over them, in the handler's release branch beside the spell pane's 0x54BAF5, not on the press. Slot i's button stands at entry i of 0x5C6C18: (353, 542), (402, 542), (451, 542), (501, 542), (550, 542). Its icon is 0x4B1570: the table 0x5B5630 for spells 0..79, else art 35 for a technological record and 36 otherwise. Hovering a slot shows the slot spell's name (0x4B1600: the spell.mes line equal to the spell's number), not its description (corrects IP5), centred in mode 8's text area (355, 506, 227 × 18). The activation (0x57BC70) makes the item's holder the caster and keeps the item as the source: nothing for a caster that is not the player's PC, is off, dead or unconscious, or a disabled spell; a self-type spell is cast at once at the caster's tile, any other opens the targeting cursor after the action point check (4 in the caster's own turn-based subturn). BEGIN's payment (0x450420) takes exactly one charge from field 105 for any Cost above 0 and sets the magical flag; a negative count spends nothing; 0 refuses and a PC parent gets spell.mes 600; the holder's fatigue is never paid. The pane is not rebuilt: only the bar's counters refresh (0x4605D0 → 0x54B3C0), which set counter 3 (the wielded weapon's ammunition, else its mana store field 92 with icon 469, else the hero's gold with icon 474) and, in mode 8, counter 4 (IP4). 0x5506C0 changes only the bar mode: the Spells and Skills toggles keep their drawing, so Spells can stay drawn down in mode 8 and its next press draws it up while it opens mode 1. | Original; the toggles' drawing is a technical bug of the exe: both rule modes bring both toggles up when mode 8 opens (owner decision 2026-11-15, #2312, [decisions](../../architecture/decisions.md)); the bar counters are built by ARC-UI-2311 ([BC1–BC8](#bottom-strip-counters-3-and-4-arc-ui-2311)). F1 |

**F0 facts raised:** the item spell UI of [FR-MAGIC-012](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-012) (0x556EF0, 0x556EA0,

drag and drop and the save: F2, implemented by a later slice). Left at F0: the drag's hit test over a hidden mode-8 button

## Hotkey bar (ARC-UI-022)

Source: the [spell cast host](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#the-hotkey-bar-arc-ui-022), the hotkey slice of ARC-UI-016 (IP7–IP9 above).
Read on 2026-10-28 by this task in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`llvm-objdump`; jump tables from the file): the activation 0x54FCF0 and its
tables 0x54FFDC and 0x54FFEC, 0x57C080, the hotkey wear 0x550000, the item helpers 0x462930 and 0x462C30, the cursor hold 0x573620 and
0x573740, the skill bar 0x579FA0 and 0x57A0A0 with its table 0x5CB250, the recent-action set 0x57EFA0 and its callers, the save and load
0x57DB40 and 0x57DBA0, the keys (0x54DE50, 0x57F1D0, 0x57DC60), the inventory callback 0x5570D0 with 0x57F2C0, and the drop on the bar
0x57E8D0..0x57ED40 with 0x465760. A second reader that saw only the questions (method step 6) read the same functions and agrees on every
row; it added the lost cast of HK4 and the recent-action object slip of HK2, and found the wear move reaching the callback (HK6). Every
direct branch stays inside `.text`. It corrects IP8 and IP9: the two "quick slots" are the latest two recent actions of any press of the
spell pane or the skill bar (HK2), not the last spell and the last skill, and only they go through 0x57EFA0 on a load (HK7); the ten
entries are read back directly.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HK1 | item entry (0x54FCF0 type 1, 0x462930, 0x462C30, 0x550000, 0x573620, 0x573740) | Nothing unless the entry's item is the PC's. The item acted on is the first other item of its prototype the PC holds (0x462930; none for the description 0x3AD9, AM1), except for a written item (type 13) that can be used or whose entry item is thrown, which keeps itself. It is used (0x462CC0) when it can be used (item flag 0x80; 0x462C30 answers 0, else 6) and the entry's own item is not thrown (flag 0x10000): with Shift held (scan codes 0x2A, 0x36) on the PC, else with no target. Otherwise by its type: a weapon wears the entry's own item into slot 1004 and an armour the entry's own item into the acted-on item's wear location (0x550000); ammunition and gold do nothing; anything else is held on the cursor (0x573740) after a held item is put back (0x575770) and the inventory window closes (0x573440), unless the entry's own item is held already. | Original |
| HK2 | recent actions (0x57EFA0; callers 0x54BB8F, 0x54BBDD, 0x54CE9F, 0x54CEED, 0x57DBD4, 0x57F301) | Two recent actions at 0x683518 (the latest) and 0x683570. A press of the spell pane (type 3, the spell) or of the skill bar (type 2, the button) records itself before its activation; an action equal to the latest (the type, then the item for type 1, the index for types 2 and 3, both for type 4) changes nothing, otherwise the latest moves to the second and the action becomes the latest. The new action's object is written to the second slot, not the latest; only a load writes types 1 and 4. Key A activates the latest, the two buttons each their slot. | Original (the object slip: technical, fixed in both modes; it only bites on a load of types the bars never write, which the session refuses) |
| HK3 | item-and-spell entry (0x54FCF0 type 4, 0x464C80, 0x464AE0) | Nothing unless the PC holds the item. The wielded weapon goes to 0x57C080 at once; another item first needs the wielded weapon off (a refusal: nothing) and the item wielded into slot 1004; a refused wield does nothing more and leaves the old weapon off. | Original (the weapon left off: technical, fixed in both modes: the session's wear swaps in one step and a refusal changes nothing) |
| HK4 | the cast after the wield (0x54FFC1) | After the wield, 0x57C080 reads the item and the spell from the entry again; the wield already ran the removal callback (HK6), which emptied that entry, so the activation gets no object and casts nothing. | Original bug (technical: a lost action; fixed in both modes without a switch: the item and spell read before the wield are cast, the entry still empties) |
| HK5 | drop on the bar (0x57E8D0..0x57ED40, 0x465760, 0x462A30) | A held item is refused (put back) when it is not the PC's, is ammunition or gold, or another item of its prototype is on an entry already (message 4000). Dropped on its own entry nothing changes. Otherwise the target's old item goes back into the PC's inventory at an automatic place, the source entry (a drag from another entry) takes the target's type and index without its object, and the target becomes a type-1 entry whose item moves to the inventory location 2000 + entry, off the grid. Spells, skill-bar buttons and a mode-8 slot's spell make types 3, 2 and 4. | Original (the source left without its object: technical, fixed in both modes: two entries trade places) |
| HK6 | inventory callback (0x5570D0 via 0x4602A0 from 0x467BE7; 0x57F2C0) | Every removal of an item from its place runs it, so a wield (0x464AE0 → 0x466E00 → 0x467860), a take-off and a move in the grid do too: every entry whose object is the item empties, whatever its type, and either recent action naming it is replaced by a default skill. On every call the item entries' counts are recomputed. The hotkey wear (0x550000) also empties its entry when it succeeds. | Original |
| HK7 | save and load (0x57DB40, 0x57DBA0) | Saved: the latest recent action, the second, then the ten entries, each with its type, its slot and by type the item's id and count, the skill or the spell, or the item's id and the spell. Loaded: the ten entries directly (an item id that no longer resolves empties the entry; an item-and-spell entry's fails the whole load), and the two recent actions through 0x57EFA0 in saved order, so a second action comes back as the latest: the two trade places on every load. | Original bug (technical: a load changes the state; fixed in both modes without a switch: the session restores the bar as saved) |
| HK8 | keys (0x54DE50 → 0x57F1D0, 0x57DC60) | The keys 1..0 (scan codes 2..11, a key event with byte +0xC 0) activate the entries 0..9 unless another critter has the turn in a turn-based round (the handler exits) or 0x566EF0 holds; 0x57F1D0 skips an entry being dragged or empty and plays sound 0. The character A or a activates the latest recent action unless 0x566EF0 or 0x541680 holds. A click on an entry's button activates it while nothing is dragged or held; with an item held it drops the item there. | Original |
| HK9 | skill bar (0x579FA0, 0x57A0A0, 0x5CB250) | A type-2 entry's index is a skill-bar button: 0 prowling (the toggle, ST1), 1 and 2 open the skill cursor (target masks 0x40000044 and object or tile: pick pocket and disarm traps, told apart by the masks), 3 repair (inventory target, RP4). | Original |

**F0 facts raised:** the hotkey bar of [FR-MAGIC-012](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-012) and

conditions 0x566EF0 and 0x541680, the default skill 0x557B50 that replaces a recent action naming a removed item, the cursor art of a held

## Hotkey bar art and drag (ARC-UI-027)

Source: the [spell cast host](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#the-hotkey-bars-art-and-drag-arc-ui-027), the art and pointer slice of the
hotkey bar (HK1–HK9 above). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`llvm-objdump`; tables and records from the file):
the bar's start and reset 0x57D700, 0x57D840 and 0x57DAB0 with their callers 0x549F60, 0x54A0F0 and 0x54A667, the slot draw 0x57E140 with
the slot button 0x57F160, the hit test 0x57E460, the drag start 0x57E5D0, the drop 0x57E8D0..0x57ED40 with 0x57ED60 and 0x57DC20, the events
0x57DC60, the in-game filter's mouse branch (0x54B5D0..0x54B8C7), the engine's mouse messages (0x4FF390..0x4FF990, tables 0x5BE840..0x5BE878),
the icons 0x4B1570 (table 0x5B5630), 0x579F70 (table 0x5CB230) and 0x554BE0/0x554C20, the count 0x462B40, the recent-action records
(0x557B20, 0x557B50, 0x5C6F40, 0x5C6F60) and the light button's record 0x5C6F30; the art names from the installed `art\interface\interface.mes`.
One reader (F1); every direct branch stays inside `.text`.

Then, unless the entry is being dragged (its flag 1) or empty or has no icon, its icon: centred in the slot's 32 × 32 (half the spare pixels each way, truncated) or clipped to it from the art's top-left. A skill-bar button's is 279 prowling, 280 pick pocket, 282 disarm traps or 278 repair (prowling's outside 0..3). An item entry draws its item's inventory art (field 0x5D) when both sides are at most 32, otherwise its type icon (0x554C20: by item type, a hexed weapon or armour, its magick or technology complexity and its subtype, interface arts 391..438 and 791..797). | HK13 | recent-action buttons (0x57D840, 0x57DAB0, 0x57EFA0 at 0x57F080, records 0x5C6F40, 0x557B50) | The two recent actions are buttons at (69, 548), the latest, and (114, 548), made by the bar's button creator (sounds 3000 and 3001, GB6) with the action's icon as their art; the recent-action set gives the moved button its icon. A left release over one activates its action (0x54FCF0, no turn test), whatever is dragged or held: the branch tests neither; the pointer entering shows its description (0x550150). A new bar holds the skill-bar buttons 1 (pick pocket, the latest) and 0 (prowling) (table 0x5C6F60). They are no drag source: a drag starts from the bar's entries, the spell pane, the skill bar or the weapon's pane only (HK15). | Original |

A right press with no item held and no drag going on starts a drag: from an entry under the pointer that is not empty and not being dragged (an item entry's item goes on the cursor, 0x573630), else (not in the compact interface) from a spell of the spell pane (type 3), a skill-bar button (type 2) or a slot of the weapon's pane (type 4); the entry hides its icon (flag 1) and the cursor shows the icon. A right release ends it; a left release ends it only while an item is held (the inventory's held item becomes a type-1 drag, 0x57DC20). Over an entry the drop is HK5's (on its own entry: its item's sound and nothing else). The pointer entering an entry that is not empty shows its description (0x550150). | Original |
