## Hotkey bar item icons (ARC-UI-032)

Source: the [spell cast host](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#the-hotkey-bars-item-icons-arc-ui-032), the item-icon slice of the hotkey bar
(HK12 above). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`llvm-objdump`; tables from the file): the type icon 0x554C20 with
its wrapper 0x554BE0, the item art getters 0x5044F0 (subtype), 0x504490 (place), 0x504550 (type), 0x504570 (coverage) and 0x502710
(number), the item art name 0x4EC290 with its name tables' init (0x4EC180..0x4EC240), the identified test 0x464370, the tables
0x5C6FE4..0x5C70A8 and the jump table at 0x554EDC; the field numbers from the executable's own field-name table (0x59BEA8); the art names
from the installed `art\interface\interface.mes`, and the inventory arts counted in the installed `art\item\item_inven.mes`. Then the
hover dispatcher 0x550150 with the callees it reaches (0x5507E0, 0x5508C0, 0x57CCF0 → 0x57CD60, 0x553BE0, 0x557370), for the next slice.
One reader (F1); every direct branch stays inside `.text`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HK17 | type icon (0x554C20; 0x464370, 0x5044F0, 0x504570, tables 0x5C6FE4..0x5C70A8, jump table 0x554EDC) | The icon is an interface art number chosen by the item's type (field 0x14F), magick/tech complexity (field 0x60) and, by type, its use-art fragment's subtype (field 0x5F, bits 6..9; 0 for an art id of another class), its inventory art's coverage (field 0x5D, bits 14..16), its ammunition type (0x8F), written subtype (0xCA) or discipline (0x61). An identified (0x464370) item with item flag 0x40 (field 0x57, the flag the AI reads as hexed, SP10) is hexed: a weapon of subtype 8 shows 792 (`hexedBowIcon`), of subtypes 2, 3, 4, 5, 7 or 13 791 (`HexedMeleeicon`), of any other 440 (`HexedIcon`); an armour with coverage 0 shows 797 for subtype 5, 794 for 1 or 8, else 793; with coverage 5 796, 6 795, another 793; any other type 440. Otherwise: a weapon (5) takes the weapon table by subtype (391 general melee, 392 at 6 and 10, 395 bows at 8), an armour (7) with coverage 0 the armour table by subtype (402 clothes, 405 shields, 408 robes) and with another coverage the coverage table (405, 411 rings at 5, 414 medallions at 6); both then add 1 for a positive complexity and 2 for a negative one (the magick and tech icons follow each general one). Ammunition (6) takes the ammunition table by type (398 arrows, 399 bullets, 400 charges, 401 fuel); gold (8) 417; food (9) 418 potions for a positive complexity, 423 food for 0, and for a negative one 419 therapeutics for discipline 7, 420 herbal for 0, 421 chemical for 1, else 423; a scroll (10) 425, a key (11) 426, a key ring (12) 427; a written item (13) the written table by subtype (428 books, 429 note, 431 newspaper, 430 telegram, 428 picture, 462 schematic, 428 plaque); a generic item (14) 432, 433 when magickal, 434 when technological. Another type returns its own number. No index is checked: the five tables lie one after the other (weapons 15, ammunition 4, armour 9, coverage 7, written 7) and are followed by the message window's icons (ML5), so subtype 15 reads the first ammunition icon, armour subtypes 9..15 and coverage 7 read the next table on (armour subtype 9 reads the coverage table's unused 0). | Original (the over-reads latent: the installed items use the tables' own range) |
| HK18 | item icon art (0x554BE0; 0x504330, 0x4EC290, 0x504490, 0x502710, 0x504550) | 0x554BE0 makes an interface art id of 0x554C20's number (0x504330 with three zero arguments); a null item gives −1. An item art id (class 6) names its file by its place (bits 12..13): 0 `art\item\item_ground.mes`, 1 `item_inven.mes`, 2 `item_paper.mes`, 3 `item_schematic.mes` (loaded together at 0x4EC180), each looked up with the same number: bits 17..27 + 20 × (subtype bits 6..9 + 50 × type bits 0..3), an armour (type 2) with coverage c ≠ 0 adding 20 × (5c + 10); the file is `art\item\` + the message. The installed `item_inven.mes` names 712 numbers, 705 of whose files exist in the installed archives; every one has a single group of one frame, and 134 are at most 32 × 32 (HK12's test), so most item entries show their type icon. | Original |
| HK19 | hover descriptions (0x550150 → 0x5507E0, 0x5508C0, 0x57CCF0) | An entry with its flag 1 (being dragged) shows nothing. By its type (field +8): an item (1) calls 0x57CCF0 with the local PC (0x40DA50) and the item — the same call the inventory window makes for the item under a still pointer (0x573C74, mouse subtype 7, with the window's holder); a skill-bar button (2) calls 0x5508C0 and a spell or an item's spell (3, 4) 0x5507E0 with the entry's index. In the message mode (0x64C6A8 = 0) these post a transient message-window entry: type 7 with 0x57A6A0 of the button for a skill, type 8 with the spell and the local PC for a spell (that entry also passed to 0x552770); in another bar mode they draw only the name (0x57A700 for a skill, 0x4B1600 for a spell) into that mode's text area (0x5509C0, rectangle 0x5C6D60 + 20 × mode). | Original (implemented by ARC-UI-035, HK20–HK23) |

The start ends before this when the speaker cannot speak (0x4AD800 with the speaker and the PC), or when the PC itself is dead or unconscious (0x45D8D0, 0x45D800 test the initiator), and a conversation state of 4 (+0x17F0) answers with the generic greeting (0x568540) and returns before the examine. The order menu's own rows have their own cases of the same callback (OL34). | Original; built (ARC-UI-1281: the hover of a follower portrait runs the examine and describes the follower in the message window, leaving clears) |

## Maintain bar (ARC-MAGIC-026)

Source: the [spell cast host](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#the-maintain-bar-arc-magic-026), magic plan slice MT20b (owner bug report
2026-10-29 10:41:46: the five top slots did not show or end maintained spells). Read on 2026-10-29 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`llvm-objdump`;
tables from the file) by this task and independently by a second reader, who agreed on every row (F2): the bar's add 0x57C290, remove
0x57C320, click 0x57C370, hover 0x57C3F0, reset 0x57C470, open count 0x57C4B0 and slot query 0x57C520; the spell test 0x459FF0 and the
icon 0x458AE0; the slot draw 0x553620 → 0x5533C0 and the enable 0x553910 → 0x553670; the hook table built at 0x57C6E0 and copied at
0x45FD40 (0x5E86A0..0x5E86AC); the button records 0x5C6E40 and 0x5C6E90 and the per-slot windows 0x5C6378 (rectangles 0x5C6EE0); the
callers in the in-game handlers 0x54B5D0 and 0x54C8E0; the save and load 0x57BB60 and 0x57BBB0. Every direct branch stays inside
`.text`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MB1 | table (0x5CB3A8, 0x57C470, 0x57C520) | Five entries {run, state}, 8 bytes each: state 0 empty, 1 showing its run, 2 its stop asked. The reset (0x57C470, the Spell-UI module's reset entry 0x57BB40) sets each to {−1, 0}, redraws it empty and sets the cached open count 0x5CB3D0 to −1; 0x57C520 answers whether a slot's state is not 0. | Original |
| MB2 | add (0x57C290 through the hook 0x5E86A4, the scheduler's 0x4604A0) | Refused (0) unless the run's record is fetched and its spell is 0..222 (0x459FF0). The open count is the PC's effective intelligence / 4 toward zero, without an upper bound. Over the first count entries: an entry in state 1 with the same run answers 1 and writes nothing; else the first entry whose run is −1 (the run only is tested) takes it: run, state 1, the slot's art set to the spell's icon (0x458AE0 → 0x4B1570), answer 1. | Original (MG12 below) |
| MB3 | add's bounds (0x57C2E9) | The "none found" value −1 is compared with the count by a signed `≥`: with every open entry taken, or a count of 0, slot −1 is written (the run into 0x5CB3A0, a neighbouring variable, and 1 into 0x5CB3A4), the records before the table redrawn, and 1 answered; a count above 5 scans and writes past the five entries (into 0x5CB3D0 and beyond). | Original bug, out of bounds (MG12): fixed in both modes — a full bar refuses the run (the scheduler then ends it) and at most five slots open |
| MB4 | remove (0x57C320 through the hook 0x5E86A8, the run rules' 0x4604C0) | For a run other than −1, the first entry holding it in a state of 1 or more becomes {−1, 0} and, below index 5, is redrawn empty (0x553620 with −1). | Original |
| MB5 | click (0x57C370; callers 0x54BD5F and 0x54D06F, event 1, the release over the button) | Only an entry whose run is not −1 and whose state is 1: the state becomes 2; in single player (bit 0 of 0x62AC6C clear) the run is interrupted (0x457110); with bit 0 set an 8-byte message {0x3B, run} is sent (0x5295F0) and the interrupt runs only with bit 1 set. A press calls neither the click nor the hover. The slots are push buttons of the bars' creator 0x54AA60 (press 3000, release 3001, GB6). | Original |
| MB6 | hover (0x57C3F0; callers 0x54C38E and 0x54D69E, event 2, the pointer entering) | Only an entry in state 1 whose run is fetched: the spell's description is posted (0x5507E0 with the record's spell, as the hotkey bar's spell hover, HK19/HK20), then the world's hovered-object routine 0x43C270 gets the first non-null of the run's handles at +0xA0, the one +0x134 points to, and +0x58 (none: no call). | Original (the description implemented; the hovered object not) |
| MB7 | open count (0x57C4B0, the interface refresh 0x5492D8 and the hook 0x5E86AC) | With a PC: count = intelligence / 4; equal to the cached 0x5CB3D0 does nothing; otherwise slots below the count are enabled (0x553910 with 1) and the slots from the count to 4 disabled (0x553910 with 0), and the count is cached. No clamp: a count above 5 enables slots 5 and up (MG12). | Original (MG12 capped in both modes) |
| MB8 | slot draw (0x553620 → 0x5533C0, 0x553910 → 0x553670; records 0x5C6E40) | The five buttons stand at (281 + 50 i, 3) on the top strip (window 0) with art 188 + i (SplSlt1–5); a second, identical set (0x5C6E90) is drawn in five 32 × 32 windows (0x5C6378, rectangles 0x5C6EE0). A slot with an icon gets the icon as its button art; an emptied slot gets art 188 + i and, at or beyond intelligence / 4 (5 without a PC), art 628 + i (SP_Plugged1–5) blitted one pixel up and left of the button. Disabling a slot: one already plugged stays; an empty one is disabled and plugged; one showing an icon is clicked (0x57C370 at 0x5538F8), so a drop of the open count ends the spell of every slot it closes. Enabling: the plug is drawn over with the strip and the button enabled. | Original |
| MB9 | save and load (0x57BB60, 0x57BBB0; the Spell-UI module table 0x5C2CB0) | The five entries are saved with the game (state 2 included) and read back on load, each non-empty slot redrawn with its icon; the cached count is not saved. | Original (the bar and its settled runs are saved since ARC-DEV-210) |

**F0 facts raised:** the maintain bar of [FR-MAGIC-006](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-006): 0x57C290, 0x57C320, 0x57C370,

## Spell pane (ARC-UI-053)

Source: the [spell cast host](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#the-spell-pane-in-the-bottom-strip-arc-ui-053), the owner's playtest report
of 2026-10-29 (the `M` pane was a text list without colleges or icons). Read on 2026-10-29 by this task in the 1.0.7.4 executable
(`llvm-objdump`; tables from the file): the bar-mode request 0x5506C0 and the Spells/Skills toggle 0x54DBF0 (from the `M` and `K` key
releases at 0x54C5A8 and 0x54C5D0), the mode set 0x552130, the mode leave 0x5504F0 (jump table 0x550678) and build 0x551210 (jump table
0x551620) with its background draw 0x5503F0 (table 0x5C6548), the interface's button init 0x5501C0 with the spell buttons' creation
0x550BD0, the college's spells shown 0x550C60 and hidden 0x550CD0, the in-game handler 0x54B5D0's release branch 0x54BAF5 and press branch
0x54BE1F, its enter branch 0x54C18D and the hovers 0x550860 and 0x5507E0, the selection's init 0x54A58C and its save and load 0x54A1C4 and
0x54A252, the magic tables 0x4B1A80 (0x5B55B0), 0x4B1570 (0x5B5630), 0x4B1A40 (names loaded at 0x4B1454 from `spell.mes` 500 +
college), 0x4B1B00 and 0x4B1950 (with 0x4B1AB0), and the button records 0x5C6618 and 0x5C6718 (x, y, art, handle). Art sizes from the
installed `art\interface` files. One reader (F1); every direct branch stays inside `.text`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SPN1 | mode request and background (0x54DBF0, 0x5506C0, 0x552130, 0x551210, 0x5503F0, table 0x5C6548) | The `M` key's release and the Spells button request bar mode 1, the spells; `K` and Skills mode 2; a request for the mode already shown returns to mode 0, the messages. Every mode's build first draws its background through 0x5503F0 from the table at 0x5C6548 (x, y, art by mode): mode 0 art 354, mode 1 art 8 (`spell_ro.art`, 410 × 107), mode 2 274, mode 8 564, all at (196, 492) of the screen. | Original |
| SPN2 | buttons (0x5501C0, 0x550BD0, 0x54AAE0; records 0x5C6618, 0x5C6718; tables 0x5B55B0, 0x5B5630) | The interface's init creates, as the bar's push buttons (sounds 3000 and 3001, GB6), a button per college with art 0x4B1A80(college) (table 0x5B55B0: 11, 44, 38, 92, 50, 86, 56, 98, 62, 68, 74, 104, 110, 80, 116, 122, the 24 × 24 `s_<college>.art` files with three frames) at y 497 and x 201, 226, 251, 276, 301, 326, 351, 376, 400, 425, 450, 475, 500, 525, 550, 575; and five buttons per college, one per rank, with the spell's icon 0x4B1570(5 × college + rank) (32 × 32, two frames) at y 528 and x 284, 334, 384, 435, 485 for every college. All start hidden. | Original |
| SPN3 | mode-1 build (0x551210 case 1, 0x4B1B00, 0x550C60, 0x4B1950) | The build shows the button of every college whose rank the PC has above 0 (0x4B1B00 reads it through 0x4B1AB0, a critter's only), sets the selected college's button (0x64C530) down (button state 0) and shows that college's spell buttons rank by rank while the PC knows the spell (0x4B1950: rank + 1 at most the college's rank), stopping at the first it does not know. The selection starts at 0 (0x54A58C), so a PC without conveyance sees the colleges and no spell until it presses one. Leaving mode 1 hides the college buttons and the selected college's spells (0x5504F0 case 1, 0x550CD0). | Original |
| SPN4 | presses (0x54BE1F, 0x54BAF5 → 0x57EFA0, 0x57BC70) | A college button's press (button event 0) in mode 1 hides the selected college's spells, selects the pressed college and shows its spells; it sets no button state, so only the next build draws the new selection down. A spell button's release over it (event 1) in mode 1, for a spell the PC knows, records the spell as the latest recent action (HK2) and activates it with the PC (SU1). A right press on a spell button starts the hotkey drag (HK15). The selection is written to the save after the bar mode (0x54A1C4) and read back (0x54A252), which also sets the selected college's button down when the loaded mode is 1. | Original (the save and the down look's lag: not ready, display only) |

Leaving changes nothing in mode 1 (0x550720 acts in mode 0 only). | Original |
| SPN6 | window (ML8) | Mode 1 holds the message window while the pointer is over the strip: a move above it returns to the messages, a move back over it to the spells while the Spells toggle is down (ML8); each return builds the mode again (SPN3). | Original |

## Spell destroy component (ARC-DEV-259)

Source: the [spell cast host](../../world/spell-cast-host.md) (B14, the thrown grenade of the installed data: spells 175 and 179 started as runs at the
shot's end but the grenade stayed on the ground). Read on 2026-11-08 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`llvm-objdump`; the dispatch table from the
file image) by this task alone (F1): the component executors' table at 0x5B0BA8 (file offset 0x1B0BA8, 25 entries in type order), the
Destroy component 0x451AF0, the object destroy 0x43CCA0 (read before, TD2) and the object-gone run endings 0x459740. Nothing was run, patched or
copied; every direct branch stays inside `.text`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DS1 | component table (0x5B0BA8, entries in type order from the no-op 0x452F00) | The Destroy component (type 7) is the executor 0x451AF0, between the Damage component 0x4518D0 (type 6) and the Dispel component 0x451B90 (type 8); the table runs no-op 0x452F00, AGoal 0x4514E0, AGoal terminate 0x4516D0, AI redirect 0x451700, Cast 0x451740, charge-and-branch 0x451850, Damage, Destroy, Dispel, Effect 0x451F20, environment flag 0x452F10, EyeCandy 0x451FE0, Heal 0x4521A0. (The magic plan's "destroy (0x451AF0)" and "dispel (0x451AF0..)" were one address; the table settles it.) | Original |
| DS2 | Destroy component (0x451AF0) | The component's target is the global 0x5E6D48 (the object the per-target stage handed the component, as the Damage executor 0x4518D0 reads it); a null handle returns. The run is the global 0x5E75F0: with a run whose resistance margin (+0x144) is not 0, an object whose spell flags (field 0x14) lack 0x4000 (summoned) is left as it is; any other case calls the object destroy 0x43CCA0 on the target. In a network game (0x62AC6C bits 0 and 1 set) a packet of the destroy is sent too. Nothing is paid or rolled. | Original |
| DS3 | the installed grenades (census of the installed spell data) | The `Parent_Atks_Location` records are thrown items' records (ledger IC): spell 175 (item 15152: a fire damage 5 to 30 and a normal damage 5 to 30, two eye candies, a knockback, a summon, a third eye candy, the Destroy) and spell 179 (item 15172: a damage of 0 to 0, two eye candies, a summon, an eye candy, the Destroy) end their BEGIN with a Destroy component whose `Apply_AoE:` is self (target mask 0x1) and carry a Destroy component in their CALLBACK too. A thrown item is its own parent (0x450A50) and its own source (IT4), so self is the item: the grenade destroys itself in the same BEGIN that damages its victims. Its CALLBACK Destroy meets an object already destroyed and leaves it (TD2: an object whose flags hold 0x1 is left as it is). | Original (installed data, `DAT_ROOT`; no bytes kept) |
| DS4 | runs of a destroyed object (0x459740, called by 0x43CCA0) | Given the object handle (a null one, a network client or a cleared run table return). Every loop over the 512 run slots skips the slot equal to the guard 0x5B0BA4, the run the rules are processing (set at 0x45116F, -1 otherwise): the Destroy component runs inside that run's BEGIN, so the run that destroyed the object is left alone. First, for a summoned object (spell flag 0x4000) the active runs that list it (the summon list at run +0x134) end by event (0x4571E0) and, unless the host is the editor (0x410060), a container's items go out (0x463860 with 1). Then each run in slot order, with no active-bit test: a run whose parent (+0x58) is the object ends by event (0x4571E0); else a run whose source (+0x10) is the object has its source cleared (0x457530). Then each active run (+0x13C bit 0x1) whose target object (+0xA0; this row first called it the other object, corrected by [DP3](#dispel-component-arc-magic-034)) is the object ends by event. So the grenade's own run, which destroys it, is skipped and ends in its own course (its interrupt runs the CALLBACK Destroy, which meets a destroyed object and leaves it, TD2); any other run with the grenade as parent ends by event. | Original (the summon branch is MT17's, not modelled) |

**F0 facts raised:** the Destroy component 0x451AF0 and the run endings 0x459740 at F1, implemented in part (registry rows: an item lying on the
ground only; the destroy script of point 2 stays the host's). The Summon component of both grenade spells (type 18, 0x450C40), the Dispel,
Movement and other components stay not ready (MT16, MT17).

## Spell run endings of a critter (ARC-DEV-301)

Source: the entangle report of 2026-11-08 ([triage](../open-bugs.md), T1). Read on 2026-11-08 in the 1.0.7.4 executable
(`llvm-objdump`; SHA-256 of the file checked) by this task alone (F1): the object-gone run endings 0x459740 and the two functions after it
(0x4598D0, 0x459A20), the kill 0x45DA20, the fatigue setter 0x45D5A0 with the unconscious test 0x45D800, and every call site of 0x459740, 0x4598D0,
0x457450 and 0x4574D0 in `.text`. Nothing was run, patched or copied; every direct branch stays inside `.text`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| UE1 | call sites of the object-gone endings (0x459740) | Five callers: the kill 0x45DA20 (at 0x45DA96), three sites inside the object destroy 0x43CCA0 (0x43CE11, 0x43D033, 0x43D0AE) and one at 0x4B5B38 (inside the record heal applier 0x4B58C0 (named in the function census): a branch that clears a flag of a critter, writes its fatigue damage 10 through the setter when it differs and then ends its runs; its trigger is not read). The summon variant 0x4598D0 has one caller, 0x4A3A63. The caster's endings 0x457450 are called by the fatigue setter 0x45D5A0 (0x45D63C), the inventory removal 0x467860 (0x467A14), the AI gate 0x4A8570 (0x4A88AF) and two sites in the interface code (0x565161 and 0x57B7D3, read in UE6: the world-map travel start check and the rest option click). | Original |
| UE2 | kill (0x45DA20) | A null object, or a kill whose script call (0x441980 with the value 12) answers 0, or an object whose field 0x13 has bit 0x1, returns at once. Otherwise it writes the death time (field 0xF4), calls 0x459740 with the dying object, then the rest of the kill. The ending of the runs is therefore part of every death that goes through, the PC's too, and the hero's death ends the hero's runs. | Original |
| UE3 | fatigue setter (0x45D5A0) | For a critter (type 15 or 16) only: it reads whether the critter is unconscious now (0x45D800: a critter whose fatigue damage reaches its maximum fatigue; 1 for an object that is not a critter, 0 for a critter flagged 0x4), writes the clamped fatigue damage (field 0xE2) and refreshes the bars (0x460260). When the new value is not 0, a test of the object (0x405BC0: a word of its record equals -1) answers no and the critter was not unconscious and is now, it demaintains the critter (0x457450: every run it is the source of, other than a technology run or a run with item triggers and no maintenance period, ends by event), fires its going unconscious (0x4CBBF0), knocks it down (0x435A90) and ends its turn (0x4B7C90). A knock-out, a spell payment and the encumbrance drain all reach it this way; no other call of the damage path demaintains a critter that falls. | Original |
| UE4 | summon variant (0x4598D0) and 0x459A20 | 0x4598D0 is a shorter 0x459740 for a summoned object (spell flag 0x4000): it empties a summoned container (0x463730 with 1), then ends the runs that list the object as a summon, the runs whose parent is the object and the active runs whose other object is the object, each through 0x457270 (not 0x4571E0); it does nothing when the global 0x5E7618 is 0. 0x459A20 is a bare `ret`. Both are the summon's (MT17). | Original |
| UE5 | the AI gate's demaintain (0x4A8570, 0x4A88AF) | When the NPC's DEMAINTAIN_SPELLS bit (0x1000000 of field 0x118) is set and the gate's leader test passes (no leader, a dead leader, or a leader not in combat mode), the gate clears the bit and calls 0x457450 with the NPC as the object. | Original |
| UE6 | travel start check (0x565140, called at 0x565161) and rest option click (0x57B710, called at 0x57B7D3) | 0x565140 (ARC-RES-120's "travel start check") asks the hero's object for the interrupt of all its runs at level 3 (0x424070 with the hero, 3, 0, 1: the table's rule 0x44E2C0 per run; the first refusal stops the sweep over the hero's runs and fails it) and answers 0 without further effect when it fails; when it passes it ends every run the hero is the source of (0x457450, the same filter as UE3 and UE5) and answers 1. Its two callers are entering the travel state (0x5615D0 with state 2: a 0 answer leaves the state unchanged, so no journey starts) and the travel step (0x564C20) at the end of a segment that another follows (a 0 answer drops every waypoint and the PC lands). The rest option click (0x57B710, one of 7 or 8 options) removes the pending rest events (0x45BD70, kind 15), takes the option's length from a table, starts the rest overlay and the followers' sleeping event, and then calls 0x457450 with the object the rest window holds (globals 0x6834A8 and 0x6834AC): resting ends the sleeper's maintained spells once the option is chosen, with no interrupt of its actions. | Original |

**F0 facts raised:** the kill's call and the fatigue setter's demaintain at F1, implemented (the cast host ends the runs through the session's actions);
left at F0 or not modelled: the routine around 0x4B5B38, the callers of 0x4598D0, the summon list of the runs (MT17), the inventory removal's call 0x467A14 (implemented before, ARC-MAGIC-022), the two interface sites 0x565161 and 0x57B7D3 (UE6: travel done, ARC-MAGIC-059; rest done, ARC-MAGIC-1161), the setter's conditions beyond the conscious-to-unconscious transition.

## Centre of a radius cast (ARC-DEV-327)

Source: the [spell cast host](../../world/spell-cast-host.md) and B12's open half ([the ARC-DEV-250 re-check](../open-bugs.md):
the area of a radius spell was gathered around the click's tile, and whether the original centres on the target's cell at the hit was not read).
Read on 2026-11-10 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`llvm-objdump`, SHA-256 `9bd80511…3d52` checked) by this task alone (F1): the target collection 0x4F40B0
(the object-radius branch, 0x4F41FC–0x4F4415), the context initialiser 0x4F2600, the rectangle collector 0x440B40 and the picker's wrapper 0x4F2680
(with its tile and object result writers 0x4F27F0 and 0x4F2810). Nothing was run, patched or copied; every direct branch stays inside `.text`.
They confirm TA10/TA11 and SU17 and join them into one answer for the question.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RC1 | object-radius branch of the collection (0x4F40B0, low mask 0x10) | The centre is read when the collection runs, which is the run's BEGIN processing (the casting goal's release, or the missile's arrival), not when the cast was clicked: a target object's location through the field getter 0x406DA0 (field 2), else the run's target tile (the registers still hold it from the sub-context's set-up). The square is `centre − radius … centre + radius` in both axes, built from the 32-bit centre (zero-extended) and the radius (sign-extended) in 64 bits, and the rectangle collector 0x440B40 lists the objects in it. A sub-context is made with the source as origin, the target tile and other object kept and the tile bit (0x8000) of the mask cleared. | Original |
| RC2 | the same, candidates | Each listed object (a scenery object with an occupant stands for the occupant, 0x4F2C60) goes through the area test (0x4F2D20) with the sub-context, and the count drops on every pass (TA11). With a target object there is no target tile in the sub-context, so the test's radius check (TA8) measures from the **source**: a candidate must stand within the radius of the caster as well as inside the square around the target. With a target tile (a tile cast) it measures from that tile. | Original |
| RC3 | picker's wrapper (0x4F2680) | For an object under the pointer the area test runs with the cursor's mask, the object as candidate and target and no tiles: with a radius name (0x10) its radius check is measured from the caster. An object farther from the caster than the radius fails and is tested again with its own location as the candidate tile and the target tile (the object kept as candidate and target); that passes (distance 0), and the result is that **tile**, not the object (SU17). An object within the radius of the caster passes the first test and stays the object. | Original |
| RC4 | consequence for a cast at a body | From afar (more than the radius from the caster) the cast is a tile cast: the centre is the body's tile at the click, whatever the body does until the missile lands; the animals that stand within the radius of that tile at the hit are hit, and the effect (an eye-candy component) is added on each of them, drawn at its cell at the hit. Within the radius of the caster the cast stays an object cast: the centre follows the body to where it stands at the hit. The original has no other rule: a runner that left the radius of the click's tile is not hit, and one that came in is. | Original |
| RC5 | Steamwoven | Both cases follow the original already: the cursor's pick (`SpellUiRules.PickTest`) makes the tile cast, and the cast host builds the gather's context from the run's target object **as it stands now** (`SessionSpellCaster.Context`: the map's object, its live cell) else the run's tile, and measures a candidate's radius as TA8 says (`SpellTargetRules.Test`). Pinned by `Dev327TheRadiusGatherCentresOnTheClickedTileFromAfarAndOnTheObjectNowNearTheCaster`, and observed by the stage `cast-multi` (the cast log shows `target=tile`, the animals within two cells of that tile at the hit are hit). No game source changed. | Steamwoven follows the original |

**F0 facts raised:** none. With neither a target object nor a target tile the object branch's centre is the zero location (the registers hold the tile fields); an installed radius spell always has one of the two.

## Summon component and the objects it creates (ARC-RES-101)

Source: the Summon research note (ARC-RES-101, deleted; rows SUM1-SUM15 below) (slice E1-r of the [missing-systems plan](../../project/missing-systems-plan.md): what the
flash of Fireflash, the five pieces of Wall of Fire and the second line of Summon Undead are, B47 of the
[2026-11-10 triage](../open-bugs.md)). Read on 2026-11-10 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`Version.txt` `VERSION:1.0.7.4`;
`llvm-objdump`; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52` checked) by this task alone (F1): the component's ProcFunc 0x452830 with the list pick 0x452900, the
summon effect 0x450C40, its helpers 0x450C10, 0x4372B0 (with the run iterator 0x44D2F0/0x44D340), 0x4554B0, the run slot's free
(0x455920 to 0x4559D5), the Dispel executor 0x451C40, the EyeCandy component 0x451FE0 with the play 0x4CCF70, the tile branches of the area
test 0x4F2D20 (0x4F3C80 to 0x4F3E48) and the tile-blocking test 0x4D7110. The installed data (`DAT_ROOT`) were read by an opt-in scratch run
of the spell reader, the eye-candy table, the prototype file names and fields and the ART headers (counts and numbers only; nothing kept
in git, no art, no text). Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied; every direct branch stays inside `.text`.
Earlier rows reused, not re-read: MG8, MG9, SC7, SC12, TA1, TA9, TA10, TA12, TA15, RP2, RP4, RP12, EC9 to EC14, DS2 to DS4, UE1, UE4, OM15 to OM17, OM25, GT75 to GT77, GT94 to GT98.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SUM1 | ProcFunc of component type 18 (0x452830), request block | Builds a 0xD0-byte request from the run (global 0x5E75F0) and the component (global 0x5E761C) and calls the summon effect 0x450C40 once for every list entry whose component area test passed: summoner (+0x00) = the run's parent (+0x58); target object (+0x30) = the run's target (+0xA0); location (+0x78) = the entry's tile (globals 0x5E6D50 and 0x5E6D54, which the per-target stage 0x4510F0 sets from each list entry, TA15); result pointer (+0x80) = the global 0x5E75B8 (a scratch cell other components reuse for the run's source, parent and target, so it is not "the last summoned"); palette (+0xC0) = component +0x60; clear-faction flag (+0xC8) = component +0x58; prototype ID (+0x60) = the component's own 24-byte ID (+0x40) when its first word is not 0, else the list pick (SUM6) for the component's list number (+0x5C). Afterwards the new handle is copied to 0x5E6D70 and 0x5E6D74, the context's "summoned" slot (TA1 +0x48), which the re-aim of `Apply_AoE: Tgt_Summoned` (0x453D40, at 0x453E12) reads, and added to the run's summoned list (SUM7). One call makes one object: a component that passes for five entries makes five. The summoned slot is written only here and cleared only by the run context 0x453630 (0x4536BB), which runs once per action, not per list entry: the re-aim of a later component meets the object of the latest entry whose Summon ran, and an entry whose Summon was refused (its area test failed) re-aims to the **previous entry's** object, or to none when it is the first of the action. A line with no component area of its own (Wall of Fire's and Summon Undead's eye-candy lines) runs for every entry; Fireflash's is safe because its re-aimed line carries `Tgt_Tile_Empty` too. | Original (F1) |
| SUM2 | summon effect, creation (0x450C40; creation 0x43CBA0 → 0x442130, OM15) | Single player (0x62AC6C bit 0 clear): the object is created at once from the prototype ID at the location. A failed creation logs and calls the C runtime's exit with 1 (0x58AA7C), so the process ends; no installed record can reach it (every installed `Proto:` has its file, SC7, SC12). A network host creates, sends a request packet (type 0x49) and, for a scenery, also plays 0x424070 (5, 0, 0) and restarts the scenery's animation (0x43F710); a client creates the object only with an ID the request carries (0x43CBF0 with the 24 bytes at request +0x88) and otherwise logs and returns. A creation that returns no object ends the function: nothing is stored and none of the steps below runs. The network paths are E10's, not read further. | Original (F1); the exit on failure is unreachable |
| SUM3 | summon effect, the steps every created object gets | The new handle is stored through the request's result pointer (0x5E75B8); the spell flag **0x4000** (`OSF_SUMMONED`, field 20) is OR-ed in (0x450C10); the type branch (SUM4, SUM5) follows; and **last**, for every type, the whole-inventory mark 0x463630 runs on it (RK13: a container with flag 0x200 first generates its contents, 0x463E20; every item gets spell flag 0x4000 and item flag 0x20; an object without an inventory, a scenery, gets nothing). The creation itself marks the object dynamic (OM16). | Original (F1) |
| SUM4 | summon effect, any type but an NPC (a scenery, a container, a PC, an item: no installed record makes anything but a scenery or an NPC) | For types without an inventory the only further step (before the common 0x463630, SUM3) is 0x4372B0: for each goal run the new object already owns (the iterator 0x44D2F0 / 0x44D340, GT4) it writes the **summoner's handle** into the run's frame slot 4 (run +0x60 and +0x64, "the source object", GT70). A scenery's main run (goal 1, or goal 77 for scenery flag `OSCF_IS_FIRE`) is started when the object enters the object list (GT77, GT98), so it exists by then and has the summoner as its source. No art or facing change, no palette, no `OF_DONTDRAW`, no critter field is touched. The effect on the burn (GT94): a piece's fire damage skips the summoner and the NPCs whose leader chain reaches a PC summoner, adds the summoner's stat-16 bonus and gives it the blame; with no source (a scenery placed by a map) none of that holds. Corrects OM25's "a PC caster is written into the spell runs (0x4372B0, inference)": the write goes into the **new object's goal runs**, for a PC or NPC summoner alike. The loop around the write never advances its address, so whatever the run's stack depth it writes only that bottom frame's slot 4. The source therefore lives **only in the run's bottom frame**, and the starter 0x433580 builds goal 1 and 77 with slot 4 empty (GT76, GT95): **any restart of the main run drops the source** (after the GT98 exhaustion sweep, a sector reload or fold, an art set), and from then on a fire piece burns its own caster too. The run exists at summon time only when the object-list insert's test (0x43D940 at 0x4F2073) passes and the object's sector is loaded (OM16). | Original (F1); OM25's inference was a reconstruction slip |
| SUM5 | summon effect, an NPC (type 16; the critter half, kept for the E1-e hand-off) | In this order: the art (field 1) is rebuilt with its rotation turned toward the summoner (0x4B8D50 of the two locations, 0x502930) and the component's palette (0x502DB0) and written back (0x43EB30); 0x45EE30 (1) runs (not identified); `OF_DONTDRAW` (0x100) is set through the flag setter 0x43D0E0; bit 0x80 of the critter flags word 2 (field 219, `OCF2_NO_PICKPOCKET`) is set; fields 285 and 286 (OM25: experience worth and pool) are cleared. Faction: with the clear-faction flag set, field 292 (the NPC block's faction slot, by range) is cleared and nothing else happens; without it (not a network client) the **parent's alignment** (stat 19, 0x4B0490 and 0x4B0980) is copied to the summoned when the run has a parent, and field 292 is copied from the summoner when the summoner is an NPC (a PC summoner leaves the prototype's faction). The summoned is then added to the run's list (SUM1) and its `Tgt_Summoned` lines (the records' ObjFlag and EyeCandy lines) follow. MG8 stands (the flag comes from `Clear_Faction:`, not from `List:`). | Original (F1); the NPC half is E1-e's |
| SUM6 | list pick (0x452900; tables 0x5B0ED8 + 12 × n: count, entries, flag) | Used only when the component names no prototype. The flag word picks the rule: **0** weighted, a d100 draw over the weights (MG9: weights sum to 100 or 120, a draw of 100 falls off the end to entry 0 with a log); **odd** by aptitude: the first entry whose threshold is not below the run's captured parent aptitude (+0x8C, RP4 and DH2; 100 under a PC's armed "spell at maximum" fate), the last threshold being 1000 so one is always found; **even and not 0** entry 0. The chosen basic number goes through the +20 rule of SC7. Installed lists (entries are weight or threshold, then the basic prototype number): list 0 weights 20, 1, 19, 20, 20, 20; list 1 six weights of 20; list 2 weights 99, 1; list 3 thresholds 25, 50, 75, 1000 (aptitude); list 4 thresholds 20, 40, 55, 70, 95, 1000 (aptitude); list 5 thresholds 20, 60, 85, 1000 (aptitude). Every entry of every list is an NPC prototype (the group of its installed prototype file). Installed records use list 0 (spell 183), 3 (74), 4 (53) and 5 (57); lists 1 and 2 are named by no installed record. | Original (F1; MG9 stands) |
| SUM7 | the run's summoned list (0x4554B0; the run slot's free 0x455920 to 0x4559D5) | A new node (at least 0x44 bytes, allocator 0x4BAC30) is put at the head of the list the run holds at +0x134: the handle (+0), an object reference built by 0x443EB0 (+8: the 24-byte permanent ID, the location at +0x18 only when 0x43D990 holds, else 0, and a map id at +0x20; so the original keeps the summoned list by permanent ID), its type (+0x38) and, for a PC or NPC only, one stat value (stat 16, read through 0x4B0490, at +0x34; the reader of that value was not found); the next link is +0x40. The run's other list (+0x130) is separate. The slot's free releases the nodes of both lists and nothing else: **the end of a run destroys nothing it summoned by itself.** The readers found: the `Tgt_Summoned` gather (TA10), the object-gone endings (DS4, UE4) and Dispel (SUM8). | Original (F1) |
| SUM8 | what removes a summoned object | (1) A Destroy component aimed at `Tgt_Summoned`, which the installed records put in their CALLBACK or END_CALLBACK line (DS2: the object destroy 0x43CCA0). (2) Dispel (0x451C40) first ends, by event (0x4571E0), every run other than the one being processed (a slot-id test for the source loop, a slot-index test for the second) whose record is not technological (record flag 0x4) and whose source (+0x10; every slot, no active test) or, among active runs (bit 0x1 at +0x13C), whose target (+0xA0), summoned list (+0x134) or run list (+0x130) holds the target; then, only for a PC or NPC target, it clears `OSF_STONED` and **destroys the target when its spell flags hold 0x4000** and undoes a perma-polymorph; for a portal or container it clears the "magically held" bit. So a Dispel on a summoned **scenery** object ends the run that made it and so that run's END path (SUM12 to SUM14), but destroys nothing itself. (3) The runs' object-gone endings (DS4, UE4) end runs, not objects. (4) The run's end (SUM7): nothing. (5) Map clear (OM19). | Original (F1) |
| SUM9 | tile tests of the Summon areas (area test 0x4F2D20, tile branches 0x4F3C80 to 0x4F3E48; terrain test `tile_is_blocking` 0x4D7110) | Confirms TA9 and spells it out. `Tgt_Tile_Empty` (high word 0x40000): the tile must pass the terrain test (not blocking; area-passability F7), and every object listed on it (mask 0x3FFEF: every type but projectile) of a type other than 5 to 14 (so a wall, portal, container, scenery, PC, NPC or trap; items never count) must carry `OF_NO_BLOCK` (0x400); one object without it makes the tile **not empty**, a standing critter included. `Tgt_Tile_Empty_Immobiles` (0x80000): the same terrain test, then only objects of types 0, 1, 3 and 17 (mask 0x2000B: wall, portal, scenery, trap) count, again unless `OF_NO_BLOCK`. A list entry that is an object has no tile, so a component area with a tile bit refuses it (TA9). Steamwoven's `SpellTargetRules.Empty` already holds both masks. | Original (F1; agrees with TA9) |
| SUM10 | census of the installed spell data (SC12's reader, like-original mode; counts, spell numbers and numbers only) | 42 Summon components, one in each of 42 records, all in the BEGIN action: **25 make a scenery** (seven prototypes, SUM11), **17 make a critter** (13 name an NPC prototype directly: spells 14, 19, 24, 29 the four elementals, 68, 71, 72, 73, 123 to 126, 135; 4 pick from a list: 53 list 4, 57 list 5, 74 list 3, 183 list 0). **None makes an item, an eye-candy object, a container or any other type.** Scenery by prototype (basic number; file number is +20): 4029 spells 11 (component 2) and 200 (1); 4030 spell 32 (0); 4031 spells 21 (0) and 180 (0); 4032 spell 17 (0); 4033 spells 26 (0) and 178 (0); 4034 spell 27 (1); 4050 sixteen spells: 22 (3), 148 (3), 166 (2), 175 (5), 176 (5), 177 (3), 179 (3), 185 (4), 198 (3), 199 (4), 201 (5), 202 (3), 205 (4), 217 (1), 219 (5), 220 (5). Records 148 to 220 lie in the technology range (the thrown grenades and gases of DS3 and IC, and a few scripted effects). | Data (opt-in, `DAT_ROOT`) |
| SUM11 | the seven scenery prototypes (installed prototype files 4049, 4050, 4051, 4052, 4053, 4054, 4070) | All are type 3 (scenery) with 100 hit points and scale 100. Object flags: all seven hold `OF_SEE_THROUGH`, `OF_SHOOT_THROUGH`, `OF_CLICK_THROUGH` and `OF_PROVIDES_COVER`; all but file 4052 hold `OF_INVULNERABLE`; all but files 4050 and 4052 hold **`OF_NO_BLOCK`**. So files 4049, 4051, 4053, 4054, 4070 (cloud, fire, fog and smoke, ice, flash) let everything pass; 4050 (Wall of Force) blocks and is invulnerable; 4052 (Wall of Stone) blocks and is **not** invulnerable (it can be broken). None has `OF_DYNAMIC`, `OF_DONTDRAW` or a spell flag; the scenery flags are 0 except file 4051 (Wall of Fire's piece), which holds **`OSCF_IS_FIRE`** (0x10), the bit that starts the scenery's main run as goal 77 (GT77). Art (type 4, scenery category 15, frame 0, group 0, palette 0): files 4049, 4053, 4054, 4070 share number 11, which is a **1 × 1 pixel single frame** (so the cloud, fog, ice and flash scenery draw nothing of their own: their look is the spell's eye-candy overlays); 4052 number 100 (49 × 125, one static frame); 4051 number 101 (75 × 130, 16 frames at 15 per second, action frame 15); 4050 number 102 (60 × 167, 15 frames at 15 per second, action frame 14). Files 4050 and 4051 have blit flag 0x10. No sound, light or script on any of them. | Data (opt-in) |
| SUM12 | Fireflash (spell 22; Summon is component 3) | BEGIN list (record area): the target tile and the objects within radius 2 other than the caster, walls and the party's critters. Components in order: the Damage, the EyeCandy 2 and 3 on the objects in the radius (the critters), then the **Summon of basic prototype 4050 (file 4070) with area `Tgt_Tile_Empty`**: it runs for the tile entry only (an object entry has no tile, SUM9), so **the flash exists only when the strike tile is empty at the landing**: a critter that still stands on it, or terrain that blocks, gives no flash (RC4: a cast at a body from afar is a tile cast at the body's tile at the click). Then EyeCandy entry 2 `Add`, `Play: Callback`, re-aimed to the new scenery (entry 6 × 22 + 2: translucent, animating, not looping, with sound and light; its art has 7 frames at 8 per second, action frame 6). The `Callback` line is `Destroy` on `Tgt_Summoned`. The scenery's life is therefore that animation up to its callback frame (EC14: the callback goals fire at the action frame; 6 steps of 125 ms, about 0.75 s from the start, derived from the ART header, not observed); then DS2 destroys it. No maintenance and no duration: the run holds only until that callback. The scenery has no flag that damages (scenery flags 0, main run goal 1): the flash only **shows**; the fire damage is the record's own Damage component. | Original (F1; the art numbers are data) |
| SUM13 | Wall of Fire (spell 21; one Summon, component 0) | There is **one** Summon component, not five: the five reports are one component applied to five list entries. The BEGIN area is `Tgt_Tile` plus `Tgt_Tile_Radius_Wall` with radius 2 (TA12: from the direction between the origin and the target tile, the centre and two tiles each way, five distinct tiles); the component's own area is `Tgt_Tile_Empty_Immobiles` (SUM9), so a tile with terrain blocking or a blocking wall, portal, scenery or trap gets no piece, while critters do not stop one. Each piece is the same prototype (basic 4031, file 4051), made at its own tile with no offset and no rotation (SUM4); the pieces differ only by tile. Its look is its own art (SUM11): the **spell has no eye-candy entries 2 and 3 in the installed table**, so the EyeCandy lines add nothing. The wall hurts: the piece's main run is goal 77 (`OSCF_IS_FIRE`) with the **caster as its source** (SUM4): fire damage to the critters and items on the piece's own tile: 3 fire at each burn step (the begin, each step at frame mod 3 = 1) outside combat, and in a turn-based round 6 fire once per round (the run's round gate, GT76, GT94); the caster and the NPCs whose leader chain reaches a PC caster are spared (GT94). Life: no timer of its own; the record maintains (cost 1, period 10, no duration, RN), so the pieces stand while the caster maintains and until the run ends by Dispel, de-maintaining (UE3, UE5), the caster's death (UE2) or an unpaid MAINTAIN (RP2). At the END: the Remove lines find nothing, the END line (EyeCandy 2 `Add`, `Reverse`, `End_Callback`) is **not played** (the entry has no art and no translucent or tint table flag, so 0x4CCF70 answers "not played" and the END callback runs at once, EC11; an art-less entry that has one of those flags calls 0x43EF10 (0x40) and answers "played"), and the END_CALLBACK `Destroy` on `Tgt_Summoned` destroys all pieces together: they vanish at once, with no fade. The `Callback` line (`OF_DONTDRAW` off) is never fired: nothing plays a callback in this record, and the summon sets no `OF_DONTDRAW` on a scenery. | Original (F1) |
| SUM14 | the other scenery Summon records (11, 17, 26, 27, 32; the technology records 148 to 220) | The same shape as SUM13. Walls of Stone (17) and Force (32): `Tgt_Tile_Empty` at the tile list and in the BEGIN area, so blocked tiles drop out of the list; the pieces block movement (SUM11), have no eye candy (entries 2 and 3 absent), maintenance 1/10 and 2/10, END as SUM13. Call Fog (26), Poison Vapours (11), Squall of Ice (27), Mustard Gas (200) and the technology smoke and fire (178, 180): `Tgt_Tile_Radius`, radius 1, a 3 × 3 square of up to nine pieces (Call Fog's piece also needs an empty tile). Spells 11, 26, 27, 178 and 200 have eye-candy entries 2 and 3 (entry 3 loops) added on every piece (on a tile whose Summon is refused they land on the previous piece, SUM1) and, at END, entries 2 and 3 removed and entry 2 played in reverse with an END callback before the END_CALLBACK destroy: those pieces fade out. Poison Vapours, Squall of Ice and Mustard Gas also have damage components in MAINTAIN. Spell 180 has no eye-candy entries: its piece is the burning scenery of SUM13 (file 4051), and its END plays nothing, so the pieces vanish at once. Every dumped flash record of the technology range (148, 166, 175 to 177, 179, 185, 198, 199, 201, 205, 219, 220) summons the flash at the target tile with the same lines as SUM12 (entry 2 with `Callback`, `Callback` Destroy on `Tgt_Summoned`); those with item triggers destroy their parent, the thrown item, in the same BEGIN (DS3). The exception is Tech: Explode (Paralysis) (202): its flash has no callback and a duration of 6 seconds, and an END `Destroy` on `Tgt_Summoned` removes it, so it stays for the run's duration. The ObjFlag line `OF_DONTDRAW` off appears in every cloud and wall record and is inert for a scenery (nothing sets the flag). Animal Lure (183) makes a creature from list 0 at an empty or offscreen tile and clears `OF_DONTDRAW` at once; Phantom Knight Death (217) first sets `OF_DONTDRAW` on the target critter, then makes the flash at the target tile with a `Callback` destroy. | Original (F1) |
| SUM15 | Summon Undead (57), Succour Beast (53), Familiar (74) | Spell 57: component 0 is the Summon (area `Tgt_Tile_Empty`, **list 5**, aptitude thresholds, SUM6, creates an NPC with SUM5 and `OF_DONTDRAW` set); **component 1** is an ObjFlag line aimed at `Tgt_Summoned` that turns the spell flag **`OSF_MIND_CONTROLLED`** (bit 27, 0x8000000) **on, on the summoned creature** (its own field 20), recorded also in the record's spell-flag set (the record's SpellFlagsSet holds the same bit); there is no line that turns it off, because the END destroys the creature. Component 2 plays EyeCandy 2 on the summoned with `Callback` (its art: 19 frames at 18 per second, action frame 12, about 0.67 s), and the `Callback` line clears `OF_DONTDRAW` on `Tgt_Summoned` (the END, CALLBACK and END_CALLBACK areas of the three records are all `Tgt_Summoned`): the creature appears when the summoning animation reaches its callback frame. At END the same entry plays in reverse with an END callback, then the `Destroy` removes the creature. Succour Beast (53) is the same pattern with list 4; Familiar (74) uses list 3, sets `OSF_FAMILIAR` on the caster and `ONF_FAMILIAR` (NPC flags) on the summoned too, and has no END lines (no maintenance, no destroy: the familiar stays). Spell 56 (Conjure Spirit) has no Summon (ARC-DEV-304). Who tests `OSF_MIND_CONTROLLED` in the AI was not read; the rows that name it: the dialog entry (script 2188 in place of the NPC's own), the reaction change, the companion wait timer, the dialog code `PA` (script-trigger-audit, scr-event-dispatcher, npc-reaction-reputation, game-event-queue). | Original (F1; the flag's AI readers are E1-e's) |
| SUM16 | anomaly classification (BUG RULE) | (a) OM25's inference about 0x4372B0: a **reconstruction slip** (SUM4); both modes follow the corrected row. (b) The Fireflash flash needs an empty tile (SUM9, SUM12): the **original's own rule and data agree**, no bug of the executable; whether the owner's cast of B47 met an empty tile is not known from the log (the component was applied, so Steamwoven's own area test passed it); a flash on a critter's tile would need a data change, not a Steamwoven rule, and is **not** proposed. (c) A failed summon creation ends the process (SUM2): an **original bug**, unreachable with installed data; Steamwoven keeps a typed refusal (project style, no switch). (d) The inert `OF_DONTDRAW` lines of the cloud and wall records (SUM14): original data, harmless. (e) An EyeCandy entry with no art and no translucent or tint flag is "not played" and its callback runs at once (SUM13): the original's rule (EC11), kept. | Original; (a) slip, (c) bug |
| SUM17 | summon list table (data at 0x5B0ED8, six rows of count, entries pointer, flag; read from the executable's initialised data, ARC-MAGIC-053) | List 0 (weighted, flag 0): weights 20, 1, 19, 20, 20, 20 for basic prototypes 28319, 28360, 28329, 28330, 28332, 28334. List 1 (weighted): six weights of 20 for 28324, 28333, 28331, 28333, 28335, 28336 (the sum is 120). List 2 (weighted): 99 and 1 for 28343, 28360. List 3 (aptitude, flag 1): thresholds 25, 50, 75, 1000 for 27371, 27372, 27373, 27374 (the familiars). List 4 (aptitude): thresholds 20, 40, 55, 70, 95, 1000 for 28319, 28332, 28334, 28330, 28329, 28360. List 5 (aptitude): thresholds 20, 60, 85, 1000 for 28468, 28469, 28470, 28471 (Summon Undead's). Entries are basic prototype numbers (the file is the number + 20, SC7). | Data (executable, F1) |
| SUM18 | pick function 0x452900 re-read | A flag word 0 draws once, 0..100 inclusive (0x4CEF00), and when the draw is above 0 walks the entries: an entry whose weight is above the draw is the pick, else the weight is taken off and the walk goes on; running off the end logs and takes entry 0. An odd flag compares the run's captured parent aptitude (+0x8C) with each threshold: the first entry whose threshold is **not below** the aptitude is picked (the loop's own end is the last entry); no draw. An even non-zero flag takes entry 0 (no installed list). The pick runs once for every list entry the component's area passed (SUM1), so each piece or creature of one component draws on its own. | Original (F1) |
| SUM19 | 0x45EE30 (1) and 0x45EE90, called by the summon effect for an NPC after the art rebuild and before OF_DONTDRAW is set (identified by ARC-MAGIC-053; the executable's decompiled export read, the art helpers named by their use) | 0x45EE30 applies 0x45EE90 to the object and to each object of its inventory list (0x441260). In single player (0x4A2BA0: bit 0 of 0x62AC6C clear, or the host) 0x45EE90 with the flag 1 builds the art with its animation number 5, the sneak pose, at that animation's last frame (0x45EFA0 through 0x503E50, 0x5030B0, 0x502C40) and, **only when that art differs from the critter's current one**, sets the critter flags bits 0x3 (field 0xDA: concealed and moving silently); it then writes the sneak art when the object is not destroyed or off, not dead or unconscious, not paralysed or stunned (critter flags 0x60) and not stoned (0x45D790). So **a summoned creature whose art has a sneak pose starts concealed** (the AI's concealed animation 5 and the awareness rules' prowling margin) until something unconceals it. The call 0x424070 (5, 0, 0) at its start is not identified. | Original (F1); **not built** in ARC-MAGIC-053 (named limit: the created critter's art path draws the stand pose only) |
| SUM20 | summon effect for an NPC, ARC-MAGIC-053's build | The critter is `oid:critter:n` (E1-c), created at the entry's tile by `CreateSummonedCritter` (not near it): spell flags 0x4000 on the created record and written in its flags, `OF_DONTDRAW` (object flags 0x100) and `OCF2_NO_PICKPOCKET` (critter flags 2, 0x80) written as the effect does, the summoner and the run kept on the record. Its row (experience worth and pool cleared, SUM5; the hero's alignment copied into stat 19 when the hero summoned) is built by `CreatedObjectRows` when the world first reads it. The summoned list is the created objects naming the run (SUM7), so `Tgt_Summoned` reaches a creature as it reaches a scenery. The follow-up lines of the records do the rest: ObjFlag `OSF_MIND_CONTROLLED` (the forced join, no flee: MA-4), the EyeCandy entry with the callback, the CALLBACK line that clears `OF_DONTDRAW`, the END callback and its Destroy. | Original (F1); Steamwoven build |
| SUM21 | 0x459740's first loop | For an object that carries the summoned spell flag the first loop ends (0x4571E0, by event) every active run but the processed one whose summoned list (+0x134) holds it. So the death of a summoned creature (the kill, 0x45DA20 after its dying script allowed it) ends the run that made it, and the run's END reaches its END_CALLBACK Destroy; the creature's corpse is destroyed then. `SpellRunSystem.ObjectGone` runs the loop first (ARC-MAGIC-053). | Original (F1); Steamwoven build |
| SUM22 | MG9 applied (owner rule on technical bugs, 2026-10-28) | The overrun of a weighted list (a draw of 100 over a total of 100 takes entry 0; a total of 120 can never reach its last entries in full) is a technical bug: **fixed in both modes without a switch**: one draw over 0..total−1 and the entry whose cumulative weight passes it. List 0 (spell 183's) is the only installed weighted list; its entries keep their intended shares (20, 1, 19, 20, 20, 20 per hundred). | Owner rule (applied) |

**F0 facts raised:** none implemented on them yet. Left at F0 or not read: who reads the summoned list's stat value (SUM7), 0x424070 (SUM2; 0x45EE30 is identified by SUM19), the loop shape of 0x4372B0 for a deeper stack (SUM4), the AI's readers of `OSF_MIND_CONTROLLED` (SUM15), the network packet's fields (SUM2), the run slot's save record (PS11) beyond its two lists, and every value that only a running game shows (how often an empty tile is met, the actual animation timings; F4).

## Magic audit executable reads (ARC-DEV-357)

Source: item MA-R of the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#slices) (ARC-RES-103): what the Identify component shows and writes, what Tempus Fugit
does, who reads the illusion, familiar and detecting-traps spell flags, what "magically held" does to a door, whether the speed stat reaches walking and
action points, and the weapon drop of Disarm. Read on 2026-11-11 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`Version.txt` `VERSION:1.0.7.4`; `llvm-objdump`; SHA-256
`9bd80511…8d52` checked) by this task and, independently, by a second reader that re-read every row in the executable (method step 6; F2, agreement on every row, the second reader added the nigh-invulnerable gate of XR15 and the plain write of XR5 for fields other than 0x14): the component table 0x5B0BA8 (slot 13 Identify 0x4522A0, slot 16 ObjFlag 0x452380), the Identify wrappers
0x4EE3A0, 0x4EE430, 0x4EE4C0, the interface callbacks 0x460300, 0x4604F0, 0x460930 with their install 0x45FD40 (called from the interface's init at
0x57CA74) and the three handlers 0x57CB80, 0x57CCF0, 0x57CF70; the ObjFlag side effects 0x454A10 with the reference count 0x455100; the stat getter
0x4B0490 and the local player's flag test 0x458A80; the follower join tail (0x45DF82 to 0x45DFB9), the follower removal 0x45E040 and its clear 0x459EA0; the
travel hide 0x45E1E0 and the summoned-associate test 0x45F460; the close-door node 0x428620, the door end nodes 0x42B640 and 0x42B6F0; Dispel's lock
branch (0x451EBF to 0x451F14); the Damage component 0x4518D0 and the damage step 0x4B4390 (head and weapon block); the speed readers 0x4B6F6B, 0x42EE90
and 0x576DB4, and the walk cost 0x42EDC0. Flag bits come from the executable's flag-name tables (`OSF_` from 0x5BA188, `ONF_` from 0x5BA44C, `OCF_` from
0x5BA348, `OIF_` from 0x5BA284) and agree with the project's constants (`OSF_SUMMONED` 0x4000, `OSF_MIND_CONTROLLED` 0x8000000, `OSF_DETECTING_ALIGNMENT`
0x80, `ONF_CAST_HIGHEST` 0x40000, `OCF_CRIPPLED_LEGS_BOTH` 0x400). The installed records (`DAT_ROOT`, `rules\spelllist.mes`) were read by an opt-in
scratch script for the record numbers, areas and flag names only. Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied; every direct branch of the
functions read stays inside `.text`, none reaches `.IIDKing`. Earlier rows reused, not re-read: RP5, DS2, DH1 to DH7, TA8, HK22, LW1 to LW3, CS2, CP1,
CHW24, IF12, CH4, RT8, CW3, CR13, CR14, LK2, LK7, LK8, GT39 to GT42, OH10, PL1, TT3, SUM15, WB7, QW6, IW20, IW26.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| XR1 | Identify component (0x4522A0) | Nothing without a current target (0x5E6D48). By the cached target type (0x5E75AC): a PC or NPC (15, 16) goes to 0x4EE4C0, a container (2) to 0x4EE430, any other type to 0x4EE3A0, each with the run's parent (+0x58) and the target. Each wrapper acts only when the parent is the local player (0x40D9F0); otherwise, with both network bits of 0x62AC6C set, a packet is sent (kinds 10, 8 and 7, 0x5295F0), and a single-player game does nothing. So an NPC caster's Identify has no effect, and the component **writes nothing**: it only opens a window or a description for the casting player. The positional word after `Type: Identify` (`IF_DISPLAY_ALL` in all three records) is never read (SC11). | Original |
| XR2 | critter: 0x4EE4C0 → 0x460930 → 0x57CF70 | The handler opens the character sheet (`charedit_open` 0x5597C0) on the **target** in mode 2: the read-only NPC sheet of the dialog's "show your abilities" reply (CS2, CP1, CHW24: no stat buttons, every hover region, all four tabs). Read Aura (7) has area `Tgt_Obj_No_Self` and `Tgt_Obj_T_Critter`, so it is never the caster's own sheet. | Original |
| XR3 | container: 0x4EE430 → 0x460300 → 0x57CB80 | The handler opens the inventory window (0x572240) on the container in **mode 4**, the mode whose caller [ID6](items.md#item-decay-arc-world-062) left unknown: its admission runs **no lock, jam, magically-held or use-script test** (LW1), so See Contents (6) shows a locked container's items; a container with flag 0x200 is generated first (LW2); the close counts as a loot and runs the junk-pile check (LW3); the decay pause holds while it is open (ID6). The window draws the `Loot` background and the other party's image as modes 2 and 3 (IW20, IW26) and groups mode 4 with modes 5 and 6 for its pane setup (0x572F35). What a press or a release may move in mode 4: nothing ([XR16](#magic-audit-executable-reads-arc-dev-357); QW6's "mode 4" is the interface cursor mode 4, the barter window's). | Original |
| XR4 | item and other types: 0x4EE3A0 → 0x4604F0 → 0x57CCF0 | The handler runs the item description of the hover window (HK22): 0x57CD60 (the examine script, point 0; the true description only when the item is identified, 0x464370, or the scripts are off), the draw 0x553BE0 and the action-point preview 0x557370. Divine Magick (9) has area `Tgt_Obj_Inven` and puts an ObjFlag line before its Identify: it sets `OIF_IDENTIFIED` (item flags bit 0x1) through 0x454A10, which has no side effect for the item-flag field (a plain write; the count of XR5 is kept for the spell flags only), so the description that follows is the identified one and the item's worth is its own (WB7). | Original |
| XR5 | ObjFlag side effects (0x454A10, set and clear branches) | For the spell flags (field 0x14) only these bits have side effects: the four detection bits 0x3C0 (XR11), invisible 0x1, shrunk 0x80000, water walking 0x200000, stoned 0x10000, entangled 0x1000000, mind controlled 0x8000000 and charmed 0x800000; on the critter flags 0xDA undead 0x4, fleeing 0x10, paralysed 0x40, blinded 0x80; on the object flags 0x13 don't-draw 0x100; on the portal flags 0x2E and container flags 0x38 the clear of locked 0x1 (the aptitude test 0x4551C0, the resist report 0x45A520, the guard alert 0x4AEE50). **Illusion (0x8000), Tempus Fugit (0x4000000), familiar (0x40000000), `ONF_FAMILIAR`, `OIF_IDENTIFIED` and magically held (0x4 of field 0x2E) have no case**: they get the plain write (0x406D40) after the reference count (0x455100: for the spell flags 0x14 a set skips while another run provides the bit and a clear keeps it while more than one does; for any other field it counts 0, so the write is plain). | Original |
| XR6 | Tempus Fugit (79), stat getter (0x4B0490), player test (0x458A80) | Record 79: area `Tgt_Self` and `Tgt_All_Party_Critters`, cost 40, maintained 5 every 10 s, `Cancels_SF` `OSF_TEMPUS_FUGIT` (also in non-college record 128), BEGIN sets and END clears the flag on the caster and every party critter. The only effect is in the stat getter: for **speed (stat 11)** of a PC or NPC, after the base level and before the effects, when the **local player's** spell flags hold Tempus Fugit (0x458A80: the player handle 0x40DA50 and field 0x14), a critter that holds it gains 10 and **every other critter loses 10**; then both crippled legs take 5 (IF12), the effects run (0x4EA690) and the result is clamped (0x4B0FC0, 0x4B0FD0). When the player does not hold it, nobody's speed changes, whoever else holds it. | Original; differs on purpose in Steamwoven (TF1, owner decision on XR14 (a)) |
| XR7 | follower join tail (0x45DF82 to 0x45DFB9), removal (0x45E040 → 0x459EA0) | A critter that joins a leader holding Tempus Fugit gets the flag by a plain write (no run, no count). When a follower leaves its leader (0x45E040, before its list entry is removed), 0x459EA0 clears its Tempus Fugit and, when it is an `ONF_FAMILIAR` NPC (NPC flags 0x118, bit 0x8000), clears `OSF_FAMILIAR` on that leader (both through the setter 0x4EFDD0). | Original |
| XR8 | readers of speed (stat 11) | Three call sites pass stat 11 to the getter: the turn-based subturn start (0x4B6F6F: action points = speed, at least 5, CH4), the real-time attack pause (0x42EEFD in 0x42EE90: `1000 / rate(speed)` clamped to 30..800 ms, RT8) and the inventory window's line (0x576DB4). The walk and run cost (0x42EDC0) reads no speed. About 49 other callers pass the stat in a register (script operations, the spell path at 0x455D90, the sheet's loops); none is a fixed speed read, though a script may ask for it. So Hasten (77, +100 %), Congeal Time (76, −50 %), the −5 effects and Tempus Fugit's ±10 change the **action points of a turn-based round and the real-time attack pause**, not walking. | Original |
| XR9 | illusion (`OSF_ILLUSION`, bit 0x8000) | Set only by Phantasmal Fiend (68) on its summoned creature (with mind control). No function tests the bit: a scan of every read of field 0x14 followed within 30 instructions by a test of 0x8000 found none, and 0x454A10 has no case (XR5); only the generic spell-flag masks of the records (`AoE_SF`, `AoE_NO_SF`, `Disallowed_SF`, `Cancels_SF`) could name it, and no installed record does. The flag changes nothing. | Original |
| XR10 | familiar flags (`OSF_FAMILIAR` 0x40000000; `ONF_FAMILIAR` 0x8000) | Familiar (74): `Disallowed_SF` `OSF_FAMILIAR` (a caster who has one cannot cast it again, through the invocation's flag test); BEGIN sets `OSF_FAMILIAR` on the **caster** and `OSF_MIND_CONTROLLED` and `ONF_FAMILIAR` on the **summoned creature**; no END lines. Readers: the "bad associates" test 0x45F460 (a PC's summoned follower that is not `ONF_FAMILIAR`, not an animal (`OCF_ANIMAL` 0x8) and whose object flags lack 0x102; its callers are the greeting's class 18000 (0x41941C) and 0x44577F, which counts the objects of a list (0x44B4E0) it answers for); 0x459EA0 (XR7); and the world-map travel hide 0x45E1E0, which hides and shows the PC and each follower except a **summoned** follower whose own spell flags lack `OSF_FAMILIAR`. The familiar creature carries `ONF_FAMILIAR`, not `OSF_FAMILIAR`, so travel leaves it shown like any other summon. | Original (see XR14) |
| XR11 | detecting traps (`OSF_DETECTING_TRAPS`, bit 0x100) | Set and cleared only by Sense Hidden (8). Setting or clearing any of the four detection bits (magic 0x40, alignment 0x80, traps 0x100, invisible 0x200) on the **local player** calls the module's screen-refresh callback (0x5E6D24, taken from the init block's +0x8 by 0x44EF50) with null; on another critter nothing else happens. The one reader of 0x100 is the trap spot ([TT3](technology.md#trap-state-arc-world-076), 0x4BBEF6 through 0x458A80): while the **local player** holds it, every spot check marks and finds the trap without a roll, whoever the observer is. | Original |
| XR12 | magically held (bit 0x4 of the portal flags 0x2E) | Magelock (75) sets it on BEGIN and clears it on END, on portals only (`OPF_MAGICALLY_HELD`; no installed record writes `OCOF_MAGICALLY_HELD`); a plain write (XR5): no aptitude test, no event (unlike the lock bit, LK3). Readers: the open attempt answers 3 (LK7; goal 11 ends the opener's run, goal 12 plays the locked sound, GT39, GT40); the close goal 50 refuses at node 3 (0x428620, GT41), so a held **open** door cannot be closed either; the door goals' end nodes skip their final toggle on a held portal (XR13); the hover line shows `intgame.mes` 27 (OH10); the target areas' "not magically held" test (TA8). Dispel clears the bit by a plain write without the count (0x451EC4, also bit 0x4 of a container's 0x38). Pick locks (PL1) and the lock event (LK5) do not read or clear it. | Original |
| XR13 | door end nodes (0x42B640 goal 70, 0x42B6F0 goal 71) | Both act only when the run's flags (+0xC) lack 0x8000 and 0x410060 answers 0, and only on a portal (type 1). Goal 70's calls the toggle (0x4F08F0) twice on a closed portal, goal 71's once on an open portal, each only when the portal is not magically held; a held portal is left as it is. | Original |
| XR14 | anomaly classification (bug rule) | (a) Tempus Fugit works only when the **local player** holds it (XR6): an NPC that casts it (record 79 has `AI_Flee` 8) changes nobody's speed unless the player holds it too, and when the player holds it every critter outside the spell is slowed by 10. (b) The travel hide (XR10) tests the caster-side flag on the creature, so a familiar is not hidden with the party on the world map. Both are what the executable does; whether either is a bug of the game (a switch under owner decision D8) is decided with the owner by the slice that implements it (MA-9 for (a), E1-e for (b)); until then both modes follow the original. | Original. (a) decided by the owner 2026-11-11 as a bug fixed in both modes: done by ARC-MAGIC-056 (TF1). (b) decided the same way, fixed by E1-e |
| XR15 | Disarm (0): Damage component (0x4518D0) and damage step (0x4B4390) | Record 0: area critters not dead and not the caster, `Resist: (stat_dexterity @ 0)`, BEGIN `Damage` `Dmg_Normal` 0 with `Weap_Dropped` (0x2000), then eye candy. A resisted target is skipped (RP5). Neither function exits on a zero amount: the step's gates are DH5's (a taking-damage script that cancels, a destroyed or invulnerable target, a dead critter, and a nigh-invulnerable critter, critter flags 0xDB bit 0x200 without an "uber" weapon, whose step returns at 0x4B44F8 before the weapon block, so Disarm never drops such a critter's weapon). Its weapon block takes the **target's** item in slot 1004 (0x4B23B0); with the bit set it drops it through 0x461A90 (CR14: 2..4 tiles away, the drop script and the no-drop flag may refuse) and shows message 19; an invulnerable weapon loses only the damage, destroy and explode bits (mask 0xFFFE3FFF), so it is still dropped. A spell's step has no critical procedure (DH5), so the attack rule "not when an NPC hits a PC" (CR9) does not apply: an NPC's Disarm drops a PC's weapon. The PC's weapon auto-switch of CW3 follows. Built in a run of Steamwoven by ARC-MAGIC-1232 (see [DW2](#small-executable-reads-illuminate-unseen-force-on-objects-disarm-arc-res-1109)). | Original |
| XR16 | inventory window mode 4 (See Contents): handler 0x573850, open 0x5727B0, close 0x573440 (ARC-RES-1327) | Read on 2026-11-12 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (decompiled C checked against the listing; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`). **Nothing in mode 4 moves an item.** (a) The left press branch of the window handler (0x57404B..0x57407A) is served only when the window mode (0x683464) is not 4, 5 or 6 (and the press latch 0x68346C is 0): the item pick (cursor modes 8, 10, 11 and 4), the use cursor (1), the examine cursor (2), the spell pick (0x10, IW44) and the item-use cursor all sit behind that test, so a mode 4 press picks nothing whatever the cursor. (b) The right release that picks an item (subtype 3, IW5, IW36; 0x5742C1..0x5742D8) is served in modes 0 to 3 only. (c) The left release acts on a held item (0x6810E0): the open clears it (0x5732C9), the picks above cannot set it in mode 4, and the only other setters (0x573630, 0x573740, the game view's pick-up into the cursor) run while the window is closed, so in mode 4 the release finds nothing held and goes to the pointer pick (0x575FA0): modes 5 and 6 (the barter lists) buy or sell there and then reset the quantity (0x68241C, 0x681440) and redraw; every other mode, mode 4 included, goes to the handler's tail and does nothing at all. (d) The pointer move describes the item under it (0x57CCF0, HK22) in every mode with nothing held; mode 4 adds none of the extra steps of modes 1, 5 and 6. (e) The open turns the drop and use boxes off in modes 4, 5 and 6 (flags 0x681394 and 0x681118 cleared at 0x572F35..0x572F6E, set in modes 0 to 3): mode 4 has no drop or use box. The Take all button is made in mode 2 only and the other party's toggles for a non-container only (IW30), so a container's mode 4 window has the arrange button (340, every mode; it arranges the PC's grid only, the other party's too only with the barter flag 0x6810FC, set in mode 1 alone), the PC's doll toggles and hot spots (IW30) and the round view, whose release closes the window in every mode (IW34). (f) The open (0x57CB80 → 0x572240 with mode 4) uses interface mode 10 (0x572510: modes 2 and 4 share it) and the loot window's art (IW26); the setup 0x572640 only generates a flag 0x200 container (LW2: no open frame or sound, and no sight test or turn, LW1); the close 0x573440 raises the loot counter and runs the junk-pile check without closing the lid (LW3). (g) **One write at the open.** Near its end 0x5727B0 calls the arrange function 0x4670A0 (layout 0, row by row) on the shown object when the barter flag 0x6810FC is 0, the object is not a PC (type 0xF) and no other window has it open (the open table read by 0x4A5460): so opening See Contents re-lays the container's grid out and writes its item locations, as the loot open (mode 2) and steal open (mode 3) do. Built by ARC-RES-1410 (the exe read answers that the write is visible: the window draws the arranged grid at every open, a taken item's old cell closes up at the next open, and items the map's data places in an order of its own take the sorted one): the viewer's open calls `GameSessionOwner.ArrangeHolderGrid` for the shown holder before the window draws, in the loot, steal and See Contents windows; the arrangement is `InventoryArrangeRules.Arrange` on the holder's list (96 rows for a container, 12 for a critter; worn and quick-slot items stay out; no room writes nothing); a seed holder's slots live in a session map by holder and item that the inventory section saves (ARC-RES-1441, schema 95: `inventory.holderSlots`, so after a load the barter window draws the arranged slots, and a list that no longer fits after an add shows the same ones the original's saved locations would), dropped when the item enters a list again; a session junk pile's slots are in its record. PROJECT: the open table test (no other window has the object) always passes in a single-player session and is not modelled; the barter window's open (mode 1) is not covered (it arranges only a holder that is no shop NPC). So See Contents is a view of the items: nothing can be taken, put, dropped, used or worn in it, and only the open's own arrangement writes them. | Original; built by ARC-RES-1327 (see [Identify](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#identify-arc-magic-032)) and (g) by ARC-RES-1410 |

**Implemented since:** XR1 to XR5 by ARC-MAGIC-032 (MA-7): [Identify](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#identify-arc-magic-032); the component answer is the viewer's line; the windows of E8-g: the NPC's sheet ARC-UI-1127 (XR2), the container's mode 4 window and the item description ARC-RES-1327 (XR3, XR4, XR16); the description of an item lying on the map or held by an NPC and the hover window of a portal or scenery named by an Identify ARC-UI-1411 (XR4).

**F0 facts raised:** none (no function is implemented by this task). These rows answer the open questions of the
[magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#not-read-not-verified) for MA-7 (Identify), MA-9 (Tempus Fugit), MA-4 (illusion and familiar), E6 (detecting traps),
MA-6 (magically held), MA-2 (speed) and Disarm. Not read: what happens on arrival to a
summoned follower the travel hide left shown (XR10), and whether the familiar's death or dismissal reaches 0x45E040 (so whether the caster's
`OSF_FAMILIAR` is cleared when a familiar dies).

## ObjFlag component of the cast host (ARC-DEV-358)

Source: slice MA-1 of the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#slices) (ARC-RES-103). Read on 2026-11-11 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`Version.txt` `VERSION:1.0.7.4`; `llvm-objdump`; SHA-256
`9bd80511…8d52` checked): the ObjFlag side effects 0x454A10 and the reference count 0x455100, re-read branch by branch against [XR5](#magic-audit-executable-reads-arc-dev-357) for the
object flags each side effect writes (the set and clear object-flag functions 0x43D0E0 and 0x43D280 and their redraw wrappers 0x43EF10 and 0x43EFA0), the unarmed damage block of the damage
step (0x4B67F1 to 0x4B6855), and the closing write 0x4550C6. Every direct branch of the functions read stays inside `.text`. Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied. The rows XR5 and
XR11 are reused, not re-read.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OB1 | ObjFlag, set branch, spell flags 0x14 (0x454A10) | After the reference count (a set stops while another run provides the bit), the bit is added to the field's value and the first special bit of the mask is handled, in this order: the four detection bits 0x3C0 (the screen-refresh callback only for the local player), invisible 0x1, shrunk 0x80000, water walking 0x200000, stoned 0x10000, entangled 0x1000000, mind controlled 0x8000000, charmed 0x800000; every other bit (floating, body of air, earth, fire and water, shielded, anti-magic shell, bonds of magick, full reflection, illusion, mirrored, passwalled, magnetic inversion, spoken with the dead, Tempus Fugit, drunk, enshrouded, familiar, hardened hands) falls through to the plain write of the value at 0x4550C6. **Invisible** calls the object-flag setter 0x43D0E0 with OF_INVISIBLE 0x200 only when the critter is not the local player (0x40D9F0), then the redraw setter 0x43EF10 with OF_TRANSLUCENT 0x40; **shrunk** sets OF_SHRUNK 0x80 through 0x43EF10; **water walking** sets OF_WATER_WALKING 0x40000 and **stoned** OF_STONED 0x80000 through 0x43D0E0; each then writes the spell flag. A mask names one bit in every installed record, so the "first special bit only" rule (SU17) does not arise for a spell. | Original |
| OB2 | ObjFlag, clear branch, spell flags (0x454DB0 onward) | The count first (a clear stops while more than one run provides the bit, the run being cleared among them); the mirror of OB1 with the clear setters 0x43D280 and 0x43EFA0: invisible clears OF_INVISIBLE (for a critter that is not the local player) and OF_TRANSLUCENT, shrunk clears OF_SHRUNK, stoned clears OF_STONED, water walking clears OF_WATER_WALKING and then **reads the critter's location and asks the tile test 0x4D7110; when it answers true it builds a damage record (0x4B2210) and runs the damage step (0x4B4390) on the critter** before reading the object flags back (what the damage is, and when the tile test is true, were not read: F0, MA-8). Entangled has no clear case (a plain write), mind controlled clears and disbands (0x45DFC0, MA-4), charmed gives −30 reaction (MA-4). | Original; the water-walking damage is F0 for MA-8 |
| OB3 | ObjFlag, object flags 0x13 and critter flags 0xDA (0x454D3E, 0x454FA2) | The object flags only have the don't-draw bit 0x100 as a special one: set, the bit is added through 0x43D0E0 (which writes the field itself, so there is no closing write); for a PC or NPC (type 15 or 16) the critter flags 0xDA then get 0x40 (paralysed) by a plain write, no flee, no interrupt; any other object type stops there.
