Clear: 0x43D280 clears the bit, and for a PC or NPC the critter flags lose 0x40; then, when the second critter flags 0xDB have bit 0x2, the function 0x435D70 runs on the critter (what it does was not read: F0). Any other object-flag bit is a plain write. On the critter flags the special bits are undead 0x4 (set or clear the object flag 0x20000000 and fall through to the closing write), fleeing 0x10 (set: flee 0x4AF130, clear: stop fleeing 0x4AF170; both return before the closing write), blinded 0x80 (set only; returns before the closing write) and paralysed 0x40 (set: the same interrupts as entangled, GT146), all the rest plain. | Original; 0x435D70 is F0 |
| OB4 | other fields (0x454C98 onward, 0x45501C, 0x45505A) | The portal flags 0x2E and container flags 0x38 have a special case only for the clear of locked 0x1 (aptitude test 0x4551C0, resist report 0x45A520, guard alert 0x4AEE50, XR5); any other bit of those fields, the item flags (Divine Magick's identified bit), the NPC flags and the trap flags are plain writes with the count 0 (0x455100 counts runs for field 0x14 only). | Original |
| OB5 | the unarmed damage block of the damage step (0x4B67F1 to 0x4B6855) | For an attacker with no weapon item, the spell flags 0x14 are tested in this order and the first match adds its bonus and ends the block: Body of Earth 0x8 (+5 to the normal damage), Body of Fire 0x10 (+15 fire), Body of Water 0x20 (+15 fatigue), then Hardened Hands 0x80000000 (+2 normal). The resolver's `UnarmedBodySpell` is one value picked in the same order, so a critter holding two gets the first only. The flags are read from the live field, so a body spell written by a spell, a prototype or a script all count. | Original (agrees with the resolver) |

**What MA-1 takes from these rows:** the whole ObjFlag component on the hero and a mobile critter for every bit of the spell, object and critter fields with its side effects (OB1, OB3), the reference
count as before (OB2, OB4), the body-spell bonus fed from the flags a spell wrote (OB5). **Left, by name:** the social and AI flags (mind controlled, charmed, undead, fleeing, blinded: MA-4), the portal and container locks (MA-6), the
item and NPC flag fields (MA-7, E1-c), the drowning damage of the water-walking clear (OB2) and the function 0x435D70 of the don't-draw clear (OB3), and the screen refresh of the detection bits (the viewer reads the flags).

## Readers of the drunk and shielded spell flags (ARC-RES-126)

Source: slice R-1 of the [technology re-audit](../technology-audit-res-125.md#6-queue) (ARC-RES-125) for the technology records 172, 181 (drunk) and 209 (shielded) and the magic audit's
[flag table](../magic-audit-res-103.md); requirements (reference only): [FR-MAGIC-010](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-010), [FR-MAGIC-011](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-011),
[FR-MAGIC-012](../../requirements/combat-magic-tech.md#fr-magic-012). Question: which code of the original reads the object spell flags `OSF_DRUNK` (bit 28, 0x10000000) and `OSF_SHIELDED` (bit 10, 0x400)
of field 0x14, and what it does. Read on 2026-11-11 in the exports of the 1.0.7.4 executable (`VERSION:1.0.7.4`; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52` checked; the decompiled C of `research-private/ghidra-export/c`,
checked against the listing `text.asm` for the immediates) by this task alone (F1; the PR's independent review repeats the scans). Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied. Two scans: (a) every
function that reads field 0x14 through the object getter 0x406CA0 with the literal 0x14 (about 100 functions and 110 to 120 call sites, the exact count depending on whether the two-argument getter form is counted) and the mask each tests; (b) every code unit with the immediate 0x10000000
(52 or 53 sites in 36 functions) and every `& 0x400`, each traced back to the field its variable came from. The data count is a throwaway local program (outside the repository, nothing of the installed data kept)
over the 223 records of `rules/spelllist.mes` through the project's reader (`LikeOriginal`): which record names either bit in `Cancels_SF:`, `Cancels_EnvSF:`, `Disallowed_SF:`, `Disallowed_TSF:` or in the area masks `AoE_SF:` and `AoE_NO_SF:` (line 0 and every action's prefixed form; the same program with all bits finds 8 area masks, none with either bit).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SFR1 | the scans | Every test of 0x10000000 in the image reads another field: the object flags 0x13 (0x442520, 0x443170, and the setter and clearer 0x43D0E0, 0x43D280), the inventory-list field 0x147 (0x43AAF0, 0x43AEC0, 0x43B390, 0x43DFD0), the combat-state field 0x118 (0x432990, 0x4AABE0, 0x4AC180, 0x4AC7B0, 0x4AC910, 0x4B8040, the setter 0x4AA1B0), the critter flags 0xDA (0x429760, 0x4AB990), the goal-run fields (0x423560), the target picker's context words (0x4F2D20, 0x4F28A0, 0x4F40B0), the interface list flags (0x4DD150 to 0x4DDB80), the spell targeting word of an area (0x57BC70), the inventory field 0x147 (0x4D9590) and constants of the file and window layer. The tests of 0x400 on field 0x14 are one: 0x459A30 (SFR3). No function shifts field 0x14 by 28 or tests a single bit by a bit-test instruction. Two functions test the whole word against masks they are given: the forbidden-flags test 0x456430 (`Disallowed_SF:`, `Disallowed_TSF:`) and the target picker 0x4F2D20 (the area's required mask `AoE_SF:`, which must be fully present, and forbidden mask `AoE_NO_SF:`, which must be absent); a third, 0x49C060 (from 0x49BF80), prints the whole word bit by bit by its `OSF_*` names, a description dump that changes no behaviour. | Original (F1) |
| SFR2 | drunk (0x10000000; records 172, 181) | **No behaviour of the executable reads it.** No instruction tests the bit of field 0x14 as a literal (the wrappers 0x459170, 0x459040 and 0x458A80 are never called with it); the only routes are the data masks of 0x456430 and 0x4F2D20 (SFR1), and no installed spell or technology record names the bit in `Cancels_SF:`, `Cancels_EnvSF:`, `Disallowed_SF:`, `Disallowed_TSF:`, `AoE_SF:` or `AoE_NO_SF:` (0 of 223 records). Nothing clears it but a run's END through the ObjFlag component (OB2). The only code that looks at it at all is the debug dump of SFR1. The bit is stored, saved and removed with its run; whatever drunkenness does in the original comes from the Effect components the records carry (172 and 181 have them, [technology audit](../technology-audit-res-104.md)), not from the flag. A draw or AI rule for the bit would be an invention. | Original (F1) |
| SFR3 | shielded (0x400; record 209 and Shield of Protection, spell 30), the hit effect (0x459A30) | The only literal reader of the bit (the data routes are SFR4): for a critter that has it, the run finder (0x459170, ER6) looks for the first active run whose set-flags word holds 0x400 and, when one is found, plays that spell's hit eye candy on the critter (entry 6 × spell + 5, ER4); with no run found it prints the module's assertion text (0x5B204C) and plays nothing. Reached from the hits of ER5 (a hit on a PC or NPC that the weapon's own hit effect does not answer: the attack end 0x4B6930 directly for a hit with no damage slot left, or through the damage effect 0x433020 with its early exits, damage 0, a setting, a hidden or off object, the no-blood flag; none when the packet has flag 0x80). The same function also looks at 0x400000 (magnetic inversion: the same run search, but with no run it also plays a fixed eye candy, 0x449) and at 0x1000000 (entangled: no eye candy here, the re-resistance and the run's end of ER4 instead). It changes no state for the shielded bit: no damage, resistance or reaction. | Original (F1; refines ER4, whose "entangled plays the hit eye candy" is read here as the re-resistance only) |
| SFR4 | the data readers of shielded | One record lists the bit: record 83 (an item shield of the 80..139 group) has it in `Disallowed_TSF:` (0x400), so its cast is refused on a target that is already shielded (the forbidden-flags test, IV5, 0x456430). No `Cancels_*`, `Disallowed_SF:` or area mask (`AoE_SF:`, `AoE_NO_SF:`, read by the target picker 0x4F2D20) names it. The session already reads this through `SpellInvocationRules` (`DisallowedTargetSpellFlags`), so the symbol search of the audit missed it only because the test is data-driven. | Original (F1; data fact) |
| SFR5 | what the bit does not do | The "shielded" of the combat code is the accidental-hit test (`HostilityRules`), not this bit; the resistance half of Shield of Protection (effects 63 and 155) comes through the effect list, not the flag (MA-2). The draw of a shielded or drunk critter has no flag-specific path in the executable: the shield's visible part is the hit eye candy of SFR3 and the cast's own eye candy components. | Original (F1) |

**What the audit takes from these rows:** for the technology records 172, 181 and 209 the viewer has no flag reader left to add except the hit eye candy of SFR3 (a presentation slice: the struck critter plays the run's
hit eye candy for shielded and magnetic inversion at a hit; the entangled branch is the re-resistance of ER4, already built); drunk needs nothing. The "no reader" statement of the technology re-audit is correct for the session and the viewer and, for drunk,
for the original too. **Left, by name:** the eye candy entries and the fixed entry of one prototype in ER4 (presentation, not decoded), the origin of the packet flag 0x200000 (ER5), and a draw rule for don't-draw,
hidden, shrunk and translucent critters, which are other bits and other readers (E4-a2's second half).

## Dispel component (ARC-MAGIC-034)

Source: slice MA-3 of the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#slices) (ARC-RES-103, owner decisions D1–D6 of 2026-11-11). Read on 2026-11-11 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`VERSION:1.0.7.4`; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`
checked) by this task alone (F1): the component 0x451B90 (`llvm-objdump`), its wrapper 0x451BB0 and the function 0x451C40 (decompiled C of the local export, checked against the disassembly's branches), the
ending by event 0x4571E0 and the run slot's free 0x455960, the object-gone endings 0x459740 and the run creation 0x455C30 (the copy of the run's object blocks), and the Trait restore 0x452CD0 that Dispel calls
for a perma-polymorph. Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied. SUM8 (the Summon audit) read the middle of 0x451C40 first; the rows below finish it.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DP1 | Dispel component (type 8, 0x451B90) and wrapper (0x451BB0) | The component has no data. It loads the run (global 0x5E75F0) and the object the per-target stage handed it (globals 0x5E6D48/4C, as the Damage and Destroy executors read it) and calls the wrapper with that object and the run's slot id (the run's first field). The wrapper first asks the network test 0x4A2BA0 (1 in single player, [multiplayer evidence](../multiplayer-evidence.md)): in single player it goes straight on; for a network client it returns at once when bit 2 of 0x62AC6C is clear and otherwise sends a packet (kind 0x4A) and then goes on. With a null object it does nothing; otherwise it calls 0x451C40 with the run's id as the run being processed. A tile target has no object: nothing. | Original (the network packet is not modelled) |
| DP2 | Dispel (0x451C40) | Given the run being processed (a slot id; -1 returns) and the object (null returns). It reads the object's type (field 0x14F). **Runs, loop 1:** every one of the 512 slots, whatever its active bit, whose source (+0x10) is the object, whose id differs from the run being processed and whose record lacks the technology flag (record +0x34 bit 0x4) ends by event (0x4571E0). **Loop 2:** every slot whose active bit is set (+0x13C bit 0x1) and whose slot index differs from the run being processed and whose record is not technological ends by event once for each of three tests it matches, in this order: its target (+0xA0), an entry of its summoned list (+0x134, SUM7) naming the object, an entry of its other object list (+0x130) naming the object. A run the first ending freed (a BEGIN never processed: 0x4571E0 frees it, 0x455960 empties its lists and clears its active bit) matches nothing after. **The object half, for a PC or NPC (type 15 or 16):** it reads the spell flags (field 0x14) once; a stoned bit 0x10000 is cleared by a plain write of that field (no reference count), and **then the object flags clear 0x43D280 is called with 0x80000** (the first read of this row missed that call; found by the MA-6 review, ARC-MAGIC-039, F1 on the decompiled C of 0x451C40 and 0x43D280): it clears the object flag stoned 0x80000 when it is set, and with it the render bit 0x2000000 of field 0x147 (a no-op when the object flag is not set), inside the stoned branch only (a critter whose spell flag is not stoned keeps its object flag), then redraws; then, on that first reading, a set summoned bit 0x4000 destroys the object (0x43CCA0); then the critter flags 2 (field 0xDB): a set perma-polymorph bit 0x400000 calls the Trait restore 0x452CD0 with the object's current art (field 1) and then clears the bit by a plain write. **For a portal (type 1):** flag 0x4 of the portal flags (field 0x2E) is cleared; **for a container (type 2):** flag 0x4 of the container flags (field 0x38). A freed slot (0x455960) keeps its +0xA0 handle but loses its active bit, so loop 2 skips it. Any other type: nothing more. Nothing is paid, rolled or logged. | Original |
| DP3 | run object blocks (0x455C30) and the object-gone endings (0x459740) | The run copies the invocation's source, parent, target and other object into four blocks of 0x48 bytes at +0x10, +0x58, +0xA0 and +0xE8 (the invocation's +0x8, +0x40, +0x70 and +0xA0). So **+0xA0 is the run's target object and +0xE8 its other object** (the attacker of an item's attack): the object-gone endings 0x459740 and Dispel 0x451C40 both read +0xA0, which DS4 called "the other object". Steamwoven's object-gone loop read the other object and ended a run that merely had the dead critter as its attacker, not the runs aimed at it. | Original (reconstruction slip in `SpellRunSystem.ObjectGone`, fixed by ARC-MAGIC-034; not an exe bug, no switch) |

**What MA-3 takes from these rows:** the runs' half of DP2 for the source, target and summoned-list tests (`SpellRunSystem.Dispel`; the host answers the summoned list, SUM7); the object half for the stoned spell flag of the
hero and a critter this host knows and the destruction of a summoned created critter (`SessionSpellCasterDispel`); DP3's fix of `ObjectGone`. **Left, by name:** the hero with the summoned bit 0x4000 (the original destroys a PC too; the host never destroys the hero), the stoned clear for a created critter (its flags are not the session's flag layer), the other object list (+0x130; no host models it; the summoned list never holds a critter until E1-e, because the cast map does not return created critters), the
perma-polymorph with its Trait restore (the session keeps no second critter flags field; the restore is MA-8's), the magically held bit of a portal or container (the lock state and its writer, Magelock, are MA-6's),
the summoned critters other than the session's created ones (a map critter whose data holds 0x4000: no spell makes one yet, E1-e), and the network packet.

## Social and AI flags (ARC-MAGIC-035)

Source: slice MA-4 of the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#slices) (ARC-RES-103, owner decisions D1–D6 of 2026-11-11). Read on 2026-11-11 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`VERSION:1.0.7.4`; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`
checked) by this task alone (F1): the ObjFlag executor 0x454A10 (decompiled C of the local export, then **re-read branch by branch in the disassembly**: the decompiler treats the plain-write call 0x406D40 as a call that does not return and drops the code
after it, so the clear and set branches of mind control and charm were taken from the instructions), its caller 0x452380 (the arguments, from the disassembly), the reaction change 0x4C0DE0, the setter 0x4AABE0 (state read 0x4AAB00), flee 0x4AF130,
stop fleeing 0x4AF170, the blinded setter 0x4B81B0, peace 0x4AA300 and the follower join 0x45DE90 and leave 0x45DFC0. Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied. XR5 (the side-effect list), OB1 to OB4 and SU17 (the first special bit only) are reused, not re-read.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SO1 | the caller 0x452380 → 0x454A10 (arguments) | The component's globals become the arguments: the target object (0x5E6D48), the run's **parent** (+0x58, copied to 0x5E6D30 at the run's start, 0x4536CF) as the third and fourth, the field, mask and on/off from the component (+0x40, +0x44, +0x48), then the run's parent again (+0x58) and the run's **source** (+0x10) as the last two pairs. So the "caster" of every side effect below is the run's **parent**, and the one a fleeing NPC flees from is the parent too. | Original |
| SO2 | charmed 0x800000 (0x454C04 set, 0x454F22 clear) | Only for an NPC (type 16); any other object goes to the closing write. **Set:** an animal (critter flags 0xDA bit 0x8) calls peace 0x4AA300 with the parent (HS18); any other NPC whose parent is a **PC** (type 15: 0x40DA20) gets reaction **+30** from the parent (0x4C0DE0: nothing for a non-PC); then, for every NPC, **NPC flag 0x800 (`ONF_KOS_OVERRIDE`) is added** (0x454C74); the closing write follows. **Clear:** reaction **−30** from a PC parent (the clear branch itself has no PC test; 0x4C0DE0 does nothing for a non-PC); the NPC flag is **not** cleared, so a charmed NPC keeps the kill-on-sight override for good. | Original (the lasting NPC flag is as the executable has it) |
| SO3 | mind controlled 0x8000000, set (0x454B69) | A **null parent** goes to the closing write (a plain write). With a parent: an object that is no NPC **returns before any write** (0x454B92 → 0x4550EF): the flag is not written on the hero. For an NPC: when the parent is a PC and the NPC's spell flags (0x14) lack 0x4000 (summoned) the reaction **−50** from the parent comes first (so the reaction reads the NPC before the flag is set: 0x4C0DE0 does nothing for an NPC mind controlled by that PC, ReactionRules.Adjust); then the spell flags are written; then **the forced join 0x45DE90 (force 1)** of the NPC under the parent (an error line when it answers 0); then **set no-flee 0x4AF1D0**; the function returns, so there is no closing write. The leader is whoever the parent is: 0x45DE90 tests only that the follower is an NPC. | Original |
| SO4 | mind controlled, clear (0x454EDB) | For an NPC: the spell flags are written (cleared), then the **forced leave 0x45DFC0 (force 1)** (an error line when it answers 0); the closing write follows (the same value again). The reaction −50 is **not** given back and no-flee stays. Any other object: the closing write. The unforced leave's refusals (an extraordinary-charisma leader, a controlling spell: LV, C6) do not apply to a forced one. | Original |
| SO5 | fleeing 0x10 (critter flags 0xDA), 0x4AF130 and 0x4AF170 | **Set:** 0x4AF130(object, parent): for an NPC the setter 0x4AABE0 with state 2 (FLEE) and the parent as the fleeing-from handle; any other object nothing; **the function returns before the closing write** (the setter writes the flags it keeps). **Clear:** 0x4AF170: for an NPC in FLEE the setter with state 0 (NONE), then 0x44E4D0 ends the runs whose goal is 25 (flee), inside the FLEE branch only (an NPC in another state is left alone, ARC-AI-1595); returns before the closing write. The hero: nothing, no write. (MR12 has the callers; SU17 the first-bit rule.) | Original |
| SO6 | blinded 0x80, set (0x4B81B0) and clear | **Set:** 0x4B81B0 reads the critter flags and, when 0x80 was not set, writes them with it, then floats the blinded text over the critter (0x4D43A0, 0x4D5450: presentation, not done; the decompiled C cut the function after the write, the disassembly shows the rest) and answers 1 (0 with no text when the bit was set); the executor returns before the closing write. **Clear:** no case: the closing write (a plain write) takes the bit off. The AI gate's blindness recovery (a 1 in N roll each pass) clears the same bit (SchedulerRules). Steamwoven keeps blindness as the **life state's blinded condition** (the one the combat resolver, the skill checks and the gate read), so the cast host writes that, not a second flag word. | Original |
| SO7 | undead 0x4 (critter flags) | **Set:** the object flag **0x20000000** is set through 0x43D0E0, then the closing write stores the critter flag; **clear:** 0x43D280 clears it and the closing write clears the critter flag. | Original |
| SO8 | what the cast host cannot do yet | (1) The NPC flag KOS_OVERRIDE (SO2): built by ARC-MAGIC-1142 (SO9, SO10); until then the session kept no NPC flag layer and the hostility rule read the data's flag only. (2) A parent that is no hero joined no one while the party had the hero as its one leader; built by ARC-MAGIC-1841 (the join under any parent, on ARC-WORLD-1840's NPC leaders). (3) The combat resolver and the life rules read the **data's** undead bit, so the undead flag a spell writes does not make the target undead for combat, healing or poison yet. (4) A creature the session made (`oid:critter:n`) takes none of these flags (E1-e). (5) The reaction facts the AI host builds carry `MindControlledByPc` false, so a later reaction change of a mind-controlled NPC is not skipped (the original skips it: ReactionRules.Adjust has the rule). (6) An END after the NPC left the shown map: the blinded condition is still cleared (the session holds the life record), but charm's −30 and the stop-fleeing need the AI host, which does not run an NPC of another map, so they are logged `SocialRefused` and the +30 stays. | Steamwoven gaps (named, not guessed) |
| SO9 | the NPC flags field (0x118) in the cast host, ARC-MAGIC-1142's build | Re-read 2026-11-12 in the text export and the listing (0x454C74, 0x4A983D): the charm's add is a read-modify-write of field 0x118 with 0x800 (the clear branch has no such write, SO2). The session now keeps field 0x118 as the sixth field of a critter's written flags (`SessionObjectFlagField.Npc`, schema 93): set and cleared bits over the data's value, no reference count (the original counts only spell flags, OB1), the data's value unknown to the cast map (so the charm's set is kept whatever the data holds). | Original (the lasting flag is as the executable has it) |
| SO10 | attack event clear of NPC flag 0x800 (0x4A9650 at 0x4A983D), undead read | After the concealment step 0x45EE30 and for a victim of type 0x10 (NPC) only, the function reads field 0x118, clears 0x800 and writes it back with the plain write 0x406D40, before the PC victim's response 0x4A9AD0 and the victim's followers; the plan (`HostilityRules.AttackEvent`) already said so (`ClearsKosOverride`) and the AI host now carries it out through the layer with the data's value, so a victim without the bit leaves no row. The hostility facts (`AreaAiHost.Find`) read NPC flags and critter flags (the undead bit, 0x4AE120's enshroud test) over the data's through the layer. The combat resolver and the life rules still read the data's undead bit when a life row is built (SO8 (3)). | Original; the combat and life reading stays a gap |

**What MA-4 takes from these rows:** the whole of SO2 to SO7 on the hero and an NPC the map knows, in the original's order (`SessionSpellCasterSocial.cs`), with the AI host's reaction, peace, flee and stop-fleeing as the cast host's social host
(`ISpellSocialHost`, `AreaSpellSocial`), the follower join and leave and no-flee through the session, and the blinded life condition (`GameSessionOwner.SetBlinded`). **Left, by name:** SO8 except (1), which ARC-MAGIC-1142 built (SO9, SO10), except (2), which ARC-MAGIC-1841 built (the join under any parent), and except the AI host's half of (3).

## Movement component and the immediate path relocation (ARC-MAGIC-036)

Source: slice MA-5 of the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#slices) (ARC-RES-103, owner decision D3b of 2026-11-11: tile movement first). Read on 2026-11-11 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`VERSION:1.0.7.4`; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`) by this
task alone (F1): the component 0x4524C0 (component table 0x5B0BA8, slot 15), the relocation 0x455350, the path request 0x41F3C0 with its two searches 0x41F840 and 0x41F9F0, the tile script 0x4C0980, the animation interrupt 0x424070 and
the move 0x43E770, all from the regenerated Ghidra export (the cut-off caveat of 0x406D40 did not touch these functions). Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied. CP10 of [change of place](../../architecture/change-of-place.md#the-s3-read-of-the-executable) read the same component's outline.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MV1 | Movement component (type 15, 0x4524C0) | It acts only with a current target object (globals 0x5E6D48/4C not both 0) **and a run whose resistance margin (+0x144) is 0**; otherwise it returns silently. It switches on the component's `Move_Location:` (+0x40). **Type 0** (Target_Tile): the location is the run's **target tile** (+0xE0/+0xE4). **Type 1** (Random_Tile_In_Radius_Self): the location is the placement 0x4F4E40 of the run's object with the component's radius (+0x44). **Type 2** (Teleport_Tile): the anti-teleport test 0x4BDD30 of the run's parent, then the teleport request to the current map (host branch) or 0x4608B0 (single player). **Type 3** (Teleport_Area_Portal): the helper 0x452650. For the types 0 and 1 a location of (0, 0) moves nothing; otherwise the relocation 0x455350 runs on the target object, with no request and no cache pass. | Original |
| MV2 | Immediate path relocation (0x455350) | It asks the one path request 0x41F3C0 for a route from the object's location to the location, **with flags 0x10 (the drop's) and a 200-rotation buffer**, and reads **only the returned count**, which the request caps at 8 (the closing clamp of 0x41F3C0: a longer route returns 8). A count of 0 (equal tiles, a blocked destination, no route) does nothing at all: no interrupt, no move. Otherwise it steps the first `count` rotations of the buffer from the object's location (0x4B8FF0; a step off the map ends the walk) and runs the **tile script (0x4C0980) of every tile it steps onto, the last one included**; then it interrupts the object's animation goals at priority 5 (0x424070, no network notice), places the object on the last tile with zero pixel offsets (0x43E770) and, for the local player, centres the view on it (0x4B8CE0). A target farther than the cap leaves the object on the eighth tile of the route. A step that fails part-way still interrupts and places the object on the last good tile. | Original |
| MV3 | the move's own notification (0x43E770 → 0x444270) | For a PC or NPC the placement runs the last tile's script **again** (modes 1 and 2), the jump point first for a PC, and the sector's script when the sector changed: so the last tile's script runs twice in one relocation (MV2's walk, then this). The first tiles' jump points are never run. | Original (kept) |

**What MA-5's first half takes from these rows:** type 0 for the hero and a critter the cast host knows (`SessionSpellCasterMovement`): the cast host logs the request (`SpellCastEventKind.Movement`) and the host asks the path (`AreaDropMap`, the drop's request), walks it
(`ImmediatePathWalk`), interrupts the walk, places the critter and runs the scripts (the hero: a tile-script-only entry per tile and the last tile's own entry; an NPC or companion: `AreaAiHost.RelocateAlongPath`). **Left, by name:** type 1 (a random tile, the placement
0x4F4E40 without the drop's ten tries), type 2 (Teleportation, spell 4: the anti-teleport zones, the change of place and the followers) and type 3 (the area portal, record 218): MA-5's second half; objects that are no critter; the interrupt of the object's
goals other than its walks (the spell's own cast run is not the host's to end); the centring of the view on a relocated hero beyond the camera following it; the tile scripts run **after** the placement (the original runs them before it, from the object's old
location); a created critter as the target.

## Teleport types and the anti-teleport test (ARC-MAGIC-038)

Source: the second half of slice MA-5 of the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#movement-ma-5-arc-magic-036) (owner decision D3b of 2026-11-11). Read on 2026-11-11 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`VERSION:1.0.7.4`; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`) by this task alone (F1), from the Ghidra
export in `research-private` (decompiled C, the disassembly and the tables of ARC-DEV-385 beside it): the Movement component 0x4524C0 with its area helper 0x452650, the anti-teleport module 0x4BD9E0 (with its two parsers 0x4BDB30 and 0x4BDC60) and test 0x4BDD30, the invocation check
0x4564E0, the world map's teleport mode 0x560790 / 0x5607E0 / 0x5618B0 with the area pick 0x4CB2A0, the last-discovered slot 0x4CB160 and the cost 0x4507D0, the placement 0x4F4E40 and the relocation 0x455350. Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied. The installed
`Rules\AntiTeleport.mes` was read by the project's own reader: numbers only, no text kept.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TP1 | Anti-teleport table (0x4BD9E0, parsers 0x4BDB30 and 0x4BDC60) | The module reads `Rules\AntiTeleport.mes` once at the module's load. **Regions** are the consecutive entries from 100 up to the first missing one: each is `x, y, radius` (three numbers separated by commas; the radius is stored **times 64**, as 64-bit values). **Disabled maps** are the consecutive entries from 1000: one number each, stored as the runtime map number (a number from 5001 up minus 4999, a lower number plus 1). A missing file only warns: the module stays **off** and every teleport is allowed. The installed file has 2 regions and 5 disabled maps. | Original |
| TP2 | Anti-teleport test (0x4BDD30) | Asked for an object handle. In this order: no object (a zero handle) refuses; the module off allows; the object's **current map** in the disabled list refuses; then the test measures a place: on the start map (map list entry 1) the **object's own tile**, on any other map the **location of the map's area** on the start map (the map list's area, 0x40FFE0, then the area table, 0x4CAED0; a map with no list row refuses). The measuring place refuses when its Chebyshev tile distance (0x4B96F0) to **any** region's centre is **below** the region's radius (signed 64-bit); the callers that pass a second, target location other than (0, 0) have it measured the same way. | Original |
| TP3 | Movement types 2 and 3 (0x4524C0, helper 0x452650) | Both run behind the component's gates of MV1 (a current target object, margin 0). **Type 2** (Teleport_Tile): the test TP2 on the run's **parent** (the spell's caster handle, no second place); a refusal shows message 10000 to the local player's screen, whoever the parent is, and stops. Otherwise a multiplayer host sends the teleport request to the current map; in a single-player game it calls the world map's teleport mode (0x4608B0 → 0x560790) with the run's source and spell, and the component itself moves nothing. **Type 3** (Teleport_Area_Portal, technology record 218): the helper finds the **area of the object** (on the start map the area whose radius covers its tile, 0x4CB4D0 with the arrival flag clear; on any other map the map's own area, 0x40FFE0); no area, or a refusal by TP2 (measured as above), shows message 10000 when the object is the local player and stops; otherwise the object is sent to the **start map at the area's location** (0x4CAED0) with the teleport spell's change-of-place flags 0x120, the sector cache emptied first (CP of [change of place](../../architecture/change-of-place.md#the-s3-read-of-the-executable)). The invocation check 0x4564E0 asks TP2 for the **parent** before the cast for spell 4 only (IV9) and shows the same message. | Original |
| TP4 | World map teleport mode (0x560790, 0x5607E0, 0x5618B0, 0x4CB2A0, 0x4CB160, 0x4507D0) | 0x560790 opens the world map from any map with the teleport-mode flag set and the run's source and spell stored (0x5C9220 holds the spell). A click picks the **nearest known area** within **320** tiles of the clicked point (0x4CB2A0); a click that finds none does nothing. The picked area's location is measured by TP2 (the object, the area as the target); a refusal shows **message 640** and the window stays open. Otherwise the spell is paid by the run's cost (0x4507D0: the cost of the spell's own record, 0x450420) **only when the source is the local player** (0x40D9F0), the area is cleared from the last-discovered slot when it was in it (0x4CB160), and the player character lands there with the world map's landing (0x561860, flags 0x20) and the window closes. The window closed any other way (Escape, the close button) pays nothing and moves nothing. The payment's answer is **ignored**: a player character that cannot pay still lands. Spell 4's record has `ChargeBeginCost: 0`, so the BEGIN of the run charges nothing: the cost is paid here (0x5618B0 reaches 0x4507D0 through its thunk 0x4507B0). | Original |
| TP5 | Random tile (type 1, 0x4F4E40) and the order of the scripts (0x455350) | Type 1 draws **one direction over 0 to 8** (0x4CEF00; 8 is in place), then walks the component's `Tile_Radius:` steps in that direction from the tile of the run's target block (the host uses the current target object's tile: the same except for a run with several targets); a step off the map means no relocation (the placement returns nothing); a location of (0, 0) moves nothing; otherwise the relocation of MV2 follows. The installed records use no type 1 (Move_Location: only 0 of spell 3, 2 of spell 4 and 3 of record 218). **Order of the relocation's effects** (read again for MV2): the tile script of every tile walked, the last included, **before** the animation interrupt and the placement (so a script sees the object on its old tile), then the placement's own notification of the last tile (MV3). | Original |
| TP6 | Scripted open and the town map (0x560790, 0x5607E0, 0x560EF0, 0x560FA0, 0x560150, 0x562B70, 0x5618B0 release of 811) | The scripted flag 0x66D9C8 is tested in **five places only**: the open (0x5607E0, three tests), the release of button 811 and the kind-0 release (0x5618B0). The open sets the town number 0x66D874 to **0** for it, where the ordinary open reads the PC's sector (0x560EF0, 0x4BE380) and sets the walkable flag; the window creation restores the kind that the close reset to 0 (0x560150), so the window opens in kind 0 and the "PC in a town-map sector goes straight to the town map" step (WM6) is skipped. A switch to the town map (0x562B70 with kind 2, from 811 or the key `Z`) returns at once while 0x66D874 is 0, and 811 with it 0 means kind 0: **the town map cannot be reached during a Teleportation pick**, from a town or any other map. The continent view (812) and its zoom need no town number and work as in the ordinary open. | Original |
| TP7 | Route points and the journey in the scripted open (0x560790, 0x5618B0 press and release, 0x562B70, 0x5615D0) | The scripted open clears the waypoint count 0x64E408 and the walkable flag 0x66D880, so the click-armed flag 0x64E410 (= walkable, set at every press and at the open) is **0**: a kind-0 release in tool state 0 takes the pick of TP4 and never adds a waypoint (the list is empty, so nothing is selected or dragged either; the project's art view keeps its own armed flag but its press is redirected to the pick); tool state 1 (the waypoint drag) does nothing without the walkable flag; the travel button 813 is hidden by the kind switch in a non-walkable kind 0 (WM62, 0x562B70), so no journey starts. The compass buttons (scroll in the eight directions, WM31), the readouts, the hover texts, the continent view and the lens branches carry no test of the scripted flag and work as in the ordinary open. | Original |
| TP8 | Refusals and the fallback of the scripted open (0x5607E0) | The refusals of WM3 apply as they do to `W`: no PC, 0x45D8D0 and 0x45D800 silently, the encounter flag 602, a blocked PC sector 605, a PC in combat that cannot leave it 600 (one that can leaves combat first). When the map module's loader (0x560AA0) answers 0, or the window cannot be built (0x560FA0), the ordinary open shows message 605 (0x25D); the scripted open shows nothing and ends in the area helper 0x452650 of TP3: **Teleportation with no world map behaves as the area portal**. The area helper reads the same world-map data, so the fallback cannot run where the data cannot be read. | Original |
| TP9 | Followers of a teleporting PC (0x561860, 0x4D3460, 0x4D3760, CP4, CP16) | The pick's landing is the change of place of the PC with flags 0x20 only (the blank screen; flag 0x100 is clear): every **direct follower for which 0x45D800 answers yes is ordered to wait** (0x4AA7A0) first, then the move list is the PC and its followers **recursively** (the follower list 0x4411C0 of each NPC object; a follower whose flag field 0x118 has bit 8 set is left out; the inventory of each object follows it), and the payload writes them all on the destination. Nothing limits it by distance, by the map a follower stands on or by what it is doing, and nothing asks the follower or charges for it: **the whole party arrives with the PC**, and the preparation writes each carried NPC's teleport map (CP16), the tie that sends a follower released later on a foreign map home (FM10). The area portal (0x452650, flags 0x120) carries the same list without the wait orders (flag 0x100). The spell's cost (TP4) is the only payment. | Original |

**What MA-5's second half takes from these rows:** `AntiTeleportTable` (Data, TP1) and `AntiTeleportRules` (Simulation, TP2); the world-map data loads the table in its background load (`AreaWorldMapData.AntiTeleport`); the cast host's `ISpellTeleportHost` answers TP2 and the area helper of TP3 over the shown map
(`AreaSpellTeleport`, World), the invocation check asks it for spell 4, and the component logs `TeleportMap` (type 2) or `TeleportPortal` (type 3); the viewer opens the world map in the teleport mode of TP4 and makes the portal's change of place (`TerrainViewerTeleport`); type 1 logs the drawn direction
(`MovementRandom`) and the host places with `DropPlacementRules.PlaceOnce` (the placement of TP5 without the drop's ten tries); the tile scripts of a walked relocation run before the object is placed, for the hero and for NPCs.
**Left, by name:** the multiplayer host branch of type 2 (the game is single-player); an NPC or other non-critter as the portal's or relocation's target (the host places critters only, and a mover with no player character cannot open the destination map); message 640 is an English
label (the text is not read from the message file); the centring of the view on the relocated hero; the interrupt of goals other than walks. Type 1 is not exercised by an installed record, so it has unit tests and a synthetic smoke event only.

**What ARC-MAGIC-1147 takes from TP6–TP9** (MT16, the teleport mode's window and the followers): the original has no town map and no route points in the Teleportation pick, so the project's window does not either (the town map is not read for the open, no waypoint is added by a click, and the Travel button is hidden by `AreaWorldMapArtLayout.TravelShown`, the Enter and button paths refuse); the compass stays. The followers go with the PC through the one landing of the world map (the change of place of CP4), so the smoke stage `teleport-follow` observes a companion that stands within the follow distance of the hero after the pick. Not built: the fallback of TP8 (it needs the same data), the followers' recursion and the field-0x118 exclusion beyond what the change of place already models.

## Lock flags of portals and containers (ARC-MAGIC-037)

Source: slice MA-6 of the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#slices) (ARC-RES-103). Read on 2026-11-11 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`VERSION:1.0.7.4`; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`
checked) by this task alone (F1): the ObjFlag executor 0x454A10 from the regenerated decompiled export (`c/`, the plain-write call 0x406D40 returns there) **and the tail of the function from the disassembly** (0x45501C to
0x4550F6: the lock-clear branch and the final write), its caller 0x452380, the unlock gate 0x4551C0, the resisted feedback 0x45A520, the guard alert 0x4AEE50 (arguments and result only), the reference count 0x455100,
the plain field write 0x406D40, the Dispel function 0x451C40 (the portal and container branch) and the per-pass fate flag in 0x4510F0; the three installed records read from the player's data (Unlocking Cantrip 1, Magelock 75, Flesh to Stone 48: components and areas, numbers only).
Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied. XR5 and XR12 (the side-effect list, what the held bit does to a door) are reused, not re-read.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LM1 | ObjFlag on the lock flags, the write (0x454A10, final write at 0x4550C6) | For the portal flags (field 0x2E) and container flags (0x38) the component's branch has side effects **only for the clear of bit 0x1 (locked)**. Every other bit, set or cleared, and the set of locked, is the plain write of the new flags value (0x406D40) and nothing else: no lock function (0x441DD0), so **no Lock event is scheduled** and a door a spell unlocked stays unlocked until a key, a script or the pick-locks skill locks it. The reference count 0x455100 counts runs only for the spell flags (field 0x14), so these fields count 0 and the first of two Magelocks to end clears the bit. The component does not test the object's type: the records' area masks keep field 0x2E to portals and 0x38 to containers. | Original |
| LM2 | the lock-clearing branch (0x45501C to 0x4550E5) | In order: the gate 0x4551C0 on the run's parent (+0x58), the object and its aptitude arguments; a gate that answers 0 plays the resisted feedback (0x45A520 → 0x456E60 with effect 0x283); **the guard alert 0x4AEE50 with loudness 0 and kind 2 follows in both cases** (called with the parent and the object; it returns at once for no parent, a parent that is no PC or NPC, an NPC with no PC up its leader chain (0x45DDA0 answers 0) and an object whose owner field (portal 0x31, container 0x3E) is 0); a refused gate **skips the write** (the lock stays) and goes to the screen refresh (0x460C30); a passed gate writes the flags and then refreshes. The slot the skip tests is the object's type (a passed gate leaves it non-zero), not the gate's answer: a decompiler reads it as one variable. | Original |
| LM3 | unlock gate (0x4551C0) | Without a parent handle it answers 1. A parent that is no PC (15) or NPC (16): 1. The flag at 0x5E75A8 (see LM4): 1. The object's type 1 reads the lock level at field 0x2F (47), type 2 at 0x39 (57), any other type answers 0. A level of 19 or less answers 1 whatever the aptitude (`0x13 < level` is tested first); above it the run's cached parent aptitude (+0x8C, ER2: captured at the run's start, 100 under the fate) must reach the level, else 0. | Original |
| LM4 | the flag 0x5E75A8 (0x4510F0) | Set at the start of every target pass: 1 when the run's source is a PC whose armed fate "spell at maximum" (kind 5) is used for the pass (the pass then also sets both cached aptitudes to 100), else 0. The gate, the first target save and the re-resistance read it. The flag is the last pass's: a nested cast overwrites it and nothing restores it. | Original |
| LM5 | Dispel on a portal or container (0x451C40 at 0x451E7D onwards) | By the object's type: a portal (1) with bit 0x4 of field 0x2E, or a container (2) with bit 0x4 of field 0x38, has the bit cleared by the plain write (no gate, no count, no event, no feedback) and the function returns. Other lock bits stay. A tile, a scenery or a wall is left alone. | Original |
| LM6 | the installed records | **Unlocking Cantrip (1)**: two BEGIN ObjFlag components, `portal_flags` bit 0x1 off with the target mask portal and `container_flags` bit 0x1 off with the mask container; the action's mask is object, portal and container. **Magelock (75)**: BEGIN `portal_flags` bit 0x4 on, END the same off; its action mask is a portal that is not magically held (TA8 reads the held bit of the lock flags) and not invulnerable. **Flesh to Stone (48)**: its first BEGIN pair stones the non-stone critters (spell flag 0x10000 on); its **second pair turns stone back to flesh on a portal that holds the stoned spell flag** (the mask object, portal, stone material, with `AoE_SF` 0x10000 required; the stone and not-stone tests are never made, TA8) and the END pairs are the reverse of both. No record writes the held bit of a container (`OCOF_MAGICALLY_HELD`). | Original (data) |

**What MA-6 takes from these rows:** the whole of LM1 to LM5 for a portal and a container the shown map knows (`SessionSpellCasterLocks.cs`, `GameSessionOwner.WriteOpenableFlag`, `ISpellCastMap.Openable`), the lock flags
in the target objects the area tests read (`AreaSpellCastMap`), the viewer's feedback lines. **Left, by name:** the guard alert's witnesses (0x4AEE50, PL6: built by ARC-AI-2084 after this slice; the cast log has a `LockGuardAlert` line, as pick locks reports
`WitnessesCalled`; the log line is made for a PC or NPC parent only, and the callee's further early returns, the leaderless NPC and the ownerless object, are the witness host's); the screen refresh (a presentation step); the stone-to-flesh pair of Flesh to Stone on a portal that holds the stoned spell flag: the session keeps written flags only for the hero and mobile objects and a portal is static
(nothing in the installed records makes a stone portal; whether any portal of the installed maps stores the stoned flag was not checked, F0); the container's held bit has no writer but Dispel (no record writes it).

## Trait component (ARC-MAGIC-040)

Source: slice MA-8 of the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#slices) (ARC-RES-103; the magic plan's MT18). Read on 2026-11-11 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`VERSION:1.0.7.4`; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`) by this task alone (F1): the component 0x452AD0 (component table
0x5B0BA8, slot 10), its body 0x452B00 and the restore 0x452CD0, with the item helpers 0x464C50, 0x464C80, 0x4649C0, 0x461A70, 0x4654F0 and the art helpers 0x502700, 0x502880, 0x502930, 0x502C40, 0x503CD0, 0x503E20, 0x503E50, 0x503F20, 0x5040D0 and the art set 0x43EB30, all from the
Ghidra export, the argument lists of the art builder 0x503CD0 and the stack cleanup of its caller from the disassembly (the decompiler shows five of its nine arguments at the call). Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied. The earlier rows MG10 of [magic](magic.md) and the triage rows of
[0x44 and 0x45](census-triage-0x44-0x45.md) named these functions; the rows below are the re-read.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TR1 | Trait component (type 10, 0x452AD0) | A wrapper: it passes the current target (0x5E6D48/4C), the component's data (+0x40, six words) and the cached target type (0x5E75AC) to 0x452B00. | Original (kept) |
| TR2 | Trait body (0x452B00), the guards | It acts only with a current target object (both words not 0) whose cached type is **15 (PC) or 16 (NPC)** and only when the component's **first data word is 1** (the field `art_num` of the data; no other value is handled): any other target, type or field does nothing and nothing is logged. The multiplayer prelude (0x4A2BA0) lets a single-player game pass; a multiplayer client returns, a host sends the packet 0x4A and goes on. The fifth data word (+0x10, the parsed positional value) is the species, **−1 restores** (0x452CD0), and the sixth (+0x14) is `Palette:`. | Original (kept) |
| TR3 | Trait apply (0x452B00), the items | Before the new body is set it walks the nine wear slots **1000 to 1008** (helmet, two rings, medallion, weapon, shield, body armour, gauntlets, boots): for each it calls the take-off 0x464C50 (the item at the slot, 0x4649C0, then the unwield 0x464C80, which moves a worn item to the inventory and answers 0 when its checks 0x466DA0 or 0x466510 refuse) twice, and when the slot still holds an item after that **drops it on the holder's tile** (0x461A70 → 0x461AB0: the drop; a refusal logs an error message). **Every** worn item goes, not only the items the new body cannot use; nothing is worn again until the restore. | Original (kept) |
| TR4 | Trait apply, the new body (0x452B00 → the monster art builder 0x503CD0) | The body is a **monster art id** (class 12 in the top four bits): the species (from the component), three zero fields, the **rotation of the old art** (0x502880), the **animation of the old art** (0x503E20), a one-bit weapon part (1 when the old art's weapon part, 0x5040D0, is not 0) and the **palette** (0 to 3); the art id the critter has now is the old art. The builder tests the fields against their widths (species < 32 compared **signed**, so a negative species passes and is masked to its low five bits; palette < 4, compared unsigned) and answers 12 when one is out of range; the caller then logs an error message and **exits the game** (0x58AA7C). The elemental test of TR5 reads the raw value, so a negative species such as −5 builds an elemental-looking body (27) without the elemental flags. The art is set through 0x43EB30: when the object's flag 0x1 is clear it writes field 1 (the current art), clears bit 0x8000000 of field 0x147 and redraws the object's rectangle. The stored art (field 24) is not written. | Original (kept); the exit is a crash: a species of 32 or more or a palette outside 0 to 3 is not ready here, and so is a negative species other than −1 (our choice: the original masks it, which no installed record needs) |
| TR5 | Trait apply, the elemental species (0x452B00) | For the species **26 to 28** (fire, water and air) it afterwards **assigns** the object field 0xDB (the critter flags 2) the value 2 and the field 9 (the blit flags) the value 0x10 by the plain write 0x406D40 (MG10: the other bits of both words are lost, the summoned "no pickpocket" bit among them). The species 3 (Body of Stone) and 20 (Polymorph) set no flag. The apply has **no reference count**: a second apply goes over the first. | Original bug (MG10, owner decision 2026-10-20: fix in Steamwoven with an Original switch) |
| TR6 | Trait restore (0x452CD0) | When the **current** art is a monster art (class 12) of the species 26 to 28 it clears bit 0x2 of the critter flags 2 and bit 0x10 of the blit flags (the restore **clears only those bits**, so it undoes MG10 only for a body that still had the elemental species); it then builds the art from the **stored art** (field 24) with the **current art's rotation and animation** (0x502930, 0x503E50), frame 0 (0x502C40), sets it (0x43EB30) and **chooses the best items for every slot** (0x4654F0 over 1000 to 1008, the starting kit's choice 0x465170). It does this for any PC or NPC, also one the spell never made a monster. | Original (kept) |

**What MA-8's first slice takes from these rows:** the Trait component of art type in the cast host (`SessionSpellCasterTrait`): the choice of the spell (species and palette) kept in the session by critter (`GameSessionOwner.WriteTraitArt`, schema 89, saved and upgraded), the original's guards (TR2), the range
tests as not ready (TR4), no reference count (TR5), and the host told by two lines, `TraitApplied` (the species, the palette, whether the body is an elemental one) and `TraitRestored` (the species it had, whether the session's record was an elemental one; the original's restore tests the **current art** instead: class 12 and species 26 to 28, whatever made it, so the host must test its own art, not only this bit, and the art write of 0x43EB30 is skipped when the object's flag 0x1 is set). The rotation, animation and frame of the body are the viewer's own and a restore
is the removal of the record, because the old art's parts are read when the body is drawn. **Left, by name:** the host's half, the next slice of MA-8: the unwield of the nine slots and the choice of the best items again (TR3, TR6: the hero's equipment, the NPC's worn items), the draw of the monster body for the hero and
NPCs, the weapon-part bit and the shape of the body's animations (a monster art has the animations of its own species); the critter flags 2 and the blit flags (TR5: built by ARC-MAGIC-047, schema 90, with MG10's switch); the Dispel's perma-polymorph undo (built by ARC-MAGIC-047) (critter flags 2 bit 0x400000: no producer in
the session); the multiplayer packet.

## Control components (ARC-MAGIC-044)

Source: slice E4-c of the [technology audit](../technology-audit-res-125.md#6-queue) and the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#slices) (ARC-RES-125, ARC-RES-103; the magic plan's MT15). Read on 2026-11-11 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`VERSION:1.0.7.4`; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`) by this task alone (F1): the component table 0x5B0BA8 (slots 0, 5, 19, 20 and 24), the executors 0x452F00, 0x451850, 0x4529D0, 0x4529F0 and 0x452ED0, the field reader 0x406CA0 and the loop 0x4510F0 that consumes the jump, all from the Ghidra export and the data-dword table. Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied. [Technology evidence](../technology-evidence.md#technological-items-fr-magic-010-fr-magic-011) already named 0x451850; the rows below are the re-read with the other four.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CN1 | NoOp (type 0, 0x452F00) | Returns at once (one byte). It touches no global and no target. | Original (kept) |
| CN2 | Terminate (type 19, 0x4529D0) | Sets the jump flag (0x5E7624) and the loop index (0x5E6D90) to the list's count (0x5E759C): unconditional, with no test of the target. The loop (0x4510F0) takes the index, adds 1 and tests it against the count, so the list of **this target** ends and the next target's list starts. It does not end the run and not the other targets. | Original (kept) |
| CN3 | ChargeNBranch (type 5, 0x451850) | Acts only when the run's source handle (+0x10, +0x14) is not 0. It calls the payment 0x450420 with the source, the component's **`Cost:`** (+0x40; no mastery halving, no dwarf factor: the run cost's `ceil(cost / 2)` is not applied), apply 1 and the run's spell (+4), so the record's `ChargesFromCells:` flag sends the payment to the cell source (TC1), an item source spends a charge and a critter pays fatigue. On a failed payment (answer 0) it sets the jump flag and the index to **`Branch:`** (+0x44); −1 gives the count (the list ends); a larger value than the count is capped at it. A successful payment, a cost of 0 or no source does nothing. | Original (kept) |
| CN4 | TestNBranch (type 20, 0x4529F0) | Acts only with a current target object (0x5E6D48 and 0x5E6D4C not both 0) whose cached type (0x5E75AC) is **0xF (PC) or 0x10 (NPC)**. It reads the object field named by +0x40 through 0x406CA0 (a 32-bit read; the object's 64-bit fields give their low word) and compares it with **`TestVal:`** (+0x48) by the index at +0x44: 0 equal, 1 `TestVal < field`, 2 `TestVal <= field`, 3 `field < TestVal`, 4 `field <= TestVal`. **A passing test returns** and the list goes on; **a failing one jumps to `Branch:`** (+0x4C) as CN3 does. An index above 4 logs `MTComponentTestNBranch_ProcFunc` (0x5B18C8) and then fails (it jumps). The installed fields: `art_num` 1, `Teleport_Tile` 242, `obj_f_critter_fleeing_from` 230, `HP_Damage` 29. | Original (kept) |
| CN5 | The jump (loop 0x4510F0) | After each component the loop tests the jump flag, clears it and takes the index from 0x5E6D90, then adds 1: a jump to **n** runs component **n + 1** next (a jump to −1 would run component 0, but −1 is stored as the count). Branch values below −1 are not data. | Original (kept) |
| CN6 | Use (type 24, 0x452ED0) | With a current target object (both words of 0x5E6D48/4C not 0; any object type, no cached-type test) it calls the script dispatcher 0x441980 with the target in all three object pairs: the triggerer (pair 1, parameters 1 and 2), the attachee (pair 2, parameters 3 and 4) **and** the extra object (pair 3, parameters 5 and 6), script point **1** (use), flag 0: the object runs its own use script as if it had used itself. A tile entry (no object) does nothing. The answer is dropped. | Original (kept) |
| CN7 | The dispatcher's use point (0x441980, as the Use component calls it) | A global gate (0x5E2F58 equal to 1) returns 1 and runs nothing. Before the script, **only for point 1**, it asks 0x4BBFE0 and then 0x4BBE40 with the triggerer pair and the attachee pair (the trap preamble: when the first answers 0 and the second does not, the dispatcher returns 0 and **no script runs**); then it reads the script slot of the attachee (field 0x20, the point), and **returns 1 without a run when the slot holds no script**. After the run, for an attachee of object type 0x11 (the trap type) whose slot has become empty it calls 0x43CCA0 on the attachee; a changed slot is written back (0x4F01D0), but the trap-type epilogue returns straight after 0x43CCA0 without that writeback. The trap preamble acts only for a triggerer of type 0xF or 0x10. Not taken (below): the trap preamble for an NPC triggerer (the target) and the trap-type epilogue. | Original (kept) |

**What this slice takes from these rows:** NoOp, Terminate, ChargeNBranch and TestNBranch in the cast host (`SessionSpellCasterControl`; the table of `SessionSpellCasterComponents`): the jump is the run system's (`SpellComponentOutcome.JumpTo`, `EndOfList`), the payment is the run's one (`SpellPaymentRules.Pay`, apply on, the component's cost, the run's spell, the cell source of the record's flag); TestNBranch reads the **hit-point damage** field (29: maximum hit points minus current, the life state's own). **Left, by name:** the other three test fields of TestNBranch (`art_num`, `Teleport_Tile`, `fleeing_from`: the component is logged not ready, as for any unmodelled field), the Use component (CN6, CN7: done by ARC-MAGIC-048 below), and a source the host cannot make a payer of (a critter with no life state: the component is logged not ready, where the original charges its fatigue).

**ARC-MAGIC-048 (Use, same readings CN6 and CN7):** the Use component is in the cast host (`SessionSpellCasterControl.UseComponent`): with a target object it asks the host seam `UseObject` (an object of the shown map, run through `AreaUseScriptHost.RunUseScript`, which takes **any** triggerer, here the object itself, under the use admission set, the slot kept as map changes) and logs `ObjectUsed` (1 when a script ran). A host without the seam leaves the component not ready; the PC and an object that is not a row of the shown map have no script (no run). **Left, by name:** the trap preamble of CN7 (a trapped target does not meet its trap here: `AreaTraps.Use` is the PC's) and the trap-type epilogue, a teleport the script asks for (the run stays unapplied, as for the examine and taking-damage runs), and an item held in a pack (its row is no row of the shown map: the item-use run `RunItemUse` is the PC's).

## Cast component (ARC-MAGIC-049)

Source: slice E4-e of the [technology audit](../technology-audit-res-125.md#6-queue) (ARC-RES-125; the magic plan's MT16 "nested casts"). Read on 2026-11-11 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`VERSION:1.0.7.4`; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`) by this task alone (F1): the component table 0x5B0BA8 (slot 4), the executor 0x451740 with its listing, the invocation init 0x455A20, the dispatcher 0x455AC0 and the run start 0x455C30, all from the Ghidra export. Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied. The checks of the invocation (0x4564E0) and the goal branch of the run start are the rows [IV1–IV9](#spell-invocation-arc-magic-006), RN4 and IT10 above.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NC1 | Cast (type 4, 0x451740), the invocation it builds | The component's `Spell:` (+0x40; 10000 when the key is absent) goes into an invocation made by 0x455A20 with a **null source handle**, so the invocation has no source and its parent, source location, target object, tile, other object, event mask and flags start empty. The component then writes: the target object (+0x70) = the **current target object** (0x5E6D48/4C); the parent (+0x40) = the **run's parent** (run +0x58, not the run's source); the target tile (+0xD0) = the **current target tile** (0x5E6D50/54); the flags (+0xDC) |= **0x1** (the cast without the casting goal); and, when the parent is not null, the invocation's source location (+0x38) = the **parent's location** (0x406DA0, type 2). With no tile and no target object it returns; with an object and no tile the tile becomes the object's location. It dispatches when an object or a tile is there. It reads no target type, no component target mask and no flag of the run: any target of the list is cast at. | Original (kept) |
| NC2 | the dispatcher (0x455AC0), the run start (0x455C30), as the component calls them | Single player: 0x455AC0 forwards to 0x455C30 (nothing while magic is off), which runs the invocation check (0x4564E0: the rows IV1–IV9 with the parent, the range from the parent's location, the line of fire, the forbidden flags, the turn-based test and the anti-teleport test; a refusal is silent except the anti-teleport message for the local player, IV9) and takes a slot. The run captures source handle 0, the parent, its start location (+0x50) = the invocation's +0x38 (**the parent's location**) and the target object and tile; its Free and Unresistable flags come from the invocation's flag bits 0x2 and 0x4 (not set here) and the record's `No_Resist:`. A source-less run pays no BEGIN cost and has no item, so it stacks and ends as any run does. With a parent and flag 0x1 the run gets **goal 73** (RN4, CG1, IT10); without a parent it gets its BEGIN as the event 2 ms later. | Original (kept; RN4, IT10 and their free-unpaid fix apply) |
| NC3 | the component's reach | The component has no depth, slot or re-entrancy guard of its own: a spell whose Cast component names itself starts one run per goal frame until the 512 slots are full (each nested run begins from its goal's release frame, never inside the component). The installed data holds the component only in technology record 203 (`Parent_Dmgs_Opponent_W_Item|Target_Attacker`, items such as weapon 6175: the opponent the item's wearer damaged, cast at as the parent's own explosion): its `Spell:` names record 205 (two Damage components, the second of fire, five to ten each, an EyeCandy pair and a Summon of the flash scenery with its Destroy on the callback). | Original (the unbounded chain is not taken: see below) |

**What this slice takes from these rows:** the Cast component in the cast host (`SessionSpellCasterNested`; the table of `SessionSpellCasterComponents`): the invocation of NC1 (no source; parent = the run's parent; the target object and tile, else the object's location; the parent's location as the invocation's source location, `SpellInvocation.SourceLocation`, so the BEGIN-area test's origin and the run's start location are the parent's; no casting goal), started through `StartSpellRun` with `SkipCastGoal` and played as the item executors' goal-73 runs are (`StartGoal`, then `Release`; a refused start frees the run unpaid, IT10's fix). **Differences, by name:** the session refuses a run start from inside the run rules, so the host starts the run **after the outer action or event pass**, in request order, with the objects' facts as the component saw them; the original starts it inside the component, so a run's slot number can differ. The chain is bounded at 64 starts per outer action (`MaxNestedCastsPerStart`; the rest is dropped and logged `NestedCastsDropped`): the original spreads it over frames and never ends. A target object the host has no facts for casts nothing, and a parent that has left the map is logged refused. The action points of goal 73 in a turn-based round are charged only when the host has its combat world (`CastCombat`, set by the viewer); without it the run is released at once. **Not changed:** nothing else of the weapons' hit path (the executor, MG46) is touched.

## Spell runs at the player's change of map (ARC-MAGIC-058)

Source: slice MT9b of the [magic implementation plan](../magic-implementation-plan.md) (missing-systems E4-h; the follower research [FM9](social.md#followers-across-maps-arc-res-076) and the persistence rows [PS26, PS28](../../architecture/original-persistence.md)). Read on 2026-11-12 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`) from the Ghidra text export (`research-private/ghidra-export/c/funcs/`: 0x44F7A0, 0x44FA70, 0x44FC30, 0x45C720 and the caller list of the three), by this task (reader one) and by the independent review of its diff (reader two, F2). Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied. Prose only.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MX1 | the teleport notification (0x45C720) | Called for the destination map's id. It first drops the queued events of types 1 and 5 (0x45BD70: the animation and magic-tech events, so every run's tick), then calls 0x44F7A0 with the destination map, then the animations (0x422B10) and the other events (0x45C7B0). The caller (0x4D3460) makes it only when the destination differs from the current map and the teleported object is a player character (PS26). | Original (this is Steamwoven's `CarryEvents` step, `IChangeOfPlaceHost.CarrySpellRuns`) |
| MX2 | spell runs at the teleport (0x44F7A0) | Opens the destination map's `MT.dat` and walks the 512 slots in order. For a slot in use (flag 0x1 of +0x13C) it tests the run's object handles against the travel list (0x4D3420, the same list PS26 names: the mover, its followers recursively and every inventory item): the source (+0x10), the parent (+0x58; it passes when it is the same handle as the source, or none, or on the list), the target (+0xA0), and every node of the two lists at +0x130 and +0x134 (the summoned list, SUM7). A handle that is zero passes. The other object (+0xE8) is not tested. A run that passes is written (0x44F3C0), counted and its slot freed (0x4558D0); any other run in use is ended by 0x457270 (state 2, its ticks removed, flag 0x40, processed at once). A run with no object at all therefore always goes. A write failure stops the walk. | Original (`SpellRunTravelRules`, `SessionSpellCaster.EndRunsLeavingMap`) |
| MX3 | the forced end (0x457270) | Unchanged from RP: END, ticks removed, forced-end flag, processed. The END actions run on the objects as they are while the hero is still on the old map. A forced end frees the run unless its END_CALLBACK components wait for an animation (the run then stays in the table in the End action). | Original (`SpellRunSystem.ForceEnd`, already built) |
| MX4 | spell runs at the map close (0x44FA70) | Writes every run in use to the left map's `MT.dat` with no test and no free. After MX2 the table holds only a run whose forced end is waiting for an END callback (MX3), which the file then keeps for the left map. | Original (not modelled: the table is global, so that run stays in it; its callback is delivered by the viewer's `AwaitedCallback` rule when its overlay is gone) |
| MX5 | spell runs at the map entry (0x44FC30) | Reads the entered map's `MT.dat` if it exists (and deletes it after reading): each record is read (0x44F620) into its own slot, or into a new one when the slot is in use (0x455740), and its next tick is queued again (0x459500). The carried runs lose their old tick events at MX1 and get a new one here. | Original (not modelled: a carried run keeps its tick event with its old deadline; the original's re-queued deadline is what 0x459500 computes, not read) |
| MX6 | scope | The step is made for the player character's change only. A non-player mover (an NPC sent to another map) goes through 0x4D3460 without it: the runs of an NPC stay in the table. | Original (Steamwoven moves the NPC's runs with it, ARC-DEV-114's departure) |

**What this slice takes from these rows:** the hero's change to another map ends, in slot order, every run in use that names an object that does not travel (`SpellRunTravelRules`; the cast host's summoned list is the second list; an item source travels with its holder, which the run knows as its parent; an item target is judged by the same parent, an approximation). Runs that travel stay in the session's table. Left, by name: the first list at +0x130 (unnamed, never written by the build), the re-queued deadline of a carried tick (MX5), the parked run of MX4's file, and the NPC side of MX6. This is the whole of the original's per-map store as far as the build can reach it: Steamwoven never holds runs of a map that is not shown.

## Tempus Fugit speed (ARC-MAGIC-056)

Source: slice MA-9 of the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#tempus-fugit-ma-9-arc-magic-056) (ARC-RES-103). The rules are XR6 and XR8 above, re-read on 2026-11-12 in the Ghidra text export (`FUN_004b0490` lines 16 to 29 and `FUN_00458a80`) before the code: the test is the local player's spell flags (`FUN_0040da50`, field 0x14) against 0x4000000, then +10 for a holder (the critter's own flag) and -10 otherwise. Owner decision of 2026-11-11 (bug rule of 2026-10-28): the original's player-only test is a bug of the game, fixed in both modes with no setting.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TF1 | stat getter 0x4B0490, player test 0x458A80 (speed, stat 11) | The change is made only while the **local player** holds `OSF_TEMPUS_FUGIT`; then a critter that holds it gains 10 and every other critter loses 10, before the crippled-leg -5 and the effects. An NPC's cast with the player not holding it changes nobody's speed. | Differs on purpose (owner decision, XR14 (a)): the change is made while **any** critter holds the spell. With the player holding it, the result is the original's; with only an NPC holding it, that NPC and every critter holding it gain 10 and every other critter, the player included, loses 10. The holders are the written spell flags of the session (the hero and the mobile NPCs), plus the data's own flags of the critter that is asked; a bit that only an NPC's map data stores starts nothing (it counts for that NPC once some written flag holds the spell). Not map-scoped, as the original's player flag is not. |
| TF2 | the three speed readers (XR8) | Turn-based action points, the real-time attack pause and the inventory window's line read the getter, so they carry the change; walking does not. | Original (kept): the shift reaches `CharacterStatContext.SpeedShift` of the hero (`HeroStatContext`, `QueryHeroAttack`, the combat round's hero stat) and of every NPC handed out by `WithNpcEffects` (the round's facts, the attack values, the spell caster's stat reads); the character sheet and the stats page show it through the same context, as the original's loops do. The stat range 1..100 clamps the result. |
| TF3 | follower join tail (0x45DF82) and removal (0x45E040 to 0x459EA0), XR7 | A critter that joins a leader holding the spell gets the flag; one that leaves loses it. | Built (ARC-MAGIC-1190), re-read 2026-11-12 in the text export (0x45DE90 tail, 0x45DFC0, 0x45E040, 0x459EA0): every join of the party (the checked join, the forced join, the unwait) writes the flag on the joiner when the hero's written flags hold it and the joiner's do not, inside the join's one revision; every leave (the leave, the loyalty step's leave, the wait timer's jilt) clears the leaver's written flag whatever the hero holds, leaving a cleared bit over the data. A wait is a leave too (the wait 0x4AA7A0 calls 0x45DFC0 and takes the leader back after), so a waiter loses its flag, in `WaitParty`, in the map change's wait orders and in the wait timer's jilt; every join first leaves an existing leader (0x45DFC0, so a re-join, an unwait or a re-append clears the flag) and then copies, so an unwait of a waiter copies again only while the hero holds it. ARC-WORLD-1840: the leader is any critter (an NPC leader's flag is read from the session's written flags), and a join under an NPC leaves the hero's party first. Limits: an entity the written flags do not cover (not the hero or a mobile `oid:` object) joins without a write; a full written-flags bound (4096) refuses a join that would need a new entry (`BudgetExceeded`, nothing changed); a bit that only the leaver's map data stores is not cleared (the session does not see it), and the familiar half of 0x459EA0 (the leader's `OSF_FAMILIAR`, XR10) stays with E1-e. The NPC sheet window's speed line also carries the shift (`AreaNpcSheet`, TF2). |
