Source: the open items of the [magic audit](../magic-audit-res-103.md#not-read-not-verified) (ARC-RES-103): whether Illuminate (65) lights the scene, whether Unseen Force (2) pushes non-critter objects and whether the weapon drop of Disarm (0) happens in a run. Read on 2026-11-12 in the 1.0.7.4 Ghidra text export (`Arcanum.exe` SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`): `FUN_004ccf70` (the eye candy add, its light branch), `FUN_004315b0`, `FUN_00431a40`, `FUN_00431b20`, `FUN_0043ee10`, `FUN_0043fb80`, `FUN_004cd940`, `FUN_004cd9c0`, `FUN_004243e0`, `FUN_004514e0`, `FUN_00434e80`, `FUN_0044d3b0`, `FUN_00424d90`, `FUN_004268f0`, `FUN_0042fa50`, `FUN_0042fd70`, `FUN_004b4390` (the object branch) and the weight test of `FUN_004f2d20`; the records come from the installed `rules\spelllist.mes` and `rules\SpellEyeCandy.mes` (read, no bytes kept). One reader (F1); the independent review of the PR reads the same functions again. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IW1 | record 65 and its eye candy entry (installed data) | Target mask any critter, cost 3, maintained, no stack; BEGIN `EyeCandy 3 Add`, END `EyeCandy 3 Remove`, no other component. Entry 3 of the spell's group (EC1: the "secondary destination" message) has an art, an additive blend, the flags overlay_f, animates and anim_forever, light art number 1 and a light colour (226, 60, 236). Of the 249 installed entries with a light, every one animates (none is lit and still) and 11 loop. | Original (installed data) |
| IW2 | eye candy add, light branch (0x4CCF70 after the parts; the art test 0x502220) | An entry whose light art is not −1 looks for the first free slot (art −1) among the **four overlay light slots** of the target (field 17; the loop's duplicate count compares an art handle the decompiler shows as the foreground art, so its exact test is unverified). None free: the add refuses like the parts' limit (GT90): in a run no run is built and nothing plays, in a static call the slots it just wrote are cleared. A free slot and a light art that loads (0x502220 answers 0): the slot goes to the call's output (the run's slot +0x88) and, when the call is not a run (+0x1C = 0), the light is written at once by 0x43EE10 with flags 0x20, the art and the colour. In a run (the cast's Add) the write is the begin's (IW3). | Original (F1) |
| IW3 | begin (0x4315B0), light slot setter (0x43EE10), first frame (0x43FB80), object lights (0x4D9590, LT1) | The begin answers true at once when the foreground, background and light slots are all −1, and ends the run when the foreground slot is −1 after the re-find; otherwise, with a light slot (+0x88), a light art (+0x98, asserted not 0, −1 means none) and a colour (+0x40) in the run, it calls 0x43EE10 twice on the target (first art −1: stops the old light's animation event and destroys its handle; then flags 0x20, the art, the colour) and then 0x43FB80 (the art set to its first frame when it has frames). 0x43EE10 writes the overlay light arrays 16, 17 and 18 (flags, art, colour), clears render flag 0x80000000 of the object and calls 0x4D9590(target, 1), the object-lights builder of LT1: a live light owned by the object, inserted into its sector's list. It follows the object when it moves (LT10), is drawn and sampled like any light (LT14; flag 0x20 only means "no light animation", LT6) and takes its own colour (LT15). | Original (F1; the re-find 0x4CD7A0 not read) |
| IW4 | end node (0x431B20, GT73), finish (0x431A40, reverse 0x4321C0), Remove (0x4CD940 → 0x4243E0 or 0x4CD9C0) | The end node and the finish write the same slot back as no light (0x43EE10 with flags 0, art −1, colour 0), which destroys the light (LT11). The Remove component of an animating entry (flag 0x20, Illuminate's) is 0x4CD940 → 0x4243E0: it finds the target's run with the same run id and spell art and interrupts it at level 6 (0x44E2C0), so the end nodes run (GS16) and the light goes. The Remove helper of a non-animating entry (0x4CD9C0) clears only the foreground, background and underlay entries; its light loop only looks (answers 1 or 0) and clears nothing, a reach no installed entry has (no entry is lit and still, IW1). | Original (F1) |
| IW5 | the answer | **Yes, Illuminate lights the scene in the original.** While the maintained spell runs, its looping eye candy keeps a live light on the target: light art class 9 number 1 (the art the carried light of LT12 also uses) in colour (226, 60, 236), owned by the target and sampled with the map's other lights. The END Remove ends it. A critter with all four overlay light slots in use gets no eye candy at all (IW2). | Original |
| IW6 | Steamwoven | **Built (ARC-MAGIC-1229).** The overlay host answers the light a lit eye candy keeps (`AreaEyeCandyHost.Lights`): one per begun, not ended run whose entry has a light art, in the first free of the owner's four overlay slots (the slots of its own record, `AreaLocalLighting.OverlaySlotMask`, and of the lights shown before it are taken, IW2), light art class 9 of the entry's number (frame 0, the carried light's own frame for number 1) in the entry's colour, flags 0x20. The light is no object light of the map and nothing of it is saved by itself (the overlay runs are, ARC-MAGIC-1280, which brings the light back after a load): the viewer stands it as a carried light where its object stands now (the hero at its placement, a map critter at its cell with its pose's shift, LT10, a created critter at its cell; `AreaEyeCandyLights`, `TerrainViewerEyeCandyLight.cs`), so the drawn glow and the darkness sampler of FR-LIGHT-011 (SC9) read the same light, and it goes when the run ends (the Remove interrupts at level 6, the end node and the finish end the run: IW4). A lit add on an owner with all four slots in use refuses like a full part list (IW2): no run, nothing plays. **The glow after a load (ARC-MAGIC-1280, built):** the overlay runs are saved ([saved runs](../../architecture/saved-runs.md#overlay-runs-arc-magic-1280)); the original writes the live light back into the object's fields at the save (LT11) and rebuilds it at the sector load (LT4), Steamwoven keeps the run and the host takes the restored runs again (`AreaEyeCandyHost.AdoptRestored`, each lit run takes its first free slot of the owner again, in table slot order). Not built: the object's own offsets and scale (the eye candy parts ignore them too) and a light art number whose frame the map's lighting did not load (number 1 is always loaded; no other number is played by an installed spell). | Built (viewer) |
| UO1 | records 2 and 133 (installed data) | Record 2: target mask "not in an inventory, not the caster" (any object type), cost 15, aggressive. BEGIN holds two pairs, each an AGoal component `anim_goal_knockback` (goal 43) with an `EyeCandy 2 Add`: one for a target that is a critter, one for a target that is an **item of weight below 5** (`Tgt_Obj_Weight_Below_5`, not the caster). Record 133 (Unseen Force (All)) has the same two pairs. | Original (installed data) |
| UO2 | spell area test (0x4F2D20, bit 0x4000000 of the low mask word; the record's name carries one more bit, 0x4) | Passes only an object whose type is 5..14 (every item type) and whose weight field (0x59) is below 5. Critters, portals, containers and scenery fail it; so a critter is pushed by the first pair only, and an item by the second only. | Original (F1; TA8 stands) |
| UO3 | AGoal component (0x4514E0, goal 0x2B branch), knockback starter (0x434E80), builder (0x44D3B0) | Takes the run's source object's tile (none: the context's own source tile, globals 0x5E6D40 and 0x5E6D44; if that is also 0, no push); the rotation is the target's art rotation plus 4 when the target stands on that tile, else the rotation from the source tile to the target's (0x4B8D50). The starter steps **four tiles** along it from the target's tile (a step off the map: no run), builds goal 43 on the target through 0x44D4E0 (the interrupt check of the goal's priority, GS12) with the destination tile and the source in its slots and starts it. **There is no test of the target's type or flags anywhere in the component or the starter**: an item gets the run an NPC gets. | Original (F1) |
| UO4 | slide (0x42FA50, 0x42FD70, GT60–GT66), damage step (0x4B4390, OD1–OD5) | The nodes read the object's location, offsets and art only. The art frame steps every sub-step for an art that is not a critter's (GT61), so an item's ground art cycles its frames; the block test is GT65's (terrain, walls and portals on the edge, living critters, blocking objects: an object blocks unless it carries OF_NO_BLOCK). A block puts the item back on its tile and builds the no-attacker damage context; the damage step then takes its object branch: an item is damaged like any non-critter object (no material reduction, only portals and containers have one, OD3), at 0 hit points the break (OD12) deletes it or marks it destroyed. What hit points the installed ground items carry (so whether a bump breaks them): a data question, not read. | Original (F1) |
| UO5 | the answer | **Yes: in the original Unseen Force pushes non-critter objects**: items lying on the ground (not in an inventory) with weight below 5, four tiles away from the caster, through the same run as a pushed NPC. Scenery, portals and containers are never pushed: every installed record with a goal-43 component (2, 12, 131, 132, 175, 176, 185, 201, 208, 219, 220) runs the object test and targets with the critter bit or the item-weight bit only (ARC-AI-2125 census; it also finds no goal-43 component in record 133, so UO1's number for the "All" variant is off, pinned by `SpellCensusRealDataTests`), so the guard-witness call of a pushed non-critter (GW10) never happens. | Original |
| UO6 | Steamwoven | **Built by ARC-MAGIC-1230 (issue #1230).** The target test is the spell area test (`SpellTargetRules`, the item type and weight below 5): the cast map now fills the weight of an item (the session's weight record for the object, else the seed's, `GameSessionOwner.QueryGroundItem`), where it stood at 0 and let every item pass. The knockback host pushes an item through an item body (`AreaKnockbackItems`, behind `AreaAiKnockbackBodies`): the goal 43 run and its round gating are an NPC's (GT61–GT66, no change), the block test is the AI host's terrain and critter test of a walker's step, the slide puts the item's ground cell on each tile (`GameSessionOwner.MoveGroundItem`, one revision a tile, no sub-tile offset: a tile a 35 ms tick), the bump is the no-attacker damage of a script (UO4) on the item's durability. An item stays a ground item whose cell is the push's: a map-data item keeps the cell as the object field `item.moved` (`SessionItemMove`), a hero- or script-placed item in its own placed record, an NPC-dropped item moves on from its drop cell; scenery, portals and containers are never pushed (the spell's target test names none) and an item in a pack, a holder, a junk pile or in flight is no ground item. | Built (F1 reads of UO1–UO4 and UO7–UO9) |
| UO7 | object move (0x43E770), flags (0x406CA0 on field 0x13), location write (0x406E10 → 0x408760), notification (0x444270), the save selection (0x410720, 0x410830, 0x410B20, 0x43D990) | **OF_DYNAMIC is not set by the move.** It reads the flags (a destroyed object, flag 1, is not moved), writes the location (field 2, the 64-bit word) and the two pixel offsets (fields 3 and 4) through the ordinary field writers, updates the sector lists when the sector changed, runs the notification (0x444270) and the object's two flag-driven updates (0x4D6210 for flag 0x8, 0x4D5570 for flag 0x200000; not read further). The only flag the notification writes is 0x20000, set or cleared by a location test (0x4D71A0, the object's own flags 0x4000000 excepted), never 0x2000; no flag is written by the move itself as far as read (the after-move call 0x4DD020 and several callees of the notification are unread). The field writer marks the object modified (its modified mark, OM10), so a **map-data item** that was pushed is saved as a difference record of the map's mobile file with its location (PS20, PS21), and a **dynamic** item (a hero's drop, a script's or a generator's) is saved whole in the dynamic file, where its new location is simply its stored location. 0x43D990 is true only for a wall, portal, scenery or trap without OF_DYNAMIC, so an item is never "static" whatever its flag. | Original (F1 for the function and the writers; F2 for the flag half: no write of 0x2000 in the function, the writers or the notification read) |
| UO8 | the same reads, Steamwoven's saved state | **No schema step.** The recorded place of a pushed map-data item is one more field of the object's directory entry (`item.moved`, a packed cell as `item.dropped`'s, in the snapshot's existing objects rows with the other mobile fields); the rows' form does not change, so the schema stays 91 and a save without it is read as before (the same course as the NPC standpoints, ARC-SCRIPT-1163). The placed items' own record already holds a cell. | Steamwoven (decision: the object field is open vocabulary, no section changes) |
| UO9 | the move and the damage step, item hit points | **The move touches no hit points.** Only a blocked step builds the no-attacker damage context of GT66 (UO4), and then the object branch of the damage step takes the normal damage (rolled 1..the line steps left; the fatigue half, drawn too, is no object's) through the item's stored resistance. The installed ground items the smoke found carry **1 hit point** (the durability model's maximum for them), so a bump breaks such an item with the break of OD12 (both arts take the destroyed bit; a second break deletes it), as Unseen Force against a wall does to a loose item. | Original (the amount path read; F2 for the hit points of the installed items: the smoke's item facts, `ai` stage 2026-11-12) |
| DW1 | Disarm (0): Damage component (0x4518D0), damage step (0x4B4390), record 0 | The original side is [XR15](#magic-audit-executable-reads-arc-dev-357) and stands unchanged: one Damage component, amount 0, `Weap_Dropped` (0x2000), the weapon block drops the **target's** weapon through 0x461A90, the zero amount is no exit. | Original |
| DW2 | Disarm in a run of Steamwoven | Before ARC-MAGIC-1232: the weapon drop was not built for a spell (`SpellDamageRules` turned the flag into the packet's hit effects, `CriticalHitEffects.DropWeapon`, and the damage step carried them, but the item drop was a session step, `CriticalDrop`, reached only through `CriticalAndWearSteps` of the hero's, the object and the NPC attack paths). **Built (ARC-MAGIC-1232):** the cast host's damage step (`SessionSpellCaster.ApplyDamage`), after the life step of a critter that was not already dead, runs the weapon block for any Damage component with `Weap_Dropped` (0x2000): `GameSessionOwner.SpellWeaponDrop` sends the **target's** slot 1004 item through the same `CriticalDrop` an attack's critical uses (CR14's drop place from the host's drop map, the drop script and the no-drop flag refuse, the hero's weapon joins its placed items, an NPC's seed item lies loose with its decay), whatever the amount (DW1: the zero amount is no exit) and whoever casts (a spell's step has no critical procedure: an NPC's Disarm drops a PC's weapon). A step the game stops (invulnerable, nigh-invulnerable, a taking-damage script that cancels) has no weapon block. The step carries the drop in `SessionSpellDamageStep.Criticals`; the viewer's floaters show message 19 from it (`AreaCombatFloaters.Of`); the cast host's log has `WeaponDropped` (the outcome) and `WeaponDropNotSaved`. A made save does not skip Disarm (the record has a damage component: a damaging record applies with a margin of at least 50, 0x4537B0), so the weapon drops when the step ran. NOT READY: the arm-hit drops of a crippling spell (the life outcome's `ArmHitDropsItems` is not read by the spell step), the hero's weapon when it is not a starting-kit item (the host lists only the kit's durability values, as for an acid), the PC's weapon auto-switch of CW3 after the drop. | Built (ARC-MAGIC-1232) |

**Implemented since:** DW2 by ARC-MAGIC-1232 (#1232), IW6 by ARC-MAGIC-1229 (the live light of Illuminate; the stage `eye-light`) and ARC-MAGIC-1280 (its glow after a load), UO6 by ARC-MAGIC-1230 (#1230: UO7–UO9 read there). **F0 facts raised:** none. **Left:** of IW6 the object's own offsets.

## Object draw flags and the hit eye candy (ARC-MAGIC-045)

Source: slice E4-a2 of the [technology audit](../technology-audit-res-125.md#6-queue), built on the R-1 rows [SFR1–SFR5](#readers-of-the-drunk-and-shielded-spell-flags-arc-res-126). Read on 2026-11-11 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`VERSION:1.0.7.4`; SHA-256 `9bd80511…8d52`) by this task alone (F1): the hit effect 0x459A30 with the eye candy set-up 0x4CCD20 and play 0x4CCF70, the draw-list builder 0x43B390, the blit set-up 0x442520 and 0x443170, the frame rectangle 0x443620, the test 0x458A80 and the stat test 0x4B0EE0, and the globals the builder reads (0x5E2F58, 0x5E2F88, 0x5E2EC8, 0x5A54AC). "Normal play" is the global 0x5E2F58 not equal to 1 (0x43A4D2 reads it: equal to 1, the map editor's mode, gives the masks 0 and 0; otherwise 3 and 0x100; 0x43AAB0 toggles another global, 0x5E2F28, and rewrites the two masks). Object flags are field 0x13.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HE1 | the hit eye candy (0x459A30, SFR3 filled in) | For the spell flag 0x400 (shielded) the run finder (0x459170) answers a run; the hit effect then sets up an eye candy play (0x4CCD20: the **spell eye candy table** `0x5E7568`, the object, the run's id as the cause, entry **6 × the run's spell + 5**; all play flags 0, no callback, no id restriction, scale and palette defaults; set-up word 1 afterwards) and plays it (0x4CCF70). No run found for 0x400 prints the module's assertion text and plays nothing. | Original (kept) |
| HE2 | magnetic inversion (0x400000) in the same function | The same run search and play with the run's spell entry (6 × spell + 5). **With no run found** the assertion text is printed and a fixed entry **0x449** (1097) is played with the cause −1 (no run). The order is 0x400, then 0x400000, then the entangled re-resistance (ER4). | Original (kept) |
| HE3 | the one prototype branch (0x459A30's tail) | When the struck object's prototype (0x49B290) is 0x6AE9 and the combat packet's field +0x5C is 0, the fixed entry **0x4EB** (1259) is played with the cause −1. The packet field's origin is not read. | Original (F0 for the packet field; not modelled) |
| HE4 | what a hit eye candy does not do | It changes no state and starts no run of its own (a plain eye candy play, the same function as the EyeCandy component's Add 0x451FE0 with play flags 0). The session's `SpellCastEventKind.EyeCandyAdded` with the detail 0 is the same presentation event. | Original (kept) |
| OD1 | the draw-list builder, the objects it takes (0x43B390) | In normal play an object whose flags hold destroyed (0x1) or off (0x2) is skipped whole (the mask 0x5E2F88 = 3). The **overlays** of an object with flag 0x10000 (the loop over its overlay arts, field 8) are built before the body's tests, so a don't-draw object still draws them (the scene plan's note, `AreaBuilder`). The **body** is built only when `(flags & 0x200) == 0` or the player sees invisible (OD2), **and** `flags & 0x100 == 0` (the second mask 0x5E2EC8 = 0x100 in normal play, 0 in the editor mode). So don't-draw hides the body, and invisible hides it from a player who cannot see invisible. | Original (kept) |
| OD2 | who sees invisible (0x458A80 with the mask 0x200, called once per frame by 0x43B390) | The local player (0x40DA50) holds the spell flag in its field 0x14, **or**, for the mask 0x200 only, the stat test 0x4B0EE0 (a PC or NPC; stat index 5, perception; the effective value 20 or more) answers yes: an extraordinary perception sees invisible. | Original (kept; the session's `SpellTargetStage.EnvironmentAllows` is the same rule for the environment test) |
| OD3 | the blit set-up of translucent and invisible (0x442520, then 0x443170) | Unless the blit flags (field 9) hold 0x10, 0x20 or 0x40 (an own mode: the early exit), an object whose flags hold 0x40 (translucent) **or** 0x200 (invisible) and no blit mode yet gets the blit alpha byte 0x80 and the constant-alpha blend mode: drawn at **alpha 128 of 255**. A seen invisible object is therefore translucent, and the invisible spell sets 0x40 on the local player (it never sets 0x200 on it, OB1), so the player draws translucent. | Original (kept) |
| OD4 | the shrunk frame (0x443620, the rectangle of an art frame; the builder also doubles the clipped source offsets, `<< 1`, for flag 0x80) | For an object whose flags hold 0x80 (shrunk) the frame's width, height and hotspot x and y are each **shifted right by one** (after the scale percent); the rectangle is the tile anchor minus the hotspot. So the body is half size about its hotspot. | Original (kept) |
| OD5 | the wading and other modes | Another blit mode (additive 0x10, multiply 0x40, constant alpha 0x100 of the data, wading) keeps its own; OD3's alpha is chosen only when none is set. The Trait component's elemental bodies set blit 0x10 (MG10) and so keep their additive blend. | Original (kept) |
| OD6 | the flags the spells write (OB1, OB2) | Invisible: OF_INVISIBLE 0x200 (not on the local player) and OF_TRANSLUCENT 0x40; shrunk: OF_SHRUNK 0x80; don't-draw 0x100 (Disintegrate, with paralysed). The data's own flags are handled by the scene plan at T0 (a data-shrunk body is placed half size; data translucent is refused there, `AREA_UNSUPPORTED_TRANSLUCENCY`; a data-invisible body is suppressed, `InvisibleNoDetectionObserver`). | Original (kept) |

**What this slice takes from these rows:**

- **Hit eye candy (HE1, HE2).** `SessionSpellCaster.CritterHit` (the cast host's hit effect) logs `EyeCandyAdded` for a critter with shielded (0x400) or magnetic inversion (0x400000): the first active run that targets the critter and whose record sets the flag (the run finder as modelled, ER6: the object lists are not) gives entry `6 × spell + 5` with the run as the cause; magnetic inversion with no run gives entry 0x449 with run −1; shielded with no run plays nothing. The viewer plays it through the existing eye candy host (`TakeEyeCandyEvent`). All of it before the entangled save, in the original's order. Not modelled: HE3 (the one prototype).
- **Draw (OD1–OD6).** `AreaBodyStyleRules` (Arcanum.World; `Drawn`, `ConstantAlpha`, `Shrink`) holds the rules; the scene plan's commands carry the frame's hotspot (`AreaDrawCommand.Hot`), and the canvas applies the style by body at the last step of a view (`SetAreaBodyStyles`): a hidden body is not drawn nor picked, a translucent or invisible-but-seen body draws at alpha 128 (unlit like any constant-alpha body, BL2), a shrunk body at half size about its hotspot. `TerrainViewerBodyStyles` computes the styles from the session's written object flags over the map data's (a change since T0 only), for the shown map's NPCs and the critters the session created there, and for the hero (translucent and don't-draw; the hero's shrink needs a hotspot its marker lacks: not applied).
- **Not done, by name:** the drunk flag (SFR2: nothing to build); the shielded and drunk bodies have no flag-specific draw (SFR5); the hero's shrink; a shrunk wading body (its wading rows are halved, nothing more); the eye candy and overlays of a hidden body keep their own place (they are other commands); **the overlays and eye candy of a shrunk body are not halved** (the builder passes the object flags to 0x443620 for those arts too, so the original halves them); an un-shrink (the data shrinks a body and a run clears 0x80: the body stays half size, `Delta`); a follower carried from another map (drawn as a moving command, not styled); a data constant-alpha body (blit 0x100) made translucent keeps the data's own alpha in the original (0x443170 reads field 0xB), ours gives 128.

## Spell component remainders (ARC-MAGIC-1146)

Source: the leftovers of the 0x44-0x45 triage ([ARC-RES-108](census-triage-0x44-0x45.md), issue #1146; the magic plan's MT15 and slices E4-c and E4-d). Read on 2026-11-12 in the 1.0.7.4 executable (`VERSION:1.0.7.4`) from the Ghidra export by this task alone (F1): the component table 0x5B0BA8 (slots 10, 17, 22, 23), the executors 0x452F10, 0x452800, 0x454920, 0x452D80 and 0x452E40 with the component parser 0x458060 (cases 0x16 and 0x17), the run lookup 0x459290 and its script action 0x4593F0, the Combat-Focus Wipe event 0x45ED70 and 0x45ECE0 with the death routine 0x45DA20 that queues it, and the decay cancel 0x45EC80 with its match test 0x45ECB0. Nothing was run in the emulator, patched or copied.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RM1 | EnvFlag (type 10, 0x452F10) | A 12-byte stub: it passes a constant string to the log writer 0x4FEB70 and returns. No object, field or flag is touched, whatever the component's data (the parser still reads the spell-flag word and the on or off word). No installed record has the component. | Original (kept: taken, no effect) |
| RM2 | Recharge (type 17, 0x452800 → 0x454920) | The thunk passes the current target object, the component's amount (+0x40) and `Max:` (+0x44). A client of a network game (0x4A2BA0 false: the host test fails) with the flag word 0x62AC6C bit 1 (value 2) clear returns 0 and does nothing; with it set it sends packet 0x4A first; then: a null object, or one whose object type (field 0x14F) is outside 5..14, answers 0 and does nothing. For an item it reads the spell charges (field 0x69); **when they are below the maximum** it writes the smaller of charges + amount and the maximum back and refreshes the interface (0x4605D0); it answers 1 for an item even when the charges already reach the maximum. A negative count (unlimited charges) is below any maximum, so the amount less one is written. The installed data holds it once: the Begin component of record 103 (amount, `Max:` 9999 by default). | Original (kept; the unlimited case and the packet are not built) |
| RM3 | TraitIdx (type 22, 0x452D80), parse (0x458060 case 0x16) | The parser stores the field (220 `obj_f_critter_stat_base_idx` or 31 `obj_f_resistance_idx`) at +0x44, the index at +0x40, **the positional value at +0x54**, and fixes +0x48 at 0 (the "flag"), the multiplier +0x4C at 1 and the divisor +0x50 at 1. The executor acts on a current target object whose cached type is 0xF or 0x10 (PC, NPC), reads the current value (field 220: the **stored** base stat through 0x4B0740, which returns the raw array entry for the stats below 8 and above 16 and derives 8 to 16 from the effective stats (the getter 0x4B0490 is the one that adds the effects; the first reading took 0x4B0740 for it), any other field the indexed read 0x4072D0), and with +0x48 at 0 (always) writes `current × 1 / 1 + value`: the data value is an **addend**. Field 220 goes through the base stat set 0x4B0980 (clamped to the stat's bounds 0x4B0FC0 and 0x4B0FD0; a derived stat 8 to 16 is not written), any other field through the indexed write 0x407340. So a base stat takes its stored value plus the value (effects not included), and the resistance field the same. No installed record has the component. | Original; built for an NPC in ARC-MAGIC-1295 (resistances; the stats 1, 2, 3, 5, 7) and ARC-MAGIC-1476 (RM3b: the stats 4, 6, 17 to 21, 25) and ARC-MAGIC-1507 (RM3c: the stats 22 and 23 for an NPC, its worn items' wield scripts run since ARC-MAGIC-1644) and ARC-MAGIC-1508 (stat 24, poison: the life state's level, see RM3b) and ARC-MAGIC-1509 (RM3b: gender and race), the rest not ready |
| RM3b | base stat setter 0x4B0980 per stat (ARC-MAGIC-1476), range tables 0x5B5194 (minimum) and 0x5B5204 (maximum) with 0x4B0FC0 and 0x4B0FD0, case table 0x4B0E74, guards 0x4C6B50, 0x4B1B90, 0x4B02B0, 0x4B1C00, callbacks 0x460860 and 0x4601C0 | A type outside 15 and 16 or an index outside 0..27 does nothing; 8 to 16 return the derived value unwritten. The value is clamped to its range (minimum table: 1 for 0 to 7, 0 for 17 and above except alignment -1000 and stat 25 at 20; maximum: 20 for the primary stats, with the race's raised maxima of 0x4B0FD0, 51 level, 2,000,000,000 experience, 1000 alignment, 100 fate points, 56 unspent points, 210 magick and tech points, 1000 poison and stat 25 (minimum 20), 1 gender, 11 race). A lowering (the clamped value under the stored one) asks the key-stat guard 0x4C6B50 first; then for intelligence (4) the spell guard 0x4B1B90 (every known rank, college number × 5 + rank, needs 5) and the degree guard 0x4B02B0 (the eight tech degrees' intelligence minima), for willpower (6) the spell guard 0x4B1C00 (the spell's willpower minimum); a refusal returns the old base and writes nothing. The case table 0x4B0E74 sends strength to the hit-point step (0x45F790 is the encumbrance level, the recalculation 0x45F820 after the write), poison to the "constitution 20 or more sets it to 0" step (0x4B0EE0), gender to the removal of the old gender effect (0x4EA2E0, cause 9) and race to that of the race effect (cause 0); then the store; then by stat: strength 0x45F820 (which also updates the animation speed 0x436FA0), intelligence the interface callback 0x4604E0 for the hero only, 0x12 the level-up 0x4A69C0 **for a PC only** (type test at its start) and the callback 0x460860, alignment (PC whose value changed: each follower gets check-leader and the too-good or too-bad bit, OL17) then the callback 0x4601C0 (falls through to fate points), magick and tech points the rewield 0x465530 (each worn slot 1000 to 1008 runs its item's scripts, points 25 and 24), poison the poison timer 0x4B1350 for a positive level and 0x460240, gender 0 adds effect 0x14A with cause 9 and race adds effect 0x40 + race with cause 0. Level, unspent points and stat 25 have no case (fate points are the label 0x14 that alignment falls through into: only the callback). So for an NPC level, experience, alignment, fate points, unspent points and stat 25 are a clamp and a store (the callbacks are interface refreshes); intelligence and willpower add the spell and degree guards; the rest has consequences the session does not model for an NPC yet. | Original; the stats 4, 6, 17 to 21, 25 built for an NPC in ARC-MAGIC-1476 (`SessionSpellCaster.TraitIndexComponent`, `CharacterStatRules.StoredRange`), magick and tech points in ARC-MAGIC-1507 (RM3c); strength built for an NPC the map's seed lists in ARC-MAGIC-1506 (`GameSessionOwner.QueryNpcCarry`, `RecalculateNpcEncumbrance`: the level before and after the store, the recalculation only on a change, entries of effect 158 with the item cause; the level message is the local PC's, the animation speed the host's), a new level from 4 sets the NPC's fatigue timer in ARC-MAGIC-1568 (0x45E820 mode 1 as the kind `life.npc.encumbrance`, drained by the timer 0x45E610 and set again by the level the fire reads from the seed and the stored strength; a pass world without the stored stats ends the chain after one drain), but not ready when that timer cannot be scheduled, for an NPC the seed does not list and without the rule of effect 158; poison built in ARC-MAGIC-1508 (stat 24 is the life state's level: current = the life state's poison, the sum wraps in 32 bits, clamp 0 to 1000, 0 for an effective constitution of 20 or more as 0x4B0EE0 called with constitution, written through the life step so the poison timers 0x4B1350 are set for a positive level; no death check, the interface callback 0x460240 is not the session's; `SessionSpellCaster.PoisonStat`, `LifeRules.SetPoison`, `GameSessionOwner.SetPoisonLevel`); differs: the original schedules the poison events for every positive level written, the session on a change of the level (a level already positive with no pending timer and left unchanged by the write starts no tick; the event queue document names the same difference); gender and race built for an NPC in ARC-MAGIC-1509 (`GameSessionOwner.AttachInnateEffects`, `SessionSpellCaster.TraitIndexComponent`: before the store the first entry of the race cause (0) or gender cause (9) leaves the NPC's list, whatever the stored value and whether or not the value changes, and after it race adds the effect 64 + race with the race cause and gender 0 adds effect 330 with the gender cause, as the setter's two cases do; the effect rules are the host's from the effect tables, and without the rule of the effect to add nothing is written; the setter's clamp of race (maximum 11) reaches a race the character rules do not have, so a store of 11 is not ready, and stays so by decision (ARC-MAGIC-1631, exe read 0x4B0980, 0x4B0FD0, 0x4C1500: the setter would store 11, clamp its stats by the default maximum table, 20, and add effect 75 with the race cause (the installed `rules\effect.mes` defines it, read in this task: strength −1, beauty +1); but the races are eleven (0 to 10) and the race-keyed tables read for this task have eleven rows with no bound check (the other race tables were not read): the reaction table 0x5B684C (88 entries) is indexed by the NPC's race times 8 plus the PC's race 0 to 7, and its row 11 starts at 0x5B69AC, a text string and pointers read as reaction terms, a latent out-of-bounds read of the original like LM12; no installed spell has the component, so no installed record reaches it); the race change takes the race's primary-stat ranges at the next TraitIdx stat store, 0x4B0FD0); an advancement-record follower and the hero built in ARC-MAGIC-1510 (summary below); the hero's magick and tech points (the state's stats 8 and 9, stored and then the rewield 0x465530 runs the worn items' wield scripts 25 and 24 over them, ARC-MAGIC-1744; the hero's light is derived from its wear), its poison (the life state's level, as an NPC's) and its experience with the recalculation (the setter's case 0x12 only: the experience is stored, then 0x4A69C0 runs over the stored value as the award's does without the difficulty, the experience effects and the dead-hero test; its award waits for the host's level-up messages and followers' step; the level has no case and is stored alone) and a record follower's gender (no sheet field: the stored field and the innate effect) and race (the sheet's and the stored field; not ready when the sheet would no longer be valid) built in ARC-MAGIC-1628; the hero's resistance array (field 31) and age (stat 25) saved in ARC-MAGIC-1658 (schema 99: `SessionHeroState.Resistances`, `Age`, the sum unclamped as the indexed write stores it; stat 25 clamped to 20..1000 and stored; every resistance reader of the hero lays the worn armour and the effects over the stored array) and its gender and race (the removal of the old innate effect of the cause, the store, the add of 0x40 + race or 0x14A: `GameSessionOwner.WriteHeroInnate`, the character owner built again from the new state with the durable entries applied to it again and the damage moved by each step as an effect's is; the stat effects and basic and tech skill changes of the attached rules are modelled, as at creation; not ready: an age not read (a save of schema 98 or before whose prototype tables do not read, ARC-MAGIC-1724), race 11, no attached rule for the effect to add, a stored stat the new race's ranges do not admit, a durable effect or blessing of the new innate effect's number); ARC-MAGIC-1700 (leftovers of 1658): the resistance changes of the hero's innate effects are read from the attached rules at each question (`GameSessionOwner.HeroEffectResistances`: the innate entries first, then the durable ones; the sheet, the doll, the attack's combatant, the spell save and the life facts' normal resistance the poison tick meets; a swap's trial reads the state it ends with), as the combat getter 0x43D6D0 reads the whole effect list; **recorded limits**: (a) an age the session has not read (a save upgraded from schema 98 or before; a new or pre-made hero's age is read at creation) was built in ARC-MAGIC-1724: the age could not have been changed before schema 99 (stat 25 was not ready for the hero), so it is still the prototype's, and the load path of the viewer stores it from the hero's own tables (`AreaQuickSave.FillHeroAge` over `WriteHeroAge`, the tables of the prototype the hero holds: a pre-made or new-character hero; nothing is stored for another prototype, tables that do not read or a prototype without an age, and stat 25 then stays not ready for that hero, as before), (b) race 11 stays not ready by the decision above (ARC-MAGIC-1631: the installed data has an effect 75, but the character rules have no race 11: its stat ranges, portraits and sheet, and the race-keyed tables of the original have no row for it, a latent out-of-bounds read; no installed record has the component, so none reaches it), (c) the innate list's display order stays a limit by decision (ARC-MAGIC-1724: the original removes the race entry and appends the new one after the gender entry, so a woman's list reads gender, race; the session derives race, gender from the state and saves no order; no reader depends on it, and a saved order bit is a schema step for a display order alone, which would have to be the last bump of the schema and merge after the other schema work; it is built when a reader needs the order), (d) an innate effect's age change (stat 25 add) is not modelled for the hero (ARC-MAGIC-1723: the effective age is no session value, the stat setter reads the stored age (RM3: effects not included) and the character sheet shows no age line, so no reader exists); ARC-MAGIC-1723 reads the special values of the innate effects like the durable ones (exe read of the special getters 0x4EAA20 maximum hit points, 0x4EAA50 maximum fatigue, 0x4EAA80 reaction, 0x4EAAB0 bad (value below 0) and good (above 0) reaction and 0x4EABC0 experience gain: each calls the aggregation 0x4EA6C0 with the "ignore innate" flag 0, so the whole list is read, the innate entries (cause 0 race, 9 gender) first; only the stat form 0x4EA930 skips them): `GameSessionOwner.HeroInnateRules` feeds the life facts' maxima, the reaction facts, the advancement modifiers and the experience award, a swap's removal and add each move the damage by the change of the maxima (including an entry that carries only specials) and the bounds of the durable adds and of a swap hold over the innate entries (`EffectDomain.Bounded`; `AttachInnateEffects` refuses rules that would carry the hero's durable entries past them); the hero's creation scheme (it runs before the host attaches the rules) and the load-time check of the saved durable list read the durable entries alone; the stat, basic and tech skill, resistance and special changes are modelled |
| RM3c | rewield 0x465530 (setter cases 0x16 and 0x17 of 0x4B0980), light 0x467E80, worn-slot getter 0x4649C0, script run 0x441980 (ARC-MAGIC-1507) | Magick points (22) and tech points (23) are stored after the clamp 0 to 210 (the lowering check 0x4C6B50 runs for a lowering, but no skill is keyed on 22 or 23, so it always passes) and then the setter calls the rewield for any holder, hero or NPC, whether or not the value changed. The rewield walks the wear slots 1000 to 1008 (0x4649C0 finds the item whose wear slot is the number); for each worn item it sets a global (0x5E87E8) and calls the light recalculation 0x467E80 for the holder, which ORs the light-size bits (item flags 0x200 to 0x1000) of every worn item shifted by the item's power ratio against the holder's aptitude (0x461540: 75 or less one size, 50 or less two, 25 or less three) and keeps the largest size in the holder's light bits, redrawing the carried light, then runs the item's script point 25 (wield off) and then 24 (wield on) with the holder as the initiator (0x441980); an item without a script on the point does nothing. So for an NPC the write is a store plus its worn items' light recalculation and wield scripts. | Original; built for an NPC in ARC-MAGIC-1507 when the session lists the NPC and none of its worn items has script point 24 or 25 (`SessionSpellCaster.RewieldReady`): the runs are then empty and the carried light stays as saved, which is exact for an NPC wearing no light item (0x467E80 returns at once for an item without light bits); the carried light of an NPC wearing a light item is recalculated by ARC-MAGIC-1561 (RM3d). With a worn wield script, or an NPC the session does not list, the component is not ready and nothing is written. ARC-MAGIC-1644 builds the wield scripts for an NPC wearer: the stat is stored after the clamp, and the item event runs of the worn items are planned first over the NPC's stored stats with the new value laid in (the rewield reads the stored points), and applied after the write: for each worn item in slot order wield off (25) and then wield on (24), the item as the attachee and the NPC as the triggerer, the condition "items are being rewielded" true (`GameSessionOwner.PlanNpcRewield`, `ItemScriptRuns` with an NPC wearer, `SessionSpellCaster.ItemScripts` for the host). The runs read the NPC's stats (get stat: the primary stats and the aptitude as the character owner derives them from the stored values and the effect list the earlier runs left, the level and alignment stored) and the power ratio against its aptitude, and add and remove effects on its list (cause item, `NpcEffectOp` sequence through `ChangeNpcOps`, one dry run before anything is written). Not ready, nothing written: an NPC the session does not list, a worn wield script without a script host, and a run the session cannot apply (the admitted script set, an effect action on another object, an effect rule the host does not know, a stat the session does not hold, a read of the aptitude or a stat that an effect change of the same run would have moved, a list the life rules refuse). ARC-MAGIC-1745: the printed lines of the runs are read for the NPC as the triggerer (`PrintedLineForNpc`, as for the NPC's best-items wield) and go to the cast log (`SpellCastEventKind.ItemScriptLine`) after the write, in print order, and the viewer posts them to the message window as any script's printed line (a line the NPC reading cannot give, such as an alternate text or `@npcname@`, makes the rewield not ready and nothing is written, as for the NPC's best-items wield). ARC-MAGIC-1744 builds the same for the hero as the wearer: the hero's stored stats (the state's array with the new magick or tech points laid in) are the runs' reads (`GameSessionOwner.PlanHeroRewield`, `ItemScriptRuns.HeroStored`: the aptitude and stat reads go through a trial character owner built over the laid array with the effect list the earlier runs left), the worn items come from the hero's wear in slot order with the script points they were worn with, the hero's effect list changes as for an equip's runs (the steps of `PrepareEffects`, one dry run before anything is written), and the plan is applied after `WriteHeroStat` by `CommitRewield`, its printed lines going to the message window as an NPC's do; not ready, nothing written, for a worn wield script without a script host and for a run the session cannot apply, as for an NPC. ARC-MAGIC-1830: the eye candy of the runs (action 120, WE4/WE5) is played as any script's is ([#1592](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/1592)): the runs' requests (`ItemScriptRuns.EyeCandies`) go with the committed rewield to the host, which plays the ones on a drawn object. Differs: a request on the worn item (no cell to draw on, [#1829](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/1829)) plays nothing, and the light recalculation is the host's for an NPC (RM3d) and derived from the wear for the hero (magick and tech points move no maximum the life rules read). ARC-MAGIC-1684 builds the NPC best-items wield (IE6 for an NPC, `PlanNpcWield`, [equipment runtime](../../world/equipment-runtime.md#npc-best-items-with-item-scripts-arc-magic-1684)); the NPC's barter, give, take and loot paths still run no item event scripts (a worn scripted item stays on, #1743) |
| RM3d | NPC carried light after the rewield and the wield hooks (ARC-MAGIC-1561): 0x467E80, light fields 0x45FA70 and 0x43EBD0, the callers 0x465530, 0x4677B0 and 0x467CB0 | 0x467E80 takes the item (or none) and the holder. With an item that has no light bits (item flags 0x1E00) it returns at once, so a wield or unwield of an item without light flags leaves the light as saved. Otherwise it ORs, over the worn slots 1000 to 1008, each worn item's light bits shifted right by 3 when its power ratio against the holder's aptitude (0x461540) is under 26, by 2 under 51, by 1 under 76, and keeps the largest size: it clears the holder's size bits (field 0xDA, mask 0x780000) and sets one of 0x400000 (extra large), 0x200000 (large), 0x100000 (medium) or 0x80000 (small), then 0x45FA70 gives the light art (art 1 and the colour 244 255 255, 255 214 172, 236 138 85 or 167 88 58 by size; art -1 and colour 0 for no size) and 0x43EBD0 writes light flags 0, that art and colour into the object's fields 13, 14 and 15 (freeing the old light art first) and redraws it. No light bit left after the shifts therefore clears the light. The rewield calls it for each worn item in slot order, so every worn light item gives the same result. | Original; built in ARC-MAGIC-1561 for an NPC the session recalculated: `AreaRestockHost.NpcLight` (the size of `EquipmentRules.Light` over the NPC's worn items and its aptitude; the viewer draws it as the NPC's carried light, `TerrainViewerApp.NpcCarriedLights`). The NPC counts as recalculated when the session dressed it (the regeneration's and the AI gate's best-items wield record) or wrote its magick or tech points (TraitIdx, stat 22 or 23), and the result applies only when an item with light flags is worn now or was worn at load (a light of no worn item stays as saved); an NPC the session never touched keeps its saved light. Differs: the original stores the size it computed at the event, Steamwoven derives it at the question with the aptitude of the moment (the hero's light does the same); the other callers of 0x467E80 (an NPC that a barter, a give or a loot unwields or wields an item for) are not built. |
| RM4 | Trait64 (type 23, 0x452E40), parse (0x458060 case 0x17) | The parser stores the field word at +0x40 (the Trait table: art number 1, teleport tile 242, fleeing from 230, hit-point damage 29) and the source word at +0x44 (0 `Target_Tile`, 1 `Caster_Obj`, 2 `Caster_Owner_Obj`). The executor does no test of the target. Source 0: the run's target tile (0x5E6D50 and 0x5E6D54) goes into the target's 64-bit field (0x406E10), then the target's field 243 (the **teleport map**) is set to the current map (0x40FF40 reads 0x5D1210). Source 1 or 2: the run's +0x58 and +0x5C (the **parent** handle) are copied into two globals and written into the field with the handle write 0x406F20 (a null handle clears it); **both sources read the same run field**, the owner is never looked up. No installed record has the component. | Original |
| RM5 | run of a spell on an object (0x459290) | Over the 512 run slots in order, the first run that is active (flag 0x1), is of the given spell and holds the object as its **target object**, in its **run list** (+0x130) or in its **summoned list** (+0x134) (the lists walked head to tail); it answers 1 and writes the slot to its out parameter, else it answers 0 and writes -1. A null object (both words 0) finds none. | Original (kept) |
| RM6 | script action 130, stop a spell on an object (0x4593F0) | Calls 0x459290 and, when it found a run, the interrupt 0x457110; answers 1 if one was found, else 0. Only the first run is stopped. The script action is entry 130 of the action dispatcher (0x447220, call at 0x44A895): operand 0 is read as a value (the spell), operand 1 as the first object of its list; with an object it calls 0x4593F0 and the answer is dropped, without one nothing happens (the run goes on with the next line either way). Read again for ARC-SCRIPT-1294 in the decompiled export. | Original (the lookup, the interrupt and the script action are kept; the action is in set S42, ARC-SCRIPT-1294) |
| RM7 | the Combat-Focus Wipe event (kind 26; schedule 0x45ED70, handler 0x45ECE0; the death routine 0x45DA20) | The death routine queues it for a victim of object type 0x10 (NPC) **before** it writes the focus (field 0x11B) := the killer, whatever the killer is (and whatever the danger state); the one-day body event 0x45EBE0 is a different event queued in the common tail (this settles the open point of [DY3 and DY6](scripts.md): **0x45ED70 is not the body decay**). 0x45ED70 refuses a null object and a prototype (0x405BC0) and queues kind 26 after 600,000 game ms with the object and the game time. The handler takes a **dead** object (0x45D8D0: null or hit points below 1) with a **non-zero focus**, computes the tile distance to it (0x441AE0 → 0x4B96F0: the larger of the two coordinate differences of the locations, with no map test); a distance **below 30** queues the event again (600,000 ms), otherwise the focus is cleared (a null handle is written). A living object or one with no focus ends the event with no new one (it answers 1). The event kind has no uniqueness key. | Original |
| RM8 | the decay cancel (0x45EC80, match 0x45ECB0) | Removes, through the queue's removal 0x45BFF0, the first pending event of kind 0x18 (Decay Dead Bodies) whose object matches the given one in each of the two pending lists (0x5E7638 and 0x5E7E14), running the kind's removal callback, and **always answers true** (0x45BFF0 answers 1 for any kind up to 0x22). Its callers (read in the census triage): before a body is destroyed, when a body is healed back to life, and by the F11 cheat. | Original (kept) |

**What this slice takes from these rows:**

- **EnvFlag (RM1)** is in the table of `SessionSpellCasterComponents` with no effect and no "not ready" line (`EnvFlagComponent`).
- **Recharge (RM2)** is `SessionSpellCaster.RechargeComponent`: an item target (type 5..14) the host knows gains the amount up to `Max:` through `GameSessionOwner.RechargeItemCharges`, which keeps the count in the session's saved charge list (`durability.charges`, the one a spent charge uses, so no schema step). A critter, a container, scenery and a tile are left (answer 0 in the original); an item the host does not read is not ready. The session count is bounded 0..1,000,000: a negative (unlimited) count or a sum outside it is refused (`ItemRechargeNotSaved`), the original's "amount less one" for an unlimited item is not built. A recorded count marks the item magical when the host reads it (the original writes the field only); the packet and the interface refresh are not built. The log line `ItemRecharged` carries the new count.
- **Trait64 (RM4)** is `SessionSpellCaster.Trait64Component` for an NPC target: source 0 with the teleport tile field (242) writes the target tile as the NPC's `npc.teleport.tile` (the packed location of `SessionNpcStandpoint`) and the open map as its teleport map (`SetTeleportTile`, one revision); source 1 or 2 with the fleeing-from field (230) writes the run's parent (none clears) as the danger record's fleeing-from outright (`WriteFleeingFrom`: no setter, no stop-flee step). Any other field and source, the hero, a non-NPC target and a parent that is no critter are not ready; no installed spell uses the component and nothing reads the teleport tile yet (the `Teleport_Tile` test field of TestNBranch is not read, as before).
- **TraitIdx (RM3, RM3b)** is built in ARC-MAGIC-1295 and ARC-MAGIC-1476 (`SessionSpellCaster.TraitIndexComponent`, [cast host](../../world/spell-cast-host.md#remaining-components-the-spell-stop-and-the-focus-wipe-arc-magic-1146)): a resistance of an NPC and its stats 1 to 7 and 17 to 25 (ARC-MAGIC-1507: 22 and 23 when no worn item has a wield script, with the carried light recalculated by ARC-MAGIC-1561; ARC-MAGIC-1506: strength, below an encumbrance level of 4); strength for a level from 4 up, the points of an NPC wearing a wield script item run its rewield scripts (ARC-MAGIC-1644, RM3c). ARC-MAGIC-1510: a follower with an advancement record runs (its sheet gives the current value, the skills and the degrees of the guards and takes the write, with the indexed stat beside it) and so does the hero for the primary stats 0 to 7, alignment, fate points and unspent points; ARC-MAGIC-1628 adds the hero's level (stored alone), experience with the recalculation 0x4A69C0, poison and its magick and tech points (with the worn items' wield scripts run by the rewield, ARC-MAGIC-1744), and a record follower's gender and race; ARC-MAGIC-1658 adds the hero's resistance, stat 25, gender and race (schema 99) (ARC-MAGIC-1508: poison is built, see RM3b; ARC-MAGIC-1509: gender and race are built, race 11 not ready).
- **The spell stop (RM5, RM6)** is `SessionSpellCaster.FindRunOnObject` and `StopSpellOnObject`: the first active run of the spell (slot order) on the object as target, in its object list or in the summoned list is interrupted through the run rules; the answer is whether one was found. The script action is built in ARC-SCRIPT-1294: action 130 of set S42 hands the spell and the object to the AI host, which calls `StopSpellOnObject` for an object of the shown map or the PC (the PC as the run rules' `pc`). ARC-SCRIPT-1576: the script host's name of a mobile row without a session entity (`oid:` and the digest) is not the cast host's id (`row:Mobile:N`), so the AI host translates it first (`SessionSpellCaster.CastIdOf`); sector objects and session entities are named alike.
- **The Combat-Focus Wipe event (RM7)** is the session event `combat.focus.wipe` (`SessionEventKinds.FocusWipe`; saved, game clock, scoped to the NPC's map, many per NPC): `GameSessionOwner.ScheduleFocusWipe` from the life step that kills an NPC (before the focus write, only when the dying script allowed the death), `FireFocusWipe` as the handler. The focus is the danger record's focus; the dead test is the life record's; the cells come from the pass world's `ISessionBodyWorld` (the NPC's, a critter focus's) and the actor's cell for the hero (any map, as the original compares raw locations). A focus that the world cannot place counts as far away (the stale reference goes); an NPC the world cannot place, or a pass world without cells, keeps the event (it is queued again; the original always reads a location). The queue's bound (`MaxFocusWipes`) is the session's.
- **The decay cancel (RM8)** is called by the life step that revives an NPC (`LifeEvents.Resurrected`): `CancelBodyDecay`, as for a destroyed created critter before.

## AI redirect and the NPC summoner's side (ARC-MAGIC-1169)

Slice E1-f of the [missing-systems plan](../../project/missing-systems-plan.md): the AIRedirect component of the cast host and the faction and alignment an NPC summoner gives its summon. Read on 2026-11-14 in the text export (`research-private/ghidra-export`, functions 0x450C40, 0x451700, 0x4A9530, 0x4A9560, 0x4A94C0, 0x452830, 0x463630) and the listing of 0x450C40; F1, one reader. The AI side of an NPC's summon (the summon list of the chooser, the target and the cast) was already built ([AI support](../../world/ai-support.md), [AI host casting](../../world/ai-host.md#casting-arc-world-102)); this slice checks that an NPC summoner is a legal parent of the Summon pipeline and builds the two things left.

| # | Address | Fact | Class |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AR1 | component 0x451700 (table entry 3, DS1) | The component builds the redirect record with 0x4A9530 from the component's target object (0x5E6D48): the target is both the one the NPCs are turned on (first handle) and the centre of the search (second handle); the record's last two words are the component's data: `MinIQ:` (+0x44, −1 when the key is absent) and the critter-flag bit (+0x40, one bit; 0 any), and 0x4A9560 runs it. A component with no target object does nothing (the record's first handle is tested before anything). | Original (F1) |
| AR2 | 0x4A9560 | The vicinity is 0x440FC0 with the type mask 0x18000 (PCs and NPCs) around the centre; each candidate is skipped when it is dead (0x45D8D0), is the target, or when `MinIQ:` is 1 or more and **the target's** intelligence (stat 4) is above it (never the candidate's: the Original setting of P3), or when the mask is not 0 and the candidate has none of its critter flags (field 0xDA). Each candidate left goes to 0x4A94C0. `MinIQ:` is absent in every installed record (the census), so P3 changes nothing installed. | Original (F1; P3 stands) |
| AR3 | 0x4A94C0 | For a candidate that is an NPC (type 16) other than the attacker: the attack event 0x4A9650 with the target as attacker, the candidate as victim, loudness 0 and no flags, then the candidate's fields 0x11B (combat focus) and 0x11C (who-hit-me-last) are written with the target (plain writes, whatever the danger state). A PC candidate is left alone. | Original (F1) |
| AR4 | callers | The only installed records with an AIRedirect component are the BEGIN and MAINTAIN lines of Animal Lure (spell 183, critter flag animal, no `MinIQ:`): the summoned animal (list 0) draws the animals near it to attack it, once at the BEGIN and at every maintenance. **Built by ARC-MAGIC-1169:** `SessionSpellCaster.AiRedirectComponent` (table entry `AIRedirect`) asks the social host (`ISpellSocialHost.Redirect`), the AI host's `SpellRedirect` runs `AiGroundRules.Redirect` over `Square(target, ScrWorld.VicinityReach)` (the fixed 10-tile play view, the same rectangle as the script lists, PG3), then the host's attack event (`Loudness.Silent`) and `GameSessionOwner.RedirectDanger` for each NPC. | Original (F1); Steamwoven build |
| SUM23 | 0x450C40, after the object's creation (0x450E6C and on), SUM5 applied to an NPC summoner | The parent (the run's +0x58) is read by the effect, not the source: only when `Clear_Faction:` is absent (the effect's branch on the component's flag): the parent's alignment (stat 19, 0x4B0490/0x4B0980) is copied to the summoned whoever the parent is (PC or NPC; the copy sits behind the single-player or host gate `(0x62AC6C & 1) == 0 || (0x62AC6C & 2) != 0`, always true here) and, when the parent is an NPC, the summoned's field 292 (faction) becomes the parent's; with `Clear_Faction:` neither is copied and the faction is set to 0. A PC parent leaves the prototype's faction. The summoned's experience worth and pool are 0 whoever summons. The same effect then marks every item the summoned carries with the spell flag 0x4000 and the item flag 0x20 (0x463630, RK13): not built (FT4, the corpse's loot rule 0x463860 would read it; issue [#1834](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/1834)). **Built by ARC-MAGIC-1169:** the row builder takes the faction (`CreatedCritterSummon.Faction`) and the alignment of an NPC summoner from the summoner's row (base stat 19, no effects; the faction a script set, else the row's), read when the world first reads the created row. Limits: the summoner's row is asked of the shown map by its current map (a summoner away from its home map, a carried companion or a created critter whose row the resolver does not give, copies nothing); `Clear_Faction:` stays not ready. | Original (F1); Steamwoven build, limits named |
| SUM24 | what an NPC summoner does not get | The original's forced join of the summoned under its parent (the ObjFlag `OSF_MIND_CONTROLLED` line, SO3) makes the summoned a follower of **an NPC parent** too (0x45DE90 tests only that the follower is an NPC), so its leader chain reaches the summoner and the two are one group. Built by ARC-MAGIC-1841 on the NPC leaders of ARC-WORLD-1840 ([party runtime](../../world/party-runtime.md#npc-leaders-arc-world-1840)): the cast host's join is `ForceJoinLeader` under any parent. The faction copy of SUM23 makes the pair faction-same (0x45D110), which is what the hostility and protect rules test for a summoner with a faction; a summoner with faction 0 and no leader has no such link. Issue [#1833](https://github.com/Simarglok/arcanum-steamwoven/issues/1833). | Original (ARC-MAGIC-1841) |

## Bottom strip counters 3 and 4 (ARC-UI-2311)

Source: the follow-up of [IP4 and IP10](#item-spell-pane-arc-ui-016) (#2304): the two number slots 0x54B3C0 refreshes. Read on 2026-11-15 by this task in the
exports of the 1.0.7.4 executable (0x54B3C0, 0x54AD00, 0x54B500, 0x54A330, 0x54A220, 0x551660, 0x57CAB0, 0x4649C0, 0x465CA0, 0x465820, 0x464780 and the
callers of 0x4605D0, with the field name tables); the executable itself was not read. Read again on 2026-11-16 by ARC-UI-2368 (the text export: 0x54AD00,
0x54A330, 0x535340/0x535390/0x535570/0x535850, 0x5351D0, 0x52CB20, 0x450420, 0x459590, 0x459680, 0x454920, the table 0x5C728C; BC9–BC11). Left at F0: the art file names
of 250..253, 469, 474 and 171, and the wrapping of a clamped re-measure.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| BC1 | slot table (0x5C63F8, filled by 0x54A330) | Six records (x, y, width, height, window) in the 800 × 600 screen: slot 0 (15, 578, 29 × 12) and 1 (755, 578, 27 × 12) the hero's health and fatigue, 2 (190, 17, 24 × 12) a stat in the top window, **3 (104, 512, 50 × 20) counter 3, 4 (264, 562, 50 × 20) counter 4**, 5 (15, 500, 29 × 12) the poison. Counters 3 and 4 live in the bottom bar window (the one at screen y 441); the table holds them in window coordinates (104, 71) and (264, 121). | Original |
| BC2 | counter 3 (0x54B3C0, then 0x4649C0, 0x465CA0, 0x465820, 0x464780) | Value 0 and icon 474 unless the hero exists. The wielded item is the first item of the hero's inventory list whose location is 1004 (the weapon slot, no other slot is read). Its ammunition type (field 125) is read only when its type is a weapon (5), else 10000 (none). **A type other than 10000:** the value is the hero's ammunition count of that type (the first ammunition item of the type in the inventory, field 142; 0 when there is none) and the icon is the dword at 0x5C728C + type × 4 (250, 251, 252, 253 for types 0..3; no bounds check). **No type:** a mana store (field 92) above 0 gives that value with icon 469. **Otherwise** (no item in the hand, or a store of 0 or less) the hero's gold (field 165 of the gold item, 0 without one) with icon 474. | Original; built (BarCounterRules.Counter3). A type above 3 reads past the table in the exe: not reproduced, no icon (BC11). |
| BC3 | icon (0x54B500) | Finds the window containing the screen point (61, 509) (the bar window) and blits the whole frame at window (61, 68), top left, unscaled, over what is there (no clear). | Original |
| BC4 | counter 4 (end of 0x54B3C0) | Runs only with a stored item (the mode-8 item globals 0x64C688/0x64C68C) and the bar mode 8. An item whose flag 0x1 (identified) is set shows field 105 (a negative count as −1); an unidentified one −2. Field 105 is named `obj_f_item_spell_mana_store` in the exe's tables (the spell charges of IP4). 0x54AD00 refuses slot 4 whenever the mode is not 8; 0x54B3C0 never clears it in any other case. | Original; built (BarCounterRules.Counter4) |
| BC5 | digits (0x54AD00 with slot, value, digits) | Nothing without the built bar. It selects the bar font (0x64C670, built from interface art 171) and fills the slot's rectangle (height + 1) with a colour (not read), except slot 5. Slots 3 and 4 with a negative value: exactly −1 writes one '-' per digit, any other negative one '?' per digit (signed compare). Every other case writes C's `%0Nd`: zero-padded to the slot's digits (6 for counter 3, 3 for counter 4; zero draws as "000000"), a longer number is not cut. Slots 0, 1 and 5 never get the runs: a negative number prints its sign (`-05`, [PA rows](ui-audio.md#combat-hud-arc-ui-017)). The text is centred in the rectangle (width and height), or starts at its left when wider (the width then clamped and measured again; not drawn when taller than the rectangle). The fill colour, the font colour and the drawing are BC9. | Original; built (BarCounterRules.Text, BarNumberRules.Place) |
| BC6 | refresh (callers of 0x54B3C0) | The bar build (0x54A330, the end), the state load (0x54A220), the mode-8 build (0x551660, only with a hero and a stored item), the UI callback 0x57CAB0 (the item's holder is the hero; it first drops a stored item whose parent is no longer the hero), the thunk 0x57CE00 behind the pointer 0x5E86CC that 0x4605D0 calls (the inventory change of the hero 0x466D60, wield on 0x4677B0 and off 0x467CB0, a gold change 0x464830, a spell payment 0x450420, the recharge components 0x454920 and 0x459680, a network packet 0x49D690 and the debug +1000 gold key 0x401560) and the main-menu close callback 0x585E20. | Original |
| BC7 | Steamwoven | **Built (ARC-UI-2311, text drawn by ARC-UI-2368).** `BarCounterRules` holds the slots, the icons, the values and the text; the viewer's `AreaBarCounterView` draws the icon from the bar's art and the two texts as `AreaBarNumber` (BC9); `SyncBarCounters` refreshes them when the session's revision, the weapon pane's mode, the loaded art or the font moves, which every callback of BC6 reaches. Counter 3's mana store (field 92) follows the session's copy since ARC-MAGIC-2371 (BC10): the weapon prototype's value until a payment drains it or a recharge restores it (`GameSessionOwner.QueryItemManaStore`, the viewer's `WieldedManaStore`). | Steamwoven |
| BC8 | open items of ARC-UI-2311 | Settled or split out: BC9 (fill and font colour), BC10 (the store changes, read; the build is the follow-up #2371) and BC11 (the table overrun). | Original |
| BC9 | digit writer colours and font (0x54AD00, 0x54A330, 0x535340–0x535850, 0x5351D0, 0x52CB20; ARC-UI-2368) | **Fill:** `tig_window_fill` (0x51D570) of the slot rectangle (height + 1 for that call only) with the colour built as `R[0] << rs \| G[0] << gs \| B[0] << bs` from the three tables (0x739F28, 0x739F2C, 0x739E98) and shifts (0x739EF0, 0x739EEC, 0x739EAC); the table builder 0x52CB20 fills each as `t[v] = v >> (8 − channel bits)`, so entry 0 is 0 in every pixel format: **opaque black**, no alpha, the same for slots 0..4, none for slot 5 (this also settles GB8's clock fill as black). **Font:** at the end of 0x54A330 the font on top of the font stack (the default font of 0x5351D0: flags 8 = shadow, colour white (each table's entry 0xFF), underline and strike colours white and unused, scale 1.5 unused with flag 1 clear) is copied (0x535390 / 0x5352E0), its art replaced by interface art 171 and the copy stored in 0x64C670. **Draw:** 0x54AD00 pushes the font (0x535340), measures (0x535390) and writes (0x51E190 → 0x535570 → 0x535850) and pops (0x535380). With flag 8 two passes: first a shadow in (10, 10, 10) (global 0x630D50) at (+1, +1), then the glyph in white, both with blit flag 0x2000 (a constant colour; the project's MW31 coverage blend, since font flag 0x80 is clear); flag 8 adds 1 to the measured width and height. Glyph for a character is frame (character − 31): '-' 14, '0'..'9' 17..26, '?' 32; the pen advance is the frame's hot x and the last glyph counts its width (`AreaArtFont`). **Place:** x = (slot width − text width) / 2 + slot x when the text is narrower than the slot, else the slot's x (the text then measured again at the slot's width; the wrap of that is F0) and nothing drawn when the slot is lower than the text; y = (slot height − text height) / 2 + slot y, both signed divisions. Slots 0, 1 and 5 (the vials' numbers, CH7) go through the same function: the same font, white and shadow; slot 0 and 1 get the same black fill, slot 5 none. | Original (F1) |
| BC10 | weapon mana store, object field 92 `obj_f_item_mana_store` (names table 0x59BEA8; 0x450420, 0x459590, 0x459680, 0x454920, 0x468930; ARC-UI-2368) | **It changes.** The payment 0x450420 for a PC or NPC caster (type 15 or 16) with apply set walks all nine worn slots 1000..1008 in order (an empty slot is skipped, the loop goes on after the cost is covered): an item whose store is **not 0** (not "above 0"; ARC-MAGIC-2371 re-read) pays first: a store at least the remaining cost (signed) keeps the difference and the cost is covered, a smaller one, a negative one included, is written as 0 and pays its value (a negative store therefore adds to the remaining cost and passes a negative amount on); the item is written back (0x406D40), 0x459590 is called with the paid amount (also 0 for a store that stays) and merge set, then the bar refresh 0x4605D0 (the hook 0x54B3C0, the local PC's counters, whoever casts); a store the payment leaves at 0 (equal to the remaining cost, smaller than it or negative; a store already 0 is skipped before any write) calls 0x4CCC40 (the one call site 0x45062B, after the write-back 0x406D40, the recharge 0x459590 and the bar refresh 0x4605D0, only with apply set) with the caster and the item, which clears the caster's whole slot of the AI action-list cache (the table 0x5FF620, forty slots of ten lists) **only if one of the slot's ten lists holds an entry for that item (compared by its handle); with no slot or no match nothing happens**; nothing on the critter or the item changes, the next AI request rebuilds the slot, SP11 (re-read by ARC-AI-2402: the charges branch of 0x450420, field 105 of types 5..14, never calls 0x4CCC40); the cost left goes to fatigue, and a refused fatigue part leaves the stores drained, the events queued and the slots cleared (MG20). The cost is halved first (minimum 1) when the critter's flags (field 0xDA) hold 0x80000000. An item as the caster (types 5..14) pays one charge from field 105 instead (IP10). **Recharge:** 0x459590 schedules (or merges the pending amount into) an event of kind 0x17 for the item, 60 s × 8 = 8 game minutes later (TE1); the handler 0x459680 restores min(pending, 2 × units) into field 92 with no cap at the prototype value, units = elapsed game seconds / 120 (minimum 1), refreshes the bar when the item's direct holder is the local PC (0x40D9F0) and reschedules the rest without merging (a fresh stamp, a new 480 s delay): about one point per game minute. A merge keeps the first event's stamp and deadline (a 0 or negative amount too), so a later drain recharges early and counts its elapsed time from the first one; a negative pending amount restores itself (the store goes back to its old negative value) and schedules nothing; the handler does not look where the item is (ground, container or another holder: it still recharges), a destroyed item has its event dropped by the queue's validation, kind 0x17 is saved and at a map change of the PC travels with its item or stays parked in the old map's file and fires late on return (a larger refill, TL19; TL33: the PC's teleport carries every event whose item is a traveller, the PC's own and its followers' with their inventories, and parks the rest in the old map's file). 0x454920 recharges field 105 (types 5..14), never field 92. The only other writers with a constant 92 are the built-in prototype builder 0x468930 (25 prototypes, values 0..80); writers taking the index from a variable (script, network, debug) were not searched. An instance keeps every field it has written (OM9), so the changed store is in the item's own data and a save holds it (OM8). | Original (F1); built (ARC-MAGIC-2371, schema 103): the session keeps a per-item store copy (`durability.manaStores`, written by the payment's drains and the recharge event, one revision each, read by the payer's worn stores, the item lookup and counter 3); the recharge event is the kind `item.recharge` (entity, stamp, pending; merged per item, 480 000 game ms, saved); **built as the original parks it (ARC-MAGIC-2400, schema 104):** the event is scoped to the map the PC was on when it was scheduled (a payment merges only into the pending event of its own map, as 0x459590 finds only the events in memory), a PC's map change carries the events of the items it holds and of its travelling followers' (worn or carried; `MoveFollowerEvents`) to the destination and leaves the others parked on the old map, where they wait and fire late on the PC's return with the elapsed time (a larger refill, `min(pending, 2 × units)` on the whole elapsed span); **the store and the pending amount are full 32-bit values (ARC-MAGIC-2401, no schema step: the saved range only widened):** a negative store is written as 0 by the payment and put back by its recharge event (the handler's signed minimum restores the negative pending amount whole, nothing is scheduled again), a merge's sum wraps as 0x459640's add does and no ceiling stops a store at a million; **the store is always written (ARC-MAGIC-2403):** the payment writes the store of every item a critter wears, as 0x450420 does, whatever the session tracks: the drain is written with `WriteItemManaStore(..., worn: true)`, which asks no attached map seed for the item (the host's map names it in the payer's slot) and needs only that the item is not deleted, and the recharge event writes a store the session already holds, tracked or not; the stores have a budget of their own (`GameSessionLimits.MaxManaStores`, 65,536: nine slots for every NPC of the installed maps, 34,128, with room for created critters), no longer shared with the durability records, so a full durability budget cannot lose one; a write is still refused (and logged as `ManaStoreNotSaved`) only for a deleted item (never worn), the 65,536th store or an exhausted revision counter. **Two payments of one event pass (ARC-MAGIC-2403):** the original pays at once, the host applies the notes of a pass after it, so a note's critter payment is planned again against the stores and the fatigue the payments before it left (`SessionSpellCaster.Replanned`, same cost) and that plan is written: the second payment drains what is left and the rest goes to fatigue, as when paid at once (the stale plan used to drain at most what was there and lose the rest of the cost); its status stays the first plan's, which already decided the run. **the AI list cache drop is built (ARC-AI-2402):** the caster's `ManaStoreEmptied` hook tells the viewer's AI host of each worn store a payment leaves at 0 (also when the session could not save the drain), and the host runs `AiListCache.ItemExhausted` (the Original setting's frozen list is rebuilt at the next request; Steamwoven builds every list fresh, so it changes nothing there) |
| BC11 | ammunition icon table 0x5C728C (0x54B3C0; ARC-UI-2368) | The dwords after the four entries are 0 (type 4), then 199, 495, 80, 48 (types 5..8: an unreferenced group that looks like a rectangle), then 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 0, 1, 0 (flags other code reads), then zeros: the exe draws art 0 for type 4 and unrelated numbers above it, with no bounds check. The prototypes' ammunition type (field 125) is 0 (12), 1 (36), 2 (2), 3 (3) or 10000 (none, 96), and no clamp exists, so no retail prototype reaches the overrun. Not reproduced (a technical bug of the exe with no installed case): no icon, as `BarCounterRules.AmmoIcon`. The .pro and .mob overrides of field 125 were not scanned. | Original (F1); Steamwoven: no icon |
